# OWNERSCORECARD REIT DESK — SURVEY (2026-07-25)

*Fifth analyst-desk filing. Eight Berkshire-schooled agents: a cohort audit, a live-defect hunt, four tag-census tiers across property types and mortgage REITs, the FFO/AFFO deep-dive, and a Munger traps lens. Desk protocol: survey -> THIS SPEC -> owner review -> extraction code -> hostile verify -> ship.*

*STATUS: FILED, NOT RATIFIED. No extraction code has been written.*

**The frame:** a REIT is a pile of buildings financed with debt and required to pay out most of what it earns. Depreciation on a well-maintained building overstates economic decline, which is why the industry reports funds from operations. But FFO then understates the true cost of ownership, because roofs, lifts and tenant improvements consume cash every year. The owner's question is what can be taken out AFTER keeping the buildings competitive and the tenants in place, and whether the distribution is covered by that or by borrowing.

---

## 1. THE COHORT, AND IT IS THE WORST THE DESKS HAVE FOUND

### REITs — Specialty & Diversified (Real Estate, family "property") — 162
162 rows / 135 distinct CIKs, all US. The catch-all: every SIC 6798 and 6519 filer lands here. WELL, AMT, EQIX, PLD, WY, IRM, SPG, HST, DLR, VTR, O, PSA, SPG, VICI, NLY, AGNC, STWD, CXW, HHH, TCI, SCCD/E/F/G. By the repo's OWN reit-subsectors.json, only 15 of the 162 rows are actually "specialty" or "diversified" — 147 are misfiled onto it (healthcare 21, mortgage 36, net-lease 18, retail 16, residential 15, office 14, hotel 9, industrial 7, self-storage 5, tower 3, data-center 2).

### REITs — Office (Real Estate, family "property") — 1
PDM (Piedmont Realty Trust) — the sole member, and only because taxonomy.json carries a per-ticker override. Every other office REIT (BXP, VNO, SLG, KRC, CUZ, HIW, ARE, DEI, ESRT, BDN, CDP, JBGS, DEA, ALX) sits on Specialty & Diversified; HPP and OPI sit on Real Estate Development & Services.

### Real Estate Development & Services (Real Estate, family "property") — 52
52 rows / 46 issuers (30 US, 17 ADR, 5 JP). Genuine services and developers: CBRE, JLL, CWK, NMRK, MMI, COMP, OPEN, FOR, BEKE, FSV, CIGI, REAX, AGNT (ex-eXp World), plus JP 8801/8802/8830/8804/3289. Contaminated by 10 genuine equity REITs (GTY, HPP, IIPR, INVH, TRNO, UE, OPI, CURB, AHRT, MRP) whose only sin is an EDGAR SIC of 6500/6510 instead of 6798.

### Mortgage & Specialty Finance (Financials, family "lender") — 12
ABR (Arbor Realty Trust) and CMTG (Claros Mortgage Trust) — both mortgage REITs, correctly routed here by per-ticker override — sitting beside non-REIT originators and fintech lenders: RKT, UWMC, PFSI, ONIT, BETR, FIGR, TREE, FINV, and AGM (Farmer Mac, a GSE). HASI (HA Sustainable Infrastructure) also here. The shelf separates mortgage REITs from buildings but not from non-REIT lenders.

### Hotels & Resorts (Consumer Discretionary, operating family) — REIT-bearing by accident — 41
Holds three genuine equity hotel REITs stranded among operators and OTAs: PK (Park Hotels & Resorts), XHR (Xenia Hotels), SHO (Sunstone Hotel Investors) — all SIC 7011, all near-zero effective tax, all with "qualify as a REIT" in a 10-K filed since 2025-01-01 (EDGAR full-text). Their peers HST, RHP, PEB, APLE, DRH, RLJ, INN, CLDT, SVC (SIC 6798) sit on the REIT shelf. Same business, split across two shelves by a 1987 SIC code.

**Mortgage REITs:** "COUNT: 19 distinct mortgage-REIT issuers occupying 36 of the 162 rows on the equity-REIT shelf — ADAM (6 rows), AGNC (7), ARI, ARR, BRSP, BXMT, CIM (2), DX, FBRT, KREF, LADR, NLY, ORC, RITM, RWT (3), SCCD/E/F/G (4), STWD, TRTX, TPTA. Two more, ABR (Arbor Realty Trust, SIC 6798) and CMTG (Claros Mortgage Trust), already sit on Mortgage & Specialty Finance via per-ticker override, bringing the pool total to 21 mortgage-REIT issuers.\n\nDOES THE TAXONOMY SEPARATE THEM? No. taxonomy.json's Real Estate sector has nine industry labels and not one of them is mortgage REITs. There is no \"REITs — Mortgage\" label and no such shelf in shelves.json. SIC 6798 covers equity and mortgage REITs alike, and taxonomy.json maps 6798 to a single destination — \"REITs — Specialty & Diversified\" — so a levered agency-MBS book and a shopping centre land on the same shelf, in the same \"property\" column family, sorted against each other on revenue and FFO. The only separation that exists is ad hoc: two per-ticker overrides (ABR, CMTG) pointing at a Financials shelf that also holds non-REIT originators (RKT, UWMC, PFSI), fintech lenders (TREE, FINV, BETR, FIGR) and a GSE (AGM).\n\nTHE PARTIAL GUARD, AND WHERE IT LEAKS: src/lib/groupingColumns.mjs line 330-335 does try to defend this — the ffo cell returns n/a unless financialProfile(company).kind === \"reit\". I tested it against the live data. It correctly marks NLY and AGNC n/a. It FAILS for five: STWD ($491m), RITM ($805m), BXMT ($178m), BRSP ($4m) and TPTA (−$24m) are all handed an FFO figure — depreciation added back to a loan portfolio, which is exactly the meaningless number the comment says it is guarding against. A guard that catches 14 of 19 is a guard that ships wrong numbers on the other five.\n\nWhat these companies actually are: repo-financed portfolios of mortgage-backed securities or first-lien commercial loans. Their earnings power is net interest spread less credit losses; their risk is duration, prepayment and repo rollover. Book value per share, spread, leverage multiple and credit reserve are the figures. Depreciation is not in the picture at all, and neither are roofs, lifts or tenant improvements."

There is no Simon Property Group, L.P., no Brandywine Operating Partnership, no ERP Operating Partnership — the universe is drawn from the Nasdaq screener of listed common, and OPs file jointly with their parent under a combined 10-K, so the pipeline never picks them up separately.\n\nWHAT IS THERE INSTEAD, AND IT IS WORSE: 27 duplicate rows on the REIT shelves from preferred shares and baby bonds sharing one CIK with their common. 162 rows on the Specialty & Diversified shelf resolve to only 135 distinct issuers. Every duplicate carries the parent's full income statement, so a shelf-level median, a peer set or a \"largest by revenue\" sort counts AGNC seven times and Adamas six. Opendoor appears twice (OPEN, OPENW).\n\nRecommendation: shelf counts, medians and rankings should be computed on distinct CIK, with non-common tickers suppressed from the table but kept for routing."

**Notes:** "TEN LARGEST REIT-SHELF MEMBERS BY REVENUE (FY2025, one row per CIK, from src/data/fundamentals.json): WELL Welltower $10,838m; AMT American Tower $10,645m; EQIX Equinix $9,217m; PLD Prologis $8,790m; WY Weyerhaeuser $6,905m; IRM Iron Mountain $6,902m; SPG Simon Property Group $6,365m; HST Host Hotels $6,114m; DLR Digital Realty $6,113m; VTR Ventas $5,834m. Correcting the misfiles does not change this ten — the largest strays are INVH $2,729m and PK $2,541m.\n\nTHE HEADLINE DEFECT — ONE SHELF DOING THE WORK OF NINE. taxonomy.json defines nine Real Estate industries. shelves.json (93 shelves total) carries only three of them: reits-office, reits-specialty-and-diversified, real-estate-development-and-services. Six labels — REITs — Retail, Residential, Industrial & Logistics, Health Care, Hotels, Data Centers & Towers — exist in the taxonomy with NO shelf entry and, consequently, zero members. And they get zero members because taxonomy.json maps SIC 6798 and 6519 to exactly one destination, REITs — Specialty & Diversified. The result: 162 rows on one shelf of which, by the repo's own reit-subsectors.json, 147 are not specialty and not diversified. That is the REIT desk's answer to the software desk's 115. REITs — Office holds a single company, PDM, and only because a per-ticker override put it there.\n\nSo the six proposed destinations above with targetExists:false are blocked on the standing gate: a new shelf needs an entry in shelves.json AND a family in groupingColumns.mjs. All six would take family \"property\", which already exists — so the work is data, not code. reit-subsectors.json (149 entries) already contains the correct assignment for nearly every stranded member and could seed all six shelves directly.\n\nIS THE FFO DEFINITION ADEQUATE? No. src/lib/reits.mjs line 15-17: ffo(L) = netIncome + depreciation − (gainOnSaleRealEstate || 0). Five faults, all verified against data I fetched this session:\n\n1. `|| 0` converts a MISSING gain into a ZERO gain — a wrong number where a missing number was the honest answer. 63 of 163 REIT-shelf rows carry no gainOnSaleRealEstate, including EIGHT of the top ten: WELL, AMT, PLD, WY, IRM, SPG, DLR, VTR. For Prologis (CIK 0001045609) I confirmed in companyfacts that no us-gaap gain-on-disposition tag carries an annual FY2024 or FY2025 10-K value at all: GainLossOnSaleOfProperties last reports FY2018, GainsLossesOnSalesOfInvestmentRealEstate last reports FY2012, GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1 has no annual rows, and Prologis's extension namespace is absent from companyfacts entirely (namespaces present: dei, invest, srt, us-gaap). The figure is unavailable — and the page still prints FFO of $5,954m. For a developer that manufactures buildings and sells them, that is the single item most likely to flatter. The fix is to withhold FFO where the gains term cannot be sourced, not to default it to zero.\n\n2. Wrong depreciation. CONCEPTS.depreciation (scripts/fetchFundamentals.mjs line 119) reads, exact spellings: DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization, DepreciationAmortizationAndAccretionNet, DepreciationAndAmortization, DepreciationAmortizationAndOther, DepreciationDepletionAndAmortizationNonproduction, CostOfGoodsAndServicesSoldDepreciationAndAmortization, Depreciation. Those are TOTAL D&A. FFO adds back real-estate depreciation only. For SPG (CIK 0001063761) I confirmed both tags exist in companyfacts and disagree: DepreciationAndAmortization FY2025 = $1,426.4m, while SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense FY2025 = $1,481.9m (FY2024 $1,118.2m vs $1,265.3m). The code takes the wrong one.\n\n3. No impairment adjustment. assetImpairment and goodwillImpairment exist in the lines vocabulary; ffo() ignores both. An impairment of depreciable property is excluded from FFO, so a REIT that writes down a mall reports a depressed figure here that no one else reports.\n\n4. It stops at FFO — which is precisely where the honest owner's question begins. There is no AFFO, no recurring capex, no tenant improvements, no leasing commissions and no straight-line rent adjustment anywhere in src/lib/reits.mjs. Yet the filings carry the pieces: SPG tags StraightLineRent (FY2025 +$27.8m, FY2024 −$0.8m, FY2023 −$9.9m) and AmortizationOfDeferredLeasingFees. And where capex might stand in as a rough proxy it is missing for PLD and DLR. So the terminal can say what FFO is and cannot yet say what cash can be taken out after keeping the buildings competitive — nor, therefore, whether the distribution is earned or borrowed. Dividend coverage is separately unanswerable for 19 rows including SPG, whose dividendsPaid is null.\n\n5. It is applied to loan books. See the mortgageReits field: five mortgage REITs pass the kind===\"reit\" guard and receive an FFO figure. HHH and TCI, which are not REITs at all, receive one too ($277m and $26m).\n\nOTHER DATA DEFECTS FOUND, none of which are shelf questions: (a) NXRT carries CIK 0001620393, which EDGAR confirms is NexPoint RESIDENTIAL Trust, Inc., but the repo's name field reads \"NexPoint Real Estate Finance\" — a different company (NREF). The name is wrong, the shelving is right. (b) GEO Group's name field reads \"Geo Group Inc (The) REIT\"; GEO's FY2025 effective tax is 25.2% and it is not a REIT — the trailing \"REIT\" is a stale Nasdaq-screener artefact, as it is on \"Equinix Inc. Common Stock REIT\", \"Rayonier Inc. REIT\" and \"W. P. Carey Inc. REIT\". (c) reit-subsectors.json assigns REIT subsectors to six companies that are not REITs — SKYH (0 REIT hits, loss-shielded), FPH (29.0% effective tax), JOE (25.3%), TRC, MLP, FRPH (19.7%) — and labels TPTA \"not-a-reit\" when it is one. It also has no entry for any of the 19 mortgage REITs, for CXW, or for OPI. (d) RWT/RWTN/RWTO carry fy \"undefined\". (e) UNIT (Uniti Group, repo CIK 0002020795, SIC 4813, Telecom Operators) is a REIT-status question I could NOT resolve this session — its FY2025 tax is −$136.5m on $1,168m pretax, which is a deferred release and proves nothing either way. I am not proposing a move for it; it needs a read of the post-Windstream 10-K. Same for HASI on Mortgage & Specialty Finance.\n\nFiles read: C:/Users/ryanr/.surplus-scout/ownerscorecard-terminal/src/data/shelves.json, /src/data/taxonomy.json, /src/data/reit-subsectors.json, /src/data/fundamentals.json (+ .adr/.eu/.jp), /src/lib/shelves.mjs, /src/lib/reits.mjs, /src/lib/groupingColumns.mjs, /src/lib/archetype.mjs, /scripts/fetchFundamentals.mjs. ---

## 2. THE LIVE DEFECT HUNT

Cohort = the 142 equity REITs in reit-subsectors.json (all present in src/data/fundamentals.json; the 38 mortgage REITs under SIC 6798 are correctly excluded). I tested the shipped surfaces (the property grouping table's revenue|ffo|dividendsPaid|netDebt|totalAssets, and buildReitScorecard's FFO / Dividend-FFO / Debt-assets / Interest-coverage cells, rendered through the real groupingColumns.mjs) against live SEC companyfacts for 109 filers and against the FY2025 10-K FFO reconciliations of 13 filers.

HEADLINE: the ffo column is materially wrong for most of the cohort. Across the 13 filers whose reported NAREIT FFO I read verbatim from the filed 10-K, the median absolute error is 33.5%; only AvalonBay agreed (0.07%). The worst case, Alexandria (ARE), ships FFO of ($722M) / ($4.24) per share where the 10-K reports +$1,310,607 thousand — a sign-flipped number on the one line a REIT is priced on. Healthcare Realty ships a 469% payout labelled "Not covered by FFO" against a true 79%; SL Green 144% against 55%; Macerich 110% against 50%. Vornado ships FFO 2.8x too high ($6.72/sh against a reported $2.42) and a payout of 10% labelled "Lightly covered" against a true 29%.

The formula fails for three separable reasons. (1) It never adds back impairments of depreciable real estate, which NAREIT does — the tag ImpairmentOfRealEstate is live and unread at ARE ($2,202,818k FY2025), Realty Income ($471,335k), Macerich ($151M), Aimco ($147.5M) and 40 more. (2) The gains chain misses GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets (no trailing "1"), GainLossOnSaleOfPropertyPlantEquipment and GainsLossesOnSalesOfOtherRealEstate, so Digital Realty's $995,586k disposition gain is simply not subtracted; worse, the "|| 0" in reits.mjs line 16 turns an UNKNOWN gain into a ZERO gain. (3) It uses total D&A and total net income rather than real-estate D&A and net income available to common, so PSA's $194,703k of preferred dividends inflate its FFO. Re-running the cohort with only the empirically confirmed tag repairs moves 24 of 109 filers by more than 10%. A residual class (SPG, PLD, FRT, KIM, WELL, VTR, HR, SLG, MAC) carries its FFO adjustments only in company extension elements that companyfacts does not serve at all — for those there is no tag fix, and doctrine says the cell must go dark rather than print a confident number.

Beyond FFO: dividends paid is dark for 21 REITs including AvalonBay, Simon, Public Storage, Federal Realty and Essex — the distribution is the whole point of the security, and three confirmed tags fix all but Simon. Aimco ships Debt/assets of 262% ("Heavy") because lines.totalDebt carries a stale mixed-vintage $4,388M against $1,676M of assets; CBL ships its FY2016 debt as FY2025; twenty REITs have a lines.totalDebt that contradicts their own history array for the same year. Broadstone's realEstateGross is 21x too small because the chain's first tag wins over the complete one. And the whole shelf is one bucket: 162 of 193 property rows sit in "REITs — Specialty & Diversified" while "REITs — Office" holds exactly one member, so a tower REIT, an apartment REIT and a hotel REIT share a group median — even though reit-subsectors.json already carries the clean ten-way split and is entirely unused by shelves.


### The existing FFO column — verdict

NOT ADEQUATE — the definition is wrong in three separable ways and the resulting column is materially incorrect for most of the cohort. Tested against the FY2025 NAREIT FFO each company reports in its own 10-K, the shipped figure erred by: ARE −155% (sign-flipped: ($722M) against +$1,311M), VNO +177.5%, HR −83.2%, SLG −61.6%, MAC −54.9%, SPG +50.2%, DLR +33.5%, FRT +24.8%, O −11.8%, ESS −8.2%, PSA +5.6%, PLD +4.8%, AVB +0.07%. Median absolute error 33.5%; one of thirteen was right.

(1) Impairments of depreciable real estate are never added back, though NAREIT adds them and ImpairmentOfRealEstate is live and unread at forty-plus members (ARE 2,202,818,000; O 471,335,000; MAC 151,000,000; AIV 147,500,000; REXR 89,000,000; BXP 86,000,000). This is the largest and most easily repaired leg — it alone takes Realty Income from 11.8% wrong to 0.4% wrong and flips Alexandria from negative to positive.

(2) The gains chain misses GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets, GainLossOnSaleOfPropertyPlantEquipment and GainsLossesOnSalesOfOtherRealEstate, so Digital Realty's $995,586,000 gain is never subtracted. Worse, the "|| 0" fallback in reits.mjs line 16 converts an UNKNOWN gain into a ZERO gain — precisely the wrong-number-worse-than-missing-number failure, because SPG, PLD, FRT, KIM, WELL, VTR, VICI, EXR, ELS, CUZ, DEI, IRM, MAC and UE file no us-gaap gain element at all and their FFO is therefore confidently overstated.

(3) It uses total D&A and total net income where NAREIT uses real-estate D&A and net income available to common, so preferred dividends (PSA $194,703,000), noncontrolling interests, non-real-estate depreciation and unconsolidated-JV depreciation are all mis-stated. This is the residual 5-10% band that remains at ESS and PSA after the first two legs are fixed.

Recommended shape: ffo = netIncomeAvailableToCommon + realEstateDepreciation − gainsOnRealEstate + impairmentOfRealEstate, with a hard withhold whenever the gains term is unreadable, whenever net income is negative with no readable impairment line, or whenever ffo/revenue exceeds ~0.85. Re-running the whole cohort with only the empirically confirmed tag repairs moves 24 of 109 filers by more than 10%; the rest need the withhold, not a better formula. Until then the column should be labelled an approximation and the per-share figure dropped — an owner told Realty Income earns $3.75 a share against the filed $4.25 has been handed a number no filing supports, and one told Healthcare Realty pays out 469% of FFO has been handed a reason to sell a covered dividend.


### Defects, ranked by owner harm

- **ARE · ffo (property grouping column, REIT scorecard "Funds from operations", ffoPerShare, and the reitQuality growth series)** [critical] — shows ($722M), labelled "about $-4.24 per share"; formula shown as "Net income ($1.4B) + depreciation $1.4B − gains on sale $642M"; truth $1,310,607 thousand. Alexandria's FY2025 10-K (filed 2026-01-26), Definitions and reconciliations: net loss to common (1,437,987) + real-estate D&A 1,341,157 − NCI share of D&A (154,727) + JV D&A 3,703 − gain on sales (330,121) + impairment of real estate rental properties and land 1,894,263 − unvested allocation (5,681) = 1,310,607. The pipeline is $2,032,144 thousand low and on the wrong side of zero.. Fix: Read ImpairmentOfRealEstate (live for ARE at 2,202,818,000 FY2025) into a new REIT line and make ffo = netIncome + depreciation − gains + impairmentRealEstate. That single change takes ARE to $1,481M, within 13% of the filed figure instead of 155% wrong. Until the JV and NCI legs are also read, drop the per-share figure and label the check approximate. And add a hard guard: never print a negative FFO for a REIT — withhold instead. (confidence: certain — reported figure read verbatim from the filed 10-K; tag value read from live companyfacts this session)
- **HR · REIT scorecard "Dividend / FFO (payout)" and the ffo column** [critical] — shows payout 469%, tone "bad", label "Not covered by FFO"; ffo $83M ($0.24 per share); truth FFO attributable to common stockholders = $490,048 thousand, $1.38 per diluted share (Healthcare Realty FY2025 10-K, filed 2026-02-13). True payout = 386,919 / 490,048 = 79%, i.e. covered. The pipeline misses a $361,090 thousand impairment of real estate properties and $27,769 thousand of unconsolidated-JV D&A and impairment.. Fix: There is NO tag repair here — HR's ImpairmentOfRealEstate is populated only through 2022 and AssetImpairmentCharges is absent for 2025, so the add-back sits in an extension element companyfacts does not serve. Doctrine applies: gate ffo/ffoPayout to withhold when netIncome is negative and no impairment line is readable. A dash is correct; "469% — Not covered by FFO" is a libel on a covered dividend and would push an owner to sell. (confidence: certain)
- **VNO · ffo, ffoPerShare, Dividend/FFO payout, and the netDebt grouping column** [critical] — shows ffo $1.4B, "about $6.72 per share"; payout 10%, label "Lightly covered"; netDebt $1.4B; truth FFO attributable to common shareholders plus assumed conversions = $486,826 thousand, $2.42 per diluted share (Vornado FY2025 10-K, filed 2026-02-09). The pipeline is 2.8x too high, +$864,294 thousand; true payout is 141,277 / 486,826 = 29%. Cause: FY2025 net income contains an $803,248 thousand "Gain on sales-type lease" plus $90,762 thousand of JV disposition gains, while the chain reads only GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1 = 35,291,000. Separately, real debt at 12/31/2025 is mortgages payable net 4,920,669 + senior unsecured notes net 747,202 + unsecured term loan net 797,337 + revolver 720,420 = $7,185,628 thousand, against a shipped totalDebt of $2,264,959 thousand.. Fix: Two fixes. (a) No us-gaap element carries the sales-type-lease gain, so ffo must withhold — gate on netIncome exceeding a gains-free proxy, or on an unread-gains flag. (b) The debtToAssets guard already withholds VNO correctly (interest/debt = 15.6% > 12%), but debtReliable() in fundamentals.mjs uses a 50% threshold, so the netDebt COLUMN still prints $1.4B on the same page. Tighten debtReliable for the property family to the same 12% test the REIT scorecard uses, so the two surfaces cannot disagree. (confidence: certain)
- **AIV · REIT scorecard "Debt / assets" plus the netDebt grouping column** [critical] — shows Debt/assets 262%, tone "bad", label "Heavy"; formula "Total debt $4.4B ÷ assets $1.7B"; netDebt $4.0B against totalAssets $1.7B in the same table row; truth Aimco's FY2025 balance sheet per live companyfacts: Assets = 1,675,733,000, Liabilities = 1,122,848,000, LongTermDebt = 738,625,000, SecuredDebt = 339,483,000. Total debt cannot exceed total liabilities. fundamentals.json's own history array carries totalDebt 2025 = $739M; the shipped lines value of $4,388,185,000 is a stale mixed-vintage figure from the pre-2020 Aimco (history shows 2018 = $4,076M). The ttm block carries $4,394,823,000, so it is stale there too.. Fix: In fetchFundamentals.mjs, totalDebt = maxOf(componentDebt, splitInstant, ...aggInstantVals), and each aggregate is picked by pickInstant across ALL fyEnds — so a tag last filed in 2018 wins the max forever. Bound the max to the anchor fiscal year: only aggregate values whose instant date equals the anchor period end may enter. Then two tripwires: fail the build when lines.totalDebt disagrees with history[latest].totalDebt by more than 2%, and when totalDebt exceeds totalLiabilities. (confidence: certain)
