### Null rates

- **capex** — 43.0% (61 of 142), defect (unread REIT tag family) plus a doctrinal gap. The chain stops at PaymentsForCapitalImprovements. Confirmed live in companyfacts FY2025: PLD PaymentsForLeasingCostsCommissionsAndTenantImprovements=562,197,000 and PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets=2,781,260,000; PSA PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets=310,737,000 and PaymentsToAcquireRealEstate=945,585,000; FRT PaymentsToAcquireRealEstate=735,274,000; ESS PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets=61,878,000. Tally over the 44 dark filers whose facts I hold: PaymentsToAcquireRealEstate 26, PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets 22, PaymentsToAcquireRealEstateHeldForInvestment 5, PaymentsToAcquireCommercialRealEstate 5, PaymentsForLeasingCosts 4, PaymentsForTenantImprovements 3, PaymentsForLeasingCostsCommissionsAndTenantImprovements 3, PaymentsToAcquireAndDevelopRealEstate 3. The doctrinal half: acquire/develop are GROWTH spending and must never be netted as maintenance; the recurring lines a roof-and-lift owner wants are PaymentsForCapitalImprovements, PaymentsForTenantImprovements and PaymentsForLeasingCosts(Commissions). Until those are read there is no AFFO on this desk at all.
- **gainOnSaleRealEstate** — 31.7% (45 of 142), mixed — roughly half defect, half structural at the filer. DEFECT half, confirmed live FY2025: GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets (no trailing digit) carries DLR=995,586,000, BXP=176,732,000, HIW=107,149,000, MAA=72,066,000, WSR=29,957,000; GainLossOnSaleOfPropertyPlantEquipment carries DLR=995,586,000, KRC=127,038,000, STAG=93,750,000; GainsLossesOnSalesOfOtherRealEstate carries COLD=-44,324,000 and RLJ=-1,526,000. None are in the chain. STRUCTURAL half: SPG, PLD, FRT, KIM, VTR, WELL, VICI, EXR, ELS, CUZ, DEI, IRM, MAC, UE, SAFE and DRH have NO us-gaap gain-on-disposition element in companyfacts at all (I grepped every us-gaap key on each) — their gains sit in extension namespaces the API does not return. SPG's own 10-K shows the missing figure is $2,887,460 thousand; FRT's is $150,111 thousand. For that half the honest fix is withholding FFO, not a tag.
- **assetImpairment** — 49.3% (70 of 142), defect measured against the FFO definition — the NAREIT add-back is not collected for REITs at all. ImpairmentOfRealEstate appears in no concept chain and is not merged into assetImpairment, yet it is live: ARE FY2025=2,202,818,000, O=471,335,000, MAC=151,000,000, AIV=147,500,000, REXR=89,000,000, BXP=86,000,000, WPC=70,000,000, AHR=50,000,000, PEB=49,000,000, JBGS=45,000,000, BNL=40,000,000, CSR=38,000,000, AKR=37,000,000, NNN=29,000,000, OHI=23,000,000, BRX=20,000,000, plus 25 more. PSA's 4,348,000 matches its 10-K FFO table to the dollar. This one omission flips ARE's shipped FFO from ($722M) to +$1,481M.
- **dividendsPaid** — 14.8% (21 of 142): AVB, BDN, BRX, EGP, ESS, FPH, FRPH, FRT, HHH, LAMR, MLP, MPT, NHP + 2 preferred tranches, PSA, SKYH, SPG, SUI, TCI, TRC, defect for 20 of 21; structural only for SPG. Chain is [PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock, PaymentsOfDividends]. Confirmed live FY2025: PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividends — AVB=992,333,000, ESS=654,068,000, SUI=1,044,300,000, LAMR=656,221,000, EGP=302,507,000, MPT=193,259,000. PaymentsOfCapitalDistribution — PSA=2,303,381,000, BRX=354,224,000. DividendsCommonStockCash (declared, not paid) — FRT=384,763,000. SPG genuinely carries no us-gaap cash-dividend element (only PaymentsOfDividendsMinorityInterest=439,384,000), so SPG stays dark by the filer's own tagging. Owner harm is high: with dividendsPaid null the Dividend/FFO check prints "Not enough data" for five of the largest REITs in the cohort.
- **interestExpense** — 19.7% (28 of 142) incl. CCI, WELL, EQR, EXR, SBAC, REG, MAC, OHI, SUI, ELS, CUBE, SBRA, FR, PECO, AAT, DEA, defect (unread successor tags). Confirmed live FY2025: InterestExpenseDebt — CCI=972,000,000, EQR=306,798,000, SUI=221,000,000, OHI=215,035,000, ELS=131,005,000, SBRA=112,489,000, CTO=26,928,000. InterestExpenseBorrowings — WELL=651,955,000. InterestExpenseDebtExcludingAmortization — CCI=956,000,000, CUBE=114,099,000, FR=84,886,000. InterestIncomeExpenseNonoperatingNet (negative = net expense) — REG=-199,548,000, PECO=-110,338,000, AAT=-78,120,000, DEA=-74,454,000. MAC InterestIncomeExpenseNet=-283,542,000. Double harm: the Interest-coverage check goes dark, AND the debtToAssets under-capture guard (interest/debt > 12%) cannot fire, so a badly under-captured debt figure prints unchallenged.
- **realEstateGross** — 7.7% (11 of 142): DOC, FRPH, HASI, MLP, MRP, PINE, RYN, SKYH, TRC, VICI, WY, mostly structural; the live defect is a wrong value, not a null. Structural: VICI holds its casinos as NetInvestmentInLeaseExcludingAccruedInterestBeforeAllowanceForCreditLoss=24,625,749,000 (sales-type leases, not depreciable property) — there is no gross-at-cost line to read and inventing one would be a fiction. DOC (Healthpeak) tags only RealEstateInvestmentPropertyNet=16,099,095,000, no gross element. WY and RYN are timber. The real defect is BNL, where the chain's first-wins order ships 265,812,000 instead of the complete RealEstateGrossAtCarryingValue=5,554,193,000 — see defects.
- **totalDebt** — 0.7% (1 of 142: HIW), defect, and a total one. Highwoods Properties, a $6,274M-asset office REIT, has totalDebt null in lines AND null in all ten history years, so the netDebt column reads "—" and Debt/assets reads "Not enough data". For a REIT, leverage is the read. Withholding is at least honest, but a ten-year blank at a single member is the signature of a chain miss, not of a debt-free landlord.
- **depreciation** — 1.4% (2 of 142: MRP, RYN), structural. RYN (Rayonier) is timber and reports depletion, not building depreciation; ffo() correctly returns null for both rather than printing net income dressed as FFO. This is the guard working.
- **costOfRevenue / inventory / netPPE / currentAssets / currentLiabilities / accountsPayable / researchDevelopment / netInterestIncome / shortTermInvestments** — 81.0% / 93.7% / 75.4% / 91.5% / 91.5% / 90.8% / 99.3% / 95.1% / 95.1%, structural — no repair wanted. A REIT files an unclassified balance sheet (no current/noncurrent split), carries buildings as real estate rather than PP&E, reports property operating expense rather than cost of goods, holds no inventory and runs no research line. These are category absences, and the grouping table already routes around them by marking operating margin, gross margin and ROIC n/a for the property family. Leave dark.
- **goodwill / goodwillImpairment / acquisitionSpend / buybacks / repurchasedShares / incomeTaxExpense** — 69.0% / 88.0% / 80.3% / 50.7% / 47.9% / 29.6%, structural. A trust that must distribute most of its taxable income pays little corporate tax and rarely repurchases stock; most grew by buying buildings rather than businesses, so no goodwill arises. Darkness here is the record telling the truth about the business form, not a gap.

---

## 3. FFO / AFFO — THE DESK'S CENTREPIECE

### Verdict

WITHHOLD a computed FFO. Publish the filer's own reported figure, verbatim-parsed, or publish nothing. This is the Oracle-backlog answer, and the evidence is not close.

(1) FFO IS NOT IN XBRL. AT ALL. Across all five 10-Ks I read this session — PLD 0001193125-26-051453, SPG 0001104659-26-019419, O 0000726728-26-000011, AVB 0000915912-26-000004, BXP 0001037540-26-000006, all FY2025, all filed Feb 2026 — not one extension schema declares an element whose name contains FundsFromOperation, FFO, Nareit, AdjustedFunds or CoreFunds, and not one ix:nonFraction in any of the five primary documents carries such a name. I verified my detector by marking every ix:nonFraction: the income statements come back densely marked (e.g. «us-gaap:Revenues» 8,790,127 for Prologis) and the FFO reconciliations come back with zero marks. So the dimension question the Tier-2 keyhole reader was built for never arises: the fact does not exist in XBRL, dimensioned or undimensioned. The earnings 8-Ks do not rescue it — AvalonBay's Q4 8-K carries a 1,786-byte extension schema and a 2,821-byte instance (cover-page dei only) alongside a 487kB untagged Ex-99.1 that contains the FFO table. A Tier-2 reader cannot unlock what was never tagged; only a text parser of the MD&A table can.

(2) THE FILERS DO NOT AGREE, AND THE DISAGREEMENT IS LOAD-BEARING. All five cite NAREIT. All five start somewhere different: Prologis from net earnings attributable to common stockholders, Simon from CONSOLIDATED net income, Realty Income from net income available to common, AvalonBay from net income attributable to common, BXP from net income attributable to BXP Inc. and then grosses back up to the partnership. Three of five add a proportionate share of unconsolidated-joint-venture depreciation that has no GAAP tag anywhere (PLD +551m, SPG +811,690k, BXP +65,446k plus +145,133k of JV impairments). Simon's headline FFO is the whole Operating Partnership including 636,189k that belongs to limited partners, not to a shareholder. BXP presents four different FFO tables in one filing. Residuals against a same-source recompute: PLD +4.8%, SPG +50.2%, O +0.42%, AVB +0.15%, BXP +0.82% or +9.2% depending on which of its FFO lines you name.

(3) ADJUSTED MEASURES: five distinct labels across the five filers — 'FFO, as modified by Prologis' and 'Core FFO' (PLD), 'Real Estate FFO' (SPG), 'Normalized FFO' and 'AFFO' (O), 'Core FFO' (AVB), and none at all (BXP). The two named 'Core FFO' share nothing but the name: Prologis removes development-property gains and current tax on dispositions; AvalonBay removes advocacy contributions, severance, executive transition compensation and for-sale condominium activity. Realty Income's AFFO alone runs twelve further adjustments. Not one of these numbers is machine-readable in any filing I fetched.

(4) THE DECISIVE ARITHMETIC. The failure is structural, not noisy. The NAREIT formula's most important term — gains on property sales — is a COMPANY EXTENSION ELEMENT for the two largest names, and SEC companyfacts carries no extension namespace (I confirmed: PLD's facts contain only dei/invest/srt/us-gaap while its income statement is built on pld:RentalRevenue, pld:GainsOnOtherDispositionsOfInvestmentsInRealEstateNet 685,831 and pld:GainsOnDispositionsOfDevelopmentPropertiesAndLandNet 257,731). Simon is worse: it fuses gains AND impairments into one element, spg:AcquisitionOfControllingInterestSaleOrDisposalOfOrRecoveryOnAssetsAndInterestsInUnconsolidatedEntitiesGainOrLossAndImpairmentNet 2,887,460, so even a reader that could see extension tags could not split the term NAREIT adds back from the term it subtracts. Result: our shipped ffo() would print 6,050,698k for Simon against a reported 4,027,116k — fifty per cent too high, on the second-largest REIT in the country, in a year it booked a $2.9bn gain. And the two cases that look tolerable are tolerable by accident: Prologis's +4.8% is a 686 error cancelling a 551 error, and repairing only the gain widens it to −7.2%; BXP's +0.82% is +156,894 of invisible JV items cancelling −120,601 of unsubtracted OP-unitholder share. We cannot know ex ante which company is in the lucky group without reading the filing — which is precisely the ex-ante complete-denominator test the doctrine requires. Two of five fail outright, two more agree for the wrong reasons, one (Realty Income) genuinely computes. A number that is right on 20% of names for the right reason is a wrong number.

TWO DEFECTS TO FIX REGARDLESS, both found in scripts/fetchFundamentals.mjs: (a) line 320's gainOnSaleRealEstate list contains 'GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1' but BXP tags us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets with no suffix, so BXP's 176,732k gain is silently read as zero; (b) src/lib/reits.mjs ffo() omits impairments entirely, which alone costs Realty Income −11.8% (3,405,150 against 3,860,323). The groupingColumns basis string 'net income + depreciation − property gains' is therefore not merely approximate, it is wrong in name (it omits impairments) and unreachable in practice (it omits gains for PLD, SPG and, through the typo, BXP). Recommend the ffo column be retired from the REIT shelf, not repaired.

(5) DIVIDEND COVERAGE — THIS IS WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS BETTER THAN FFO. Every input is a GAAP-tagged cash number, undimensioned, in companyfacts. FY2025, cash from operations less the capital spending that keeps buildings competitive and tenants in place, divided by dividends and distributions actually paid: AVB 1.42x (1,671,105 − 261,769 'Capital expenditures - existing real estate assets') / 992,333; O 1.32x (3,994,754 − 131,800 'Improvements to real estate, including leasing costs') / 2,932,919; PLD 1.18x (5,008,434 − 562,197 'Tenant improvements and lease commissions on previously leased space') / 3,764,745, falling to 1.09x if you also charge the 327,355 'Property improvements' that sits behind the custom tag pld:PaymentForPropertyImprovements; BXP 1.07x (1,245,157 − 216,687 'Building, pre-development and other capital improvements' − 338,808 'Tenant improvements') / 643,107. That BXP figure is an owner's fact worth printing on its own: the office landlord's distribution is covered 1.07 times after keeping its buildings and its tenants, before a dollar of development.
NOT COMPUTABLE, and must be said so: Simon does not disaggregate maintenance from expansion — its cash flow shows one line, us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets 934,346 labelled 'Capital expenditures, net' — so its coverage is only boundable between 0.99x (charge all of it) and 1.28x (charge none), and its dividend line is a custom tag (spg:PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividendsCommonStockAndPreferredStock 2,792,872) our concept list does not read at all, so today the pipeline sees Simon's dividends as null. Also not computable across the board: straight-line rent, tagged undimensioned by only two of five (BXP us-gaap:StraightLineRent 101,878 and us-gaap:StraightLineRentAdjustments 107,100; SPG us-gaap:StraightLineRent 27,790; PLD, O and AVB tag none). And do not reach for us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense as a real-estate-only depreciation substitute — it is present for four of five (PLD 2,011,907, SPG 1,481,880, O 1,623,713, AVB 913,376) but it excludes in-place-lease intangible amortisation, so for Prologis it is 2,012 against the 2,539 the FFO table uses.

The desk can exist. It cannot exist on a computed FFO.


### Proposed formula

Three rules, replacing the ffo column.

RULE 1 — FFO is quoted, never computed. Print only the filer's own reported figure, obtained by a Tier-2-style keyhole TEXT parse of the 10-K MD&A reconciliation table (the tables are not XBRL; the parse must anchor on the caption and capture the total line verbatim), stored with the accession number, the caption verbatim, and the measure name exactly as the filer wrote it ('NAREIT defined FFO attributable to common stockholders/unitholders', 'Dilutive FFO allocable to common stockholders', 'FFO available to common stockholders', 'FFO', 'Funds from Operations attributable to BXP, Inc.'). Label it on the page as 'as the company reports it'. If the parse does not tie, print nothing. Never our own arithmetic, and never a cross-company FFO ranking, because the five definitions above are not the same measure.

RULE 2 — the computable owner's number, from tags I confirmed this session:
  coverage = ( us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivities − Σ upkeep ) ÷ Σ distributions
  upkeep = the sum of whichever of these the filer actually tags, undimensioned, with the captured tag names printed beneath the figure:
    us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements   (O 131,800 · AVB 261,769 · BXP 216,687)
    us-gaap:PaymentsForTenantImprovements    (BXP 338,808)
    us-gaap:PaymentsForLeasingCostsCommissionsAndTenantImprovements  (PLD 562,197)
  distributions = us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock (O 2,920,895) or us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends (PLD 3,764,745 · BXP 643,107) or us-gaap:PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividends (AVB 992,333), plus us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsMinorityInterest / us-gaap:MinorityInterestDecreaseFromDistributionsToNoncontrollingInterestHolders where present (O 12,024).
  WITHHOLD the whole figure unless at least one upkeep tag and one distribution tag are captured. That single guard is what stops Simon from printing: it tags neither a disaggregated upkeep line nor a standard dividend line, so it correctly reads '—' rather than a flattering 1.28x. Where a filer lumps capex (us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets, Simon 934,346, caption 'Capital expenditures, net'), publish a RANGE with both bounds and the caption, or publish nothing — never the midpoint.

RULE 3 — tripwires, in the spirit of the correctness campaign:
  (a) For any company on the REIT shelf, if gainOnSaleRealEstate resolves to null while depreciation is non-null, refuse every depreciation-add-back derivative for that filer. Null-gain is not zero-gain; for PLD and SPG it means the gain is in an extension namespace we cannot see.
  (b) Warn when a REIT's netIncome exceeds cashFromOps (SPG FY2025: 5,364,120 against 4,136,551) — that is the signature of a large non-cash disposal gain sitting inside the earnings the FFO formula is about to add depreciation to.
  (c) Fix scripts/fetchFundamentals.mjs line 320: 'GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1' should read 'GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets' (add the correct spelling; keep both). Confirmed against BXP FY2025 = 176,732,000.
  (d) Never publish an AFFO, Core FFO, Normalized FFO or Real Estate FFO we computed. Quote them or omit them. Realty Income deducting $335 thousand of 'recurring capital expenditures' against $131,800 thousand of 'Improvements to real estate, including leasing costs' in the same filing is the reason.


### Per filer

**PLD (Prologis, CIK 0001045609, FY2025 10-K acc 0001193125-26-051453, filed 2026-02-13)** — tagged: NO. Extension schema pld-20251231.xsd contains no element whose name matches FundsFromOperation/FFO/Nareit/AdjustedFunds/CoreFunds. The raw iXBRL 10-K contains zero ix:nonFraction with an FFO-named element. The whole reconciliation table (p.45) is untagged prose/numbers — I marked every ix:nonFraction in the document and the FFO block carries none, while the income statement 40k characters earlier is densely marked. companyfacts CIK0001045609.json exposes only taxonomies dei/invest/srt/us-gaap — no extension namespace at all, so the question of dimension never arises: the fact does not exist in XBRL in any form, dimensioned or not. The Q4 earnings 8-K (0001193125-26-017256) carries FFO in Ex-99.1/99.2 as plain HTML; its XBRL is cover-page only.
- reported: NAREIT-defined FFO attributable to common stockholders/unitholders $5,680m. Verbatim build (in millions): Net earnings attributable to common stockholders 3,322 + Real estate related depreciation and amortization 2,539 − Gains on other dispositions of investments in real estate, net of taxes (excluding development properties and land) 685 + Adjustments related to noncontrolling interests (47) + Our proportionate share of adjustments related to unconsolidated entities 551 = 5,680. Then 'FFO, as modified by Prologis' 5,779 and 'Core FFO' 5,561.
- NAREIT recompute: From tags empirically confirmed in companyfacts: us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss 3,328,231 + us-gaap:DepreciationAndAmortization 2,626,028 − gain 0 (NO gain tag exists) + impairment 0 (no us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate fact) = 5,954,259 (thousands). The property-gain lines are COMPANY EXTENSION elements — pld:GainsOnOtherDispositionsOfInvestmentsInRealEstateNet 685,831 and pld:GainsOnDispositionsOfDevelopmentPropertiesAndLandNet 257,731 — which companyfacts does not carry, so the deduction the NAREIT formula requires is structurally unreachable from our data source.
- residual: +274,259k, i.e. +4.8% vs reported 5,680,000k. Composition, exact to the dollar: (total D&A 2,626,028 − real-estate-only D&A 2,539,000) = +87,028; failing to deduct the 685,831 gain = +685,831; omitting the NCI adjustment (47,000) = +47,000; omitting the +551,000 unconsolidated-entity share = −551,000. 87 + 686 + 47 − 551 = +269 (≈274 after rounding of the reported millions). The 4.8% miss is an ACCIDENT: a 686 error and a 551 error of opposite sign nearly cancel. Fix only the gain (using the custom tag) and the error widens to −411,572k / −7.2%. That is the Munger point in one line — the number is close for the wrong reason, and we cannot know that ex ante.
- adjusted names: Two beyond NAREIT FFO: 'FFO, as modified by Prologis attributable to common stockholders/unitholders' (adds unrealized FX/derivative losses, deferred tax, NCI and JV reconciling items) and 'Core FFO' (further removes gains on dispositions of development properties and land, current tax on dispositions, debt-extinguishment gains/losses). No AFFO — the single 'AFFO' string match in the 10-K is the word 'afforded'. Neither measure is machine-readable.

**SPG (Simon Property Group, CIK 0001063761, FY2025 10-K acc 0001104659-26-019419, filed 2026-02-25)** — tagged: NO. spg-20251231.xsd has no FFO-named element; no ix:nonFraction in the 10-K carries one; the FFO schedule (p.77) is entirely untagged. companyfacts exposes only dei/srt/us-gaap.
- reported: 'FFO of the Operating Partnership' $4,663,305k; 'Dilutive FFO allocable to common stockholders' $4,027,116k; 'Real Estate FFO' $4,811,778k. Verbatim build (thousands): Consolidated Net Income 5,364,120 + D&A from consolidated properties 1,410,595 + our share of D&A from unconsolidated entities incl. Klépierre/TRG 811,690 + (Gain) loss on acquisition of controlling interest, sale or disposal of, or recovery on, assets and interests in unconsolidated entities and impairment, net (2,887,460) + Net (gain) attributable to NCI holders in properties (4,815) + NCI portion of D&A (26,322) + Preferred distributions and dividends (4,503) = 4,663,305; less FFO allocable to limited partners 636,189 = 4,027,116. Simon states it follows the NAREIT Funds From Operations White Paper - 2018 Restatement.
- NAREIT recompute: us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic 4,624,275 + us-gaap:DepreciationAndAmortization 1,426,423 − gain 0 (NONE AVAILABLE) + impairment 0 (no us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate fact) = 6,050,698 (thousands). Simon bundles gains AND impairments into a single extension element, spg:AcquisitionOfControllingInterestSaleOrDisposalOfOrRecoveryOnAssetsAndInterestsInUnconsolidatedEntitiesGainOrLossAndImpairmentNet = 2,887,460, plus spg:GainLossOnDisposalExchangeOrEvaluationOfEquityInterests = (86,119). Neither is in companyfacts. There is no way to separate the gain from the impairment even if we could read the tag — they are one number in the filing.
- residual: +2,023,582k, i.e. +50.2% vs the 4,027,116k a shareholder is entitled to; +29.8% even against the whole-partnership 4,663,305k. Composition: essentially the entire 2,887,460 gain we cannot see, partly offset by the 811,690 JV depreciation we cannot add and the 636,189 limited-partner share we do not remove. This is the disqualifying case — a 50% overstatement in a year Simon booked a near-$2.9bn gain, on the second-largest name in the sector. Feeding the custom tag in does not rescue it: 4,624,275 + 1,426,423 − 2,887,460 = 3,163,238, which is −21.5%, because the same tag also contains the impairment that NAREIT adds back.
- adjusted names: One: 'Real Estate FFO' (FFO excluding other platform investments net of tax, gain/loss on disposal-exchange-revaluation of equity interests net of tax, and unrealized fair-value moves on publicly traded equity and derivative instruments). No AFFO, no Core FFO, no Normalized FFO — zero string matches for any of them. Not machine-readable. Note also that Simon's dividends-paid line is a custom tag, spg:PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividendsCommonStockAndPreferredStock 2,792,872, so our dividendsPaid concept list ['PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock','PaymentsOfDividends'] reads null for Simon.

**O (Realty Income, CIK 0000726728, FY2025 10-K acc 0000726728-26-000011, filed 2026-02-25)** — tagged: NO. o-20251231.xsd has no FFO-named element; the FFO, Normalized FFO and AFFO tables (pp.45-48) carry no ix:nonFraction markers. companyfacts exposes dei/srt/us-gaap/ffd only.
- reported: FFO available to common stockholders $3,860,323k (diluted FFO 3,869,719k); Normalized FFO 3,884,537k; AFFO 3,885,898k. Verbatim build (thousands): Net income available to common stockholders 1,058,590 + Depreciation and amortization 2,524,200 + Depreciation of furniture, fixtures and equipment (2,622) + Provisions for impairment of real estate 434,497 + Gain on sales of real estate (177,640) + Proportionate share of adjustments for unconsolidated entities 33,345 + FFO adjustments allocable to noncontrolling interests (10,047) = 3,860,323.
- NAREIT recompute: All four inputs exist as undimensioned us-gaap facts: us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss 1,058,590 + us-gaap:DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization 2,524,200 − us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfProperties 177,640 + us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate 471,335 = 3,876,485 (thousands). This is the only one of the five where the NAREIT formula is fully computable from standard tags. Note our pipeline as written omits impairment entirely, which would print 3,405,150 — a −11.8% miss.
- residual: +16,162k, i.e. +0.42%. Composition, exact: the income-statement impairment tag 471,335 exceeds the FFO table's 'provisions for impairment of real estate' 434,497 by 36,838 (that 36,838 is the non-cash change in allowance for credit losses, which reappears verbatim in the AFFO bridge — the impairment tag is not real-estate-only); we omit the FF&E depreciation reversal (2,622) = +2,622; we omit the unconsolidated-entity share +33,345 = −33,345; we omit the NCI adjustment (10,047) = +10,047. 36,838 + 2,622 − 33,345 + 10,047 = 16,162.
- adjusted names: Two beyond FFO, both defined by management: 'Normalized FFO' (FFO excluding merger, transaction and other costs, net — 24,214 in FY2025) and 'AFFO' (Normalized FFO plus 12 further adjustments: amortization of debt discounts and deferred financing costs, amortization of acquired interest-rate-swap value, leasing costs and commissions (9,481), recurring capital expenditures (335), non-cash change in allowance for credit losses, share-based compensation, straight-line rent and expenses net (169,217), above/below-market lease amortization, deferred tax, JV share, preferred redemption excess, other). Realty Income explicitly warns its AFFO 'may not be comparable to AFFO, CAD or FAD reported by other companies'. None machine-readable. The owner-relevant scandal is arithmetic: AFFO deducts $335 THOUSAND of 'recurring capital expenditures' while the cash flow statement shows us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements of $131,800 thousand under the label 'Improvements to real estate, including leasing costs' — a 393x gap, entirely a definitional choice by management.

**AVB (AvalonBay Communities, CIK 0000915912, FY2025 10-K acc 0000915912-26-000004, filed 2026-02-27)** — tagged: NO. avb-20251231.xsd has no FFO-named element; the FFO/Core FFO table (p.43) carries no ix:nonFraction markers. companyfacts exposes dei/invest/srt/us-gaap/ecd/ffd. The Q4 8-K (0000915912-26-000002) confirms the pattern: its extension schema is 1,786 bytes and its instance 2,821 bytes — cover-page dei facts only — while the 487kB Ex-99.1 press release carrying FFO is untagged HTML.
- reported: FFO $1,627,863k; Core FFO $1,605,577k. Verbatim build (thousands): Net income attributable to common stockholders 1,051,301 + Depreciation - real estate assets, including joint venture adjustments 905,701 + Income attributable to noncontrolling interests 5,298 + Gain on sale of previously depreciated real estate, net (335,713) + Casualty loss and impairment on real estate 1,276 = 1,627,863. FFO per diluted share $11.40; Core FFO per diluted share $11.24.
- NAREIT recompute: us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic 1,051,301 + us-gaap:Depreciation 913,376 − us-gaap:GainsLossesOnSalesOfInvestmentRealEstate 335,713 + us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate 1,276 = 1,630,240 (thousands). All four confirmed undimensioned. Trap to note: AVB also tags us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfProperties 339,954, a DIFFERENT number that is not the FFO input; our concept list happens to prefer GainsLossesOnSalesOfInvestmentRealEstate and so picks correctly, but only by ordering luck.
- residual: +2,377k, i.e. +0.15%. Composition, exact: consolidated us-gaap:Depreciation 913,376 exceeds the FFO table's real-estate-plus-JV depreciation 905,701 by +7,675; we omit the +5,298 noncontrolling-interest add-back = −5,298. 7,675 − 5,298 = 2,377. The tightest fit of the five, and the reason is structural: AvalonBay is an unlevered-by-JV, single-asset-class filer whose income statement uses stock us-gaap tags throughout.
- adjusted names: One: 'Core FFO', defined by an explicit 13-item list (JV gains, casualty/impairment on non-depreciable assets, debt extinguishment, expensed pursuit costs, legal recoveries and costs, insurance proceeds, non-depreciable asset gains, advocacy contributions, hedge ineffectiveness, SIP credit-loss changes, severance, executive transition compensation, for-sale condominium activity, income taxes). No AFFO. Not machine-readable.

**BXP (BXP Inc / Boston Properties, CIK 0001037540, FY2025 10-K acc 0001037540-26-000006, filed 2026-02-27)** — tagged: NO. bxp-20251231.xsd has no FFO-named element (the only near-match in its extension is 'Writeoffoffullydepreciatedrealestate'); the four FFO reconciliation tables (pp.88-90, BXP Inc and BPLP, basic and diluted) carry no ix:nonFraction markers. companyfacts exposes dei/srt/us-gaap/ecd.
- reported: FFO attributable to the Operating Partnership common unitholders (including BXP, Inc.) $1,209,636k; FFO attributable to BXP, Inc. $1,089,035k (90.03% share); diluted FFO attributable to BXP, Inc. $1,089,398k. Verbatim build (thousands): Net income attributable to BXP, Inc. 276,800 + NCI-common units of the OP 32,014 + NCI in property partnerships 75,181 = Net income 383,995; + D&A 912,088 − NCI in property partnerships' share of D&A 86,109 + BXP's share of JV D&A 65,446 − corporate-related D&A 2,479 + non-real-estate D&A 8,521 + loss on sales-type lease 2,490 + impairment losses 85,803 + impairment losses within loss from unconsolidated JVs 145,133 − gain on sale/consolidation within JVs 53,685 − gains on sales of real estate 176,732 + unrealized loss on non-real-estate investments 346 − NCI in property partnerships 75,181 = 1,209,636. I re-added this chain and it ties to the dollar.
- NAREIT recompute: us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss 276,800 + us-gaap:DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization 912,088 − us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets 176,732 + us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate 85,803 = 1,097,959 (thousands). CRITICAL PIPELINE DEFECT FOUND: our gainOnSaleRealEstate concept list in scripts/fetchFundamentals.mjs line 320 contains 'GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1' (with the trailing 1) — a different element. BXP tags us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets, no suffix. As shipped, the pipeline reads gain = 0 and prints 1,188,888 (+9.2%), or 1,274,691 (+17.0%) if impairment were added.
- residual: +8,924k, i.e. +0.82% vs FFO attributable to BXP, Inc. — but −111,677k / −9.2% vs FFO of the Operating Partnership, and the 0.82% is again coincidence. We start from net income after 107,195 of NCI, then add total consolidated D&A that still contains 86,109 of NCI-partnership depreciation, and we are blind to +145,133 of JV impairments, +65,446 of JV depreciation and −53,685 of JV gains. Those JV items net to +156,894 and happen to be roughly the mirror of the 120,601 OP-unitholder share we never subtract. Two errors of ~10% of FFO each, pointing opposite ways.
- adjusted names: NONE. BXP reports FFO only — zero occurrences of AFFO, Core FFO, Normalized FFO, CAD or FAD in the 10-K. It is the one filer of the five that does not manufacture a second measure, and it is also the one whose reported FFO carries the largest unreachable joint-venture content.


---

## 4. MUNGER INVERSION — THE TRAPS

### Straight-line rent: revenue booked years before the cash arrives
- **Verdict:** VERIFIED, and the more useful figure is the balance-sheet STOCK, not the annual flow
- **Example:** BXP FY2025 10-K (acc 0001037540-26-000006, filed 2026-02-27): the cash-flow line reads 'Accrued rental income, net (101,878)' — a deduction from net income of $101.878m. Against revenue of $3,482.279m that is 2.9%; against net income attributable to BXP ($276.800m) it is 36.8%; against BXP's OWN reported FFO ($1,089.035m, its Nareit reconciliation) it is 9.4%. The cumulative stock is far more telling: DeferredRentReceivablesNet ran $1,038.788m (2019) → $1,538.515m (2025), i.e. 29.9% of BXP's book equity of $5,147.190m is rent recognised but never received, and it has grown every single year. BXP's own taxable-income reconciliation subtracts 'Straight-line rent and net above- and below-market rent adjustments (91,180)' to bridge GAAP net income of $276.800m to taxable income of $600.045m. Realty Income (O) shows the same stock growing 6x in six years: DeferredRentReceivablesNet $147.047m (2019) → $880.341m (2025), though only 2.2% of its much larger equity. Vornado FY2025: StraightLineRent $71.960m, DeferredRentReceivablesNet $752.545m = 12.6% of equity ($5,986.727m). REFUTATION of the universal case: Simon's straight-line rent is trivial and frequently NEGATIVE — us-gaap:StraightLineRent was −19.950m (2020), −22.619m (2021), −25.234m (2022), −9.866m (2023), −0.802m (2024), then +27.790m (2025), i.e. 0.4% of $6,364.505m revenue. A mall REIT with percentage rent and short leases does not accrue what a 15-year office lease does. Annaly has no such tag at all, correctly.
- **XBRL:** STRUCTURED, partially. Flow: us-gaap:StraightLineRent (confirmed with FY values for BXP, SPG, VNO; the tag EXISTS for Realty Income but carries no annual 10-K values, so O's flow must be withheld). BXP additionally carries us-gaap:StraightLineRentAdjustments ($107.100m FY2025) which does NOT equal StraightLineRent ($101.878m) — two tags, two numbers, same idea. Stock: us-gaap:DeferredRentReceivablesNet (confirmed BXP, VNO, O; ABSENT for SPG). Also present: us-gaap:IncreaseDecreaseInDeferredRentReceivables (BXP, SPG, VNO). The sign convention is NOT stable across filers or years, and the flow does not reconcile to the change in the stock (BXP 2025 flow $101.878m vs stock change $72.295m) because of write-offs and dispositions.
- **Gate:** Build the STOCK line, not the flow: 'Rent booked but not yet received' = us-gaap:DeferredRentReceivablesNet ÷ stockholders' equity, with the 7-year trend. Label bands on the trend, not the level (a rising balance in a falling-rent market is the tell). Show the flow only as a secondary 'of which recognised this year' and NEVER assume its sign — take absolute value and label direction from the sign observed. Withhold entirely where DeferredRentReceivablesNet is absent (SPG) rather than substitute the flow. Never net StraightLineRent against StraightLineRentAdjustments.

### Maintenance vs development capex: management defines the split, and often does not disclose one
- **Verdict:** VERIFIED in the strongest form — for two of four equity REITs read, the split does not exist in the filing at all, and where it exists the tags mean OPPOSITE things across filers
- **Example:** SIMON (FY2025 10-K, acc 0001104659-26-019419): the investing section carries ONE line, 'Capital expenditures, net (934,346)'. There is no maintenance/development split anywhere in the cash flow statement. An owner cannot separate them. BXP splits three ways — 'Construction in progress (683,750)', 'Building, pre-development and other capital improvements (216,687)', 'Tenant improvements (338,808)' — but the MD&A footnote says the $216.687m 'included approximately $39.4 million ... of pre-development expenditures' (2024: $47.9m), so 18% of BXP's own 'capital improvements' bucket is development, and that disclosure is untagged prose. BXP also charges 'Tenant leasing costs (122,618)' inside OPERATING cash flow, so its OCF is already net of leasing commissions while Simon's is not (Simon capitalises leasing costs and amortises them inside D&A). VORNADO is the mirror-image gap: us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements has NO values, only us-gaap:PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets ($144.609m FY2025) — development visible, maintenance invisible. THE TAG TRAP: BXP's DEVELOPMENT line is tagged us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherProductiveAssets ($683.750m) while Simon's ALL-IN capex is tagged us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets ($934.346m). Same tag family, opposite meanings. Best available evidence of the true cost of keeping tenants is text-only: Vornado's FY2025 leasing table shows tenant improvements and leasing commissions of $148.41 per square foot on second-generation New York office relets = 13.4% of initial rent, or $13.13 per square foot per annum against initial rent of $90.69.
- **XBRL:** MIXED and non-comparable. Confirmed tags: us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements (BXP $216.687m, O $131.800m; EMPTY for VNO, ABSENT for SPG), us-gaap:PaymentsForTenantImprovements (BXP $338.808m only — not used by SPG, VNO or O), us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherProductiveAssets (BXP, = development), us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets (SPG, = everything), us-gaap:PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets (VNO), us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireCommercialRealEstate (BXP, O), us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireRealEstateAndRealEstateJointVentures (SPG). There is NO us-gaap element for 'recurring' or 'maintenance' capex — I searched every tag name in all five filers' companyfacts and found none. It is management's word, not an accounting concept.
- **Gate:** Do NOT build a cross-filer 'maintenance capex' or AFFO. Build a per-filer capex ladder that names what was actually tagged and refuses to total across filers: 'Capital spending, as the filing splits it' listing each tagged line with its filing label. Add a hard gate: only compute any 'cash after keeping the buildings' figure when BOTH a capital-improvements tag AND a tenant-improvements tag carry values (true for BXP; false for SPG, VNO); otherwise print 'This filer reports one undifferentiated capital-expenditure line — the split between keeping the buildings and building new ones is not in the filing.' Also carry a comparability flag for where leasing commissions sit (operating for BXP, capitalised for SPG).

### Capitalised interest: interest buried into development cost instead of charged against earnings
- **Verdict:** VERIFIED and extractable, but the tag and the denominator both fragment across filers
- **Example:** BXP FY2025 supplemental cash-flow disclosure: 'Cash paid for interest (net of amounts capitalized) $677,156' and 'Interest capitalized $50,626'. Against expensed interest of $653.138m, 7.2% of all interest incurred was added to buildings rather than charged to earnings — an unbroken series 2019-2025 ($54.911m, $53.881m, $53.097m, $52.130m, $42.633m, $41.976m, $50.626m). SIMON FY2025: $30.961m capitalised against $974.835m expensed = 3.1%. VORNADO is the heaviest and the most awkward: $38.648m capitalised against interest of $392.516m incurred = 9.8%, but VNO tags it as us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalizedAdjustment (its older us-gaap:InterestPaidCapitalized series stops in 2022) and does not tag us-gaap:InterestExpense at all — it reports us-gaap:InterestAndDebtExpense ($353.868m). REALTY INCOME shows the counter-case: us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized last carries a value in 2019 ($0.751m) — a net-lease REIT that buys finished buildings capitalises essentially nothing, so a zero must be withheld, not printed as virtue.
- **XBRL:** STRUCTURED, three-tag family, all empirically confirmed: us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized (BXP, SPG, O), us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalizedAdjustment (VNO), us-gaap:InterestPaidCapitalized (VNO, through 2022 only). Denominators: us-gaap:InterestExpense (BXP, SPG), us-gaap:InterestAndDebtExpense (VNO), us-gaap:InterestPaidNet (all, labelled 'Interest Paid, Excluding Capitalized Interest').
- **Gate:** Build 'Interest charged to buildings, not to earnings' = capitalised interest ÷ (capitalised + expensed interest), reading the capitalised figure from the three-tag family and the expensed figure from InterestExpense OR InterestAndDebtExpense. Gate: withhold when the capitalised tag has no value for the year rather than printing 0% (Realty Income). Label it as what it is: 'X% of this year's interest bill never passed through earnings.' Pair it with construction-in-progress spend so the reader sees why.

### Gains on sale — and non-cash remeasurement gains — recycled through earnings
- **Verdict:** VERIFIED, and it is worse than the brief states: the largest 'gain' in the pool this year was not a sale at all, and no us-gaap tag exposes it
- **Example:** SIMON FY2025: consolidated net income $5,364.120m against $2,729.021m in 2024 — a doubling. The cash-flow statement removes '(Gain) loss on acquisition of controlling interests, sale or disposal of, or recovery on, assets and interests in unconsolidated entities and impairment, net (2,887,460)'. Simon's own MD&A: 'a non-cash gain of $2.9 billion, or $7.56 per diluted share/unit, related to the remeasurement of our previously held 88% noncontrolling equity interest' — diluted EPS went $7.26 → $14.17 and $7.56 of that is a bookkeeping remeasurement on consolidating something Simon already owned. VORNADO FY2025: net income attributable to common $842.851m versus its OWN Nareit FFO of $486.826m; the wedge is an $803.248m non-cash 'Gain on sales-type lease' plus $90.762m of joint-venture gains on sale. Net income more than 70% above FFO. BXP FY2025: gains on sales of real estate $176.732m; net income attributable to BXP went $14.272m (2024) → $276.800m (2025), and $176.1m of that $262.5m increase is simply the swing in gains. The tax law confirms it: BXP's dividend tax-character table shows 35.48% of the 2025 dividend was 'Capital gain income' ($1.29 of $3.64 per share) versus 0.79% in 2024. REALTY INCOME is the clean case: us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfProperties $177.640m = 16.8% of net income $1,058.590m, properly tagged.
- **XBRL:** BADLY FRAGMENTED, and this is a LIVE BUG in the repo. C:/Users/ryanr/.surplus-scout/ownerscorecard-terminal/scripts/fetchFundamentals.mjs line 320 lists gainOnSaleRealEstate as [GainLossOnSaleOfPropertiesNetOfApplicableIncomeTaxes, GainLossOnDispositionOfRealEstate, GainsLossesOnSalesOfInvestmentRealEstate, GainLossOnSaleOfProperties, GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1]. BXP tags its gain as us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets — WITHOUT the trailing '1' — confirmed value 176,732,000 for 2025-01-01..2025-12-31. That exact spelling is not in the list. BXP's GainLossOnSaleOfProperties series stops in 2018 and GainLossOnSaleOfPropertiesNetOfApplicableIncomeTaxes stops in 2014. So BXP's gain is silently dropped and its FFO is overstated by $176.7m. SIMON tags its $2.887bn gain ONLY as the company extension spg:AcquisitionOfControllingInterestSaleOrDisposalOfOrRecoveryOnAssetsAndInterestsInUnconsolidatedEntitiesGainOrLossAndImpairmentNet — verified in the raw inline-XBRL of spg-20251231x10k.htm — and company extension namespaces are absent from the companyfacts API (SPG's companyfacts exposes only dei, srt, us-gaap). Vornado's $803.248m sales-type-lease gain carries no inline tag at all in the FFO table. Working tags confirmed: us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfProperties (O), us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets (BXP), us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1 (VNO).
- **Gate:** (a) Add us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets to the gain concept list immediately — that is a one-line correctness fix worth $176.7m on BXP's printed FFO. (b) Build a positive tripwire rather than trusting the gain tag: when net income jumps more than ~50% year-on-year and no gain tag carries a value, WITHHOLD the FFO figure and print 'This year's earnings contain a large item we could not identify from the tagged filing.' Simon FY2025 trips this. (c) Add a 'Gains as a share of the change in earnings' line: gain ÷ (net income this year − net income last year), which for BXP 2025 reads 67%.

### Unconsolidated joint ventures: debt and assets held off the balance sheet at share
- **Verdict:** VERIFIED for Vornado and Simon; REFUTED as a universal for BXP, where the off-balance-sheet debt is almost exactly offset by partners' share of consolidated debt
- **Example:** VORNADO FY2025 Item 7A: 'Pro rata share of debt of non-consolidated entities $2,478,544' against 'Consolidated debt $7,214,457'. Look-through debt is 34.4% higher than the balance sheet shows. Vornado's share of JV depreciation ($94.867m) and JV gains on sale ($90.762m) sit only in the untagged FFO table. SIMON's joint-venture summarised balance sheet is the starkest: JV total assets $15,608.818m against JV MORTGAGES of $16,374.773m and a partners' DEFICIT of $(2,385,343); Simon's share of that deficit is $(1,247,554). Sixteen billion dollars of mortgage debt sits outside Simon's $28,430.175m of consolidated long-term debt, against depreciated assets worth less than the mortgages. Simon does NOT disclose its share of the JV DEBT in that table — only its share of the deficit — so a look-through leverage ratio cannot be computed honestly for Simon. BXP REFUTES the blanket claim: its MD&A capitalisation table reads Consolidated Debt $16,609,483, ADD BXP's share of unconsolidated joint venture debt $1,221,666, SUBTRACT Partners' share of Consolidated Debt $1,364,360, giving BXP's Share of Debt $16,466,789 — 0.9% BELOW the consolidated figure. The two adjustments nearly cancel. Each gross component is still 7-8% of debt, and BXP's JV losses are where the pain hides: us-gaap:IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments was −$343.177m (2024) and −$239.543m (2023), of which BXP's own FFO table adds back $341.338m and $272.603m as JV impairments.
- **XBRL:** MOSTLY INVISIBLE to the companyfacts API, for two distinct reasons. (1) EXTENSION TAGS: Vornado's pro-rata JV debt is tagged vno:EquityMethodInvestmentProRataShareDebtOfPartiallyOwnedEntities — verified in the raw inline XBRL — a company namespace companyfacts drops. (2) DIMENSIONAL FACTS: Simon's JV mortgages ARE tagged with us-gaap:LongTermDebt and JV assets with us-gaap:Assets, but dimensionally qualified to an unconsolidated-entities member. I verified empirically that companyfacts returns ONLY the undimensioned fact (SPG us-gaap:LongTermDebt at 2025-12-31 returns 28,430,175,000 and nothing else) — so the $16.37bn is tagged in the filing and still unreachable through the API we consume. (3) PLAIN TEXT: BXP's $1,221,666 and $16,609,483 carry no inline tag whatsoever. What IS visible: us-gaap:IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments (all four equity REITs), us-gaap:InvestmentsInAffiliatesSubsidiariesAssociatesAndJointVentures (BXP $999.309m, 2025), us-gaap:EquityMethodInvestmentUnderlyingEquityInNetAssets (SPG).
- **Gate:** Never print a look-through leverage figure — the input is not reachable for three of four filers. Instead build an honest ABSENCE line: 'Leverage shown here is the consolidated balance sheet only. This trust also holds interests in properties it does not consolidate [carrying value from us-gaap:InvestmentsInAffiliatesSubsidiariesAssociatesAndJointVentures]; its share of the debt inside them is disclosed only in the 10-K text and is not in the filed data.' Add a severity flag from what IS tagged: when |IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments| exceeds 20% of net income (BXP 2024: $343.177m against $14.272m), label the joint ventures as material and point the reader to the note.

### The dividend: is it earned, or borrowed, or sold off the roof?
- **Verdict:** VERIFIED — and two of the three largest filers read failed the honest test in FY2025, by small margins, which is exactly what makes it worth publishing
- **Example:** BXP FY2025: operating cash flow $1,245.157m less capital improvements $216.687m less tenant improvements $338.808m = $689.662m available. Dividends and distributions $643.107m plus distributions to noncontrolling interests in property partnerships $83.129m = $726.236m paid. SHORT BY $36.6m — before a single dollar of the $683.750m spent on construction in progress, which was substantially funded by $682.514m of proceeds from selling buildings. BXP's own tax table confirms the character: 35.48% of the 2025 dividend was capital gain income, and in 2024 10.92% ($0.40 per share) was pure return of capital. SIMON FY2025: operating cash flow $4,136.551m less capital expenditures $934.346m = $3,202.205m available; dividends and distributions $2,792.872m + $439.384m to limited partners + $1.166m preferred = $3,233.422m paid. SHORT BY $31.2m, funded by net new borrowing of $390.4m (debt proceeds $3,668.892m less repayments $3,278.469m). REALTY INCOME is the counterexample and deserves saying so: OCF $3,994.754m less capital improvements $131.800m = $3,862.954m against dividends paid $2,920.895m — a 75.6% payout, comfortably earned. Its growth, not its dividend, is what the capital markets fund: diluted shares went 316.2m (2019) → 908.3m (2025) while it spent $4,647.873m on acquisitions in 2025 alone.
- **XBRL:** STRUCTURED for BXP, O and NLY; ENTIRELY ABSENT for SIMON — the single most important number for the largest US mall REIT is unreachable. Simon's actual payment ($2,792.872m) is tagged spg:PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividendsCommonStockAndPreferredStock, a company extension. us-gaap:PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividends stops at FY2018. The ONLY us-gaap dividend flow Simon has tagged since 2019 is us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsMinorityInterest = $439.384m — the operating partnership's limited partners. Any pipeline that reached for 'whatever dividend tag exists' would print $439m against a real $2,793m, a 6.4x understatement. There is no fallback: Simon's us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared carries one stray 2020 value. Confirmed working: us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends (BXP $643.107m, NLY $1,882.237m), us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock (O $2,920.895m, VNO $141.277m). The tax character of the dividend is tagged only as an extension (bxp:DividendsTreatmentForFederalTaxPurposesPercentage) and Simon and Vornado do not disclose it in the 10-K at all.
- **Gate:** Build 'Was the distribution earned?' = operating cash flow − (capital improvements + tenant improvements) − ALL distributions including those to noncontrolling partners and OP unit holders, printed as a dollar surplus or shortfall, not a ratio. Gate it hard: compute ONLY where the capex split exists (BXP, O) and where the total-distribution figure is complete; otherwise print 'The distribution is not fully captured in this filer's tagged data' with an explicit named reason. Add a companion line, 'Sold buildings funded X% of this year's distribution', from proceeds-of-sale versus the shortfall. Do NOT widen the dividendsPaid concept list to include PaymentsOfDividendsMinorityInterest — that is the trap, not the fix.

### MORTGAGE REITS: our lines, and which are actively wrong
- **Verdict:** PARTLY REFUTED — the routing is already right, and one feared trap is already gated; but the return-of-capital reality is not shown anywhere, and two live errors remain
- **Example:** ANNALY (NLY, FY2025 10-K acc 0001043219-26-000013). ALREADY RIGHT: archetype.mjs routes SIC 6500-6799 filers with net interest income and negligible depreciation to kind 'bank', subtype 'mortgage-reit' — verified that NLY tags us-gaap:InterestIncomeExpenseNet ($1,135.500m, 2025) and has no depreciation values, and AGNC likewise tags InterestIncomeExpenseNet with no depreciation tag at all. Neither gets the FFO scorecard. ALSO ALREADY RIGHT: efficiencyRatio() in financials.mjs requires netInterestIncome > 0 and noninterestIncome > 0 — which saves us, because NLY's net interest income was NEGATIVE $111.384m in 2023 and an ungated ratio would have printed a flattering 'Low cost ratio' grade on a negative denominator. WRONG #1 — return on equity: returnOnEquity() divides us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss (before preferred dividends) by us-gaap:StockholdersEquity (which INCLUDES $1,802.480m of preferred, 11.2% of NLY's 2025 equity). NLY 2025 prints 12.60% against a true return to common of 13.08%; in 2023 it prints −14.60% against a true −18.36%. WRONG #2 — the dividend figure: CONCEPTS.dividendsPaid lists us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock FIRST, and for NLY FY2025 that tag carries $494.881m — which is NOT the year's dividend but 'Distributions paid to common stockholders after period end', the January payment, verified by reading the surrounding text in the filing. The real cash-flow line is 'Dividends paid (1,882,237)' tagged us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends. A 3.8x understatement waiting to print. MISSING ENTIRELY — the whole story: common book value per share fell from $31.88 (2021) to $20.21 (2025), −36.6%, while dividends declared over 2022-2025 totalled $11.52 per share against a book-value decline of $11.67 per share. Across 2019-2025 Annaly earned cumulative net income of $2,447m and paid dividends of $10,937m. Annaly's own 10-K says distributions 'could be classified as return of capital' and that it 'may make additional returns of capital'. Its own non-GAAP 'earnings available for distribution' was $2.92 per share against a $2.80 declared dividend in 2025, but was $1,554m in 2023 while GAAP net income was −$1,643m.
- **XBRL:** BOOK VALUE PER SHARE IS EXTRACTABLE AND I VERIFIED IT TO THE PENNY: (us-gaap:StockholdersEquity − us-gaap:PreferredStockValue) ÷ us-gaap:CommonStockSharesOutstanding gives $20.21 (2025), $19.14 (2024), $19.44 (2023) against Annaly's disclosed $20.21, $19.15, $19.44. Leverage inputs are visible but fragmented: us-gaap:SecuritiesSoldUnderAgreementsToRepurchaseGross ($84,303.067m) and us-gaap:SecuritySoldUnderAgreementToRepurchaseAfterOffsetSubjectToMasterNettingArrangement ($81,865.723m) differ by $2.4bn, and the older us-gaap:SecuritiesSoldUnderAgreementsToRepurchase series stops at 2022. Annaly's own GAAP leverage of 7.2:1 (total GAAP debt $116,188.855m ÷ total equity $16,159.911m) is a text table. 'Earnings available for distribution', 'economic leverage' and 'economic book value' are non-GAAP, untagged, management-defined — reproduce none of them. The return-of-capital character of the dividend is TEXT ONLY for Annaly. NOTE a preferred-stock landmine: NLY does not tag us-gaap:PreferredStockLiquidationPreferenceValue at all (its PreferredStockValue happens to equal liquidation value); AGNC tags both. A filer carrying preferred at par would break the BVPS formula silently.
- **Gate:** (1) Build 'Common book value per share' for mortgage REITs from the verified formula, with the seven-year record beside the dividends declared per share — that single pairing tells the whole return-of-capital story without our making a claim. Gate it: require the preferred deduction to be present AND to be more than ~1% of equity (otherwise it is par value, not liquidation preference) and withhold if not. (2) Fix returnOnEquity for this subtype: numerator us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic, denominator equity less preferred. (3) Reorder CONCEPTS.dividendsPaid to prefer PaymentsOfDividends over PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock, or add a sanity gate that rejects a dividend figure below 40% of the declared-per-share × shares cross-check. (4) Add an explicit label wherever a mortgage REIT's dividend is shown: 'A mortgage REIT's distribution is not an earnings figure. Read it against the change in book value per share.' (5) Note that src/data/reit-subsectors.json contains no mortgage REITs and no mortgage subsector — the classification depends entirely on the archetype tie-break, so the tie-break must never be loosened.

### OUR OWN FFO DEFINITION IS THE LARGEST WRONG NUMBER IN THE SHELF
- **Verdict:** VERIFIED against each filer's own Nareit reconciliation — 'net income + depreciation − property gains' is not FFO and should not be labelled FFO
- **Example:** VORNADO FY2025 is the worst: the repo formula gives $904.955m (NetIncomeLoss) + $481.456m (D&A) − $35.291m (GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1) = $1,351.120m. Vornado's OWN Nareit reconciliation in the same 10-K arrives at FFO attributable to common shareholders of $486.826m. We would print 2.8 times the truth. The bridge Vornado uses and we do not: it strips an $803.248m sales-type-lease gain, adds only $411.114m of real-property D&A (not the whole $481.456m), adds its SHARE of joint-venture D&A ($94.867m), strips its share of JV gains ($90.762m), and adjusts for noncontrolling interests (+$32.270m). BXP FY2025: the repo formula gives $276.800m + $912.088m = $1,188.888m because the gain tag is missed — 9.2% above BXP's reported FFO of $1,089.035m. Fix the gain tag and it gives $1,012.156m, 7.1% BELOW, because it still misses BXP's share of JV depreciation (+$65.446m), impairments (+$85.803m consolidated and +$145.133m inside JV losses), the noncontrolling partners' share of depreciation (−$86.109m), and the OP unit holders' share of FFO (−$120.601m). SIMON: the formula would add depreciation to a net income containing the $2,887.460m remeasurement gain. The structural defect is visible in the arithmetic itself: we add 100% of the entity's depreciation to the PARENT's share of income. BXP's net income attributable to BXP is $276.800m against whole-entity net income of $383.995m — a 28% gap — while D&A of $912.088m is 100% of the entity.
- **XBRL:** FFO IS NEVER XBRL-TAGGED. I confirmed across five filers that companyfacts exposes only dei, srt, us-gaap, ecd and ffd — no company namespaces — and separately confirmed by reading the raw inline XBRL that the FFO reconciliation tables in the BXP and Vornado 10-Ks carry no inline tags at all. FFO lives in MD&A, which is not detail-tagged. The same is true of AFFO, 'recurring capex', same-store NOI, earnings available for distribution and economic book value. There is no route to the industry's own headline number from filed structured data.
- **Gate:** Rename the column and the check. It is not 'Funds from operations' — call it 'Earnings before property depreciation' with the basis line 'net income + depreciation − property gains, our arithmetic on the filed data; this is NOT the trust's reported Nareit FFO, which we cannot compute from tagged data.' Then add three gates before the number prints at all: (a) withhold when whole-entity income (ProfitLoss) exceeds parent income (NetIncomeLoss) by more than ~15%, because adding entity-wide depreciation to parent income is then apples-to-oranges — BXP trips this at 28%; (b) withhold when |IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments| exceeds 20% of net income, because joint-venture depreciation and impairments are then material and unreachable; (c) withhold when net income jumps more than 50% with no gain tag captured. Under those gates BXP, Simon and Vornado would all correctly show a withheld figure with a stated reason. Files: C:/Users/ryanr/.surplus-scout/ownerscorecard-terminal/src/lib/reits.mjs (ffo, ffoPerShare, ffoMargin, ffoPayout, reitQuality) and src/lib/groupingColumns.mjs line 65.

### Impairments and joint-venture losses swing 'earnings growth' both ways
- **Verdict:** EXTENDED — this is missing from the brief's list and it is larger than gains on sale for the office REITs
- **Example:** BXP's equity-method line, us-gaap:IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments, was −$239.543m (2023), −$343.177m (2024) and −$103.560m (2025). BXP's own FFO table shows why: 'Impairment losses included within loss from unconsolidated joint ventures' of $272.603m, $341.338m and $145.133m in those three years, plus consolidated impairment losses of $85.803m in 2025 and $13.615m in 2024. Read on GAAP net income, BXP's 2025 looks like a 19x improvement ($14.272m → $276.800m); read on its own FFO it was a DECLINE ($1,120.478m → $1,089.035m). Two opposite verdicts from the same filing. Vornado's 650 Madison Avenue joint venture — written to zero in 2022 — received a notice of default in October 2025 on an $800m non-recourse mortgage; the $244.543m non-recourse mortgage on 888 Seventh Avenue matured unpaid in December 2025 and lenders declared default. Neither event moves a consolidated leverage ratio by a cent.
- **XBRL:** PARTIALLY STRUCTURED. us-gaap:IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments is confirmed with full annual series for BXP, SPG, VNO and O — that is the tripwire input. The decomposition into impairment versus operations is untagged MD&A. us-gaap:EquityMethodInvestmentOtherThanTemporaryImpairment exists as a tag name for SPG and VNO but I did not confirm annual values for it. The JV loan defaults are narrative text only.
- **Gate:** Build a 'Joint ventures' flag driven by us-gaap:IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments: when the absolute value exceeds 20% of net income, mark the year and suppress any year-over-year earnings-growth language. Add a plain sentence: 'A large part of this year's result came from properties this trust does not consolidate; the filing's note, not the balance sheet, is where the debt inside them is described.'

### Reverse splits corrupt a mortgage REIT's per-share history inside a single tagged series
- **Verdict:** EXTENDED — not on the brief's list, and it silently manufactures a 287% dividend 'increase'
- **Example:** Annaly's companyfacts series for us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared reads $1.05 (2019), $0.91 (2020), $3.52 (2021), $3.52 (2022), $2.60 (2023), $2.60 (2024), $2.80 (2025). us-gaap:CommonStockSharesOutstanding reads 1,430,106,199 (2019), 1,398,240,618 (2020), then 364,934,065 (2021). Nothing economic happened between 2020 and 2021 — the later filings restate for the 1-for-4 reverse split while the rows sourced from older filings do not. Any per-share growth line spanning that seam reports a 287% dividend increase and a 74% share-count reduction that never occurred. The pre-split rows and post-split rows carry the same tag, the same units and no marker distinguishing them.
- **XBRL:** STRUCTURED but poisoned. The discontinuity is detectable arithmetically — a ~4x step in per-share values and an inverse ~4x step in share counts between adjacent fiscal years — and us-gaap:StockholdersEquityNoteStockSplitConversionRatio1 is present in Annaly's tag set as corroboration, though I did not confirm its annual values.
- **Gate:** Extend the existing shareScale work to per-share FLOW series, not just share counts: detect any year-on-year ratio between 1.8x and 5x in CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared or in shares outstanding, and truncate the record at the seam rather than restating. Label: 'The record before [year] is stated on a different share basis and is not comparable.' A wrong growth rate is worse than a shorter record.

### Where leasing commissions and ground rent sit changes 'cash from operations' between filers
- **Verdict:** EXTENDED — a comparability defect that makes any cross-filer 'OCF less capex' league table dishonest unless flagged
- **Example:** BXP charges 'Tenant leasing costs (122,618)' INSIDE operating cash flow, so its $1,245.157m OCF is already net of the commissions paid to fill space. Simon capitalises leasing costs and amortises them within depreciation ('Amortization of deferred leasing costs is a component of depreciation and amortization expense'), so its $4,136.551m OCF is not. Comparing the two on 'OCF less capex' silently penalises BXP. Separately, ground rent: Simon is 'subject to ground leases that cover all or a portion of 29 of our consolidated properties with termination dates extending through 2105'; BXP paid $13.662m of finance-lease obligations in 2025 through FINANCING activities and recorded $163.056m of finance-lease right-of-use assets in 2023 — so the principal portion of a ground rent bypasses operating cash flow entirely.
- **XBRL:** PARTIALLY STRUCTURED. BXP's leasing-cost line is inside the operating section and I confirmed us-gaap:AmortizationOfDeferredLeasingFees and us-gaap:DeferredCostsLeasingAccumulatedAmortization exist for Simon, but the classification difference itself is a presentation choice readable only from the statement, not from a tag. Finance-lease payments are visible in the financing section.
- **Gate:** Do not build a cross-filer ranking on 'operating cash flow less capital spending'. Where the figure is shown, attach the presentation note: 'This trust charges the commissions paid to fill space against operating cash flow; others capitalise them, so the two are not directly comparable.' Where a filer discloses material ground leases, note that part of its property rent is paid below the operating line.

### Depreciation and net-income tag gaps that make the REIT scorecard silently non-computable
- **Verdict:** VERIFIED — Simon does not tag the two most basic inputs the way the pipeline expects
- **Example:** SIMON does not tag us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss at all in its companyfacts — only us-gaap:ProfitLoss ($5,364.120m, whole entity) and us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic ($4,624.275m). It does not tag us-gaap:DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization either — it uses us-gaap:DepreciationAndAmortization ($1,426.423m), while the figure in its cash-flow statement is $1,554.346m. So even before any conceptual objection, three different 'depreciation' numbers exist for Simon in one filing year and the tag the pipeline reaches for first is absent. VORNADO does not tag us-gaap:InterestExpense at all (us-gaap:InterestAndDebtExpense $353.868m), so the ebitdaCoverage() check in reits.mjs cannot fire for it.
- **XBRL:** STRUCTURED but tag-dependent. The repo's depreciation concept list (fetchFundamentals.mjs line 119) does include DepreciationAndAmortization as a fallback, so Simon resolves — but to the income-statement figure, not the cash-flow figure, a $128m difference. NetIncomeLoss absence for Simon is real and I confirmed it directly.
- **Gate:** Where two depreciation tags exist with different values, print the one used and its label, never a blended figure. Where NetIncomeLoss is absent, do not silently fall back to ProfitLoss for a per-share or FFO-style calculation — the parent/entity distinction is exactly what breaks the arithmetic (see the FFO trap). Add an audit assertion that every REIT-shelf filer resolves netIncome from a PARENT-level tag or is excluded from the FFO column.

### Revenue for a REIT is not all rent
- **Verdict:** EXTENDED — affects the ffoMargin line and any rent-based ratio
- **Example:** SIMON FY2025 revenue of $6,364.505m comprises lease income $5,839.160m, management fees and other revenues $144.426m and other income $380.919m; it also holds investments in retailers (Catalyst, formerly J.C. Penney and SPARC Group) and books a $414.8m pre-tax gain on selling its remaining Authentic Brands Group interests in 2024. BXP's revenue of $3,482.279m against operating lease income of $3,236.007m — 93% rent. Realty Income's revenue of $5,749.377m includes reimbursements and interest on financing receivables. A margin computed on 'Revenues' is not a margin on rent, and the mix differs by a factor of three across the shelf.
- **XBRL:** STRUCTURED and easily fixed: us-gaap:OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome carries full annual series for BXP ($3,236.007m) and SPG ($5,839.160m), against us-gaap:Revenues for both.
- **Gate:** Add a 'rent share of revenue' line = OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome ÷ Revenues, and compute any margin-style REIT ratio on lease income rather than total revenue where the tag exists. The existing guard in ffoMargin() (returning null when the ratio exceeds 1) is a symptom of this and should be replaced by naming the denominator explicitly.


### Owner-earnings frame for a REIT

BUFFETT'S ARITHMETIC FOR A REIT, stated as an owner would: cash rent actually collected, less cash operating costs, less all interest actually incurred (including the part capitalised into buildings), less the capital spending genuinely required to hold the present rent roll — roofs, lifts, tenant improvements and the commissions paid to keep tenants in place — less the trust's share of the same inside properties it does not consolidate, and finally less the share of what remains that belongs to partners rather than to shareholders. What is left is what may be taken out. Graham's question follows immediately: is the distribution smaller than that number, and was it smaller in the bad years too.

WHICH OF OUR LINES FEED EACH TERM (every tag below empirically confirmed in companyfacts data fetched this session, exact spelling):
- Cash rent less cash costs and cash interest: us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivities. Confirmed with full 2019-2025 annual series for BXP ($1,245.157m FY2025), SPG ($4,136.551m), VNO ($1,258.385m), O ($3,994.754m), NLY ($692.911m). This is the honest starting point precisely because it already excludes straight-line rent and gains on sale.
- The non-cash rent inside GAAP revenue, if starting from earnings instead: us-gaap:StraightLineRent (BXP, SPG, VNO) and the cumulative stock us-gaap:DeferredRentReceivablesNet (BXP, VNO, O).
- Interest actually incurred: us-gaap:InterestPaidNet (labelled by the taxonomy as 'excluding capitalized interest') plus us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized (BXP, SPG, O) or us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalizedAdjustment (VNO). Expensed interest for the coverage read: us-gaap:InterestExpense (BXP, SPG) or us-gaap:InterestAndDebtExpense (VNO).
- Keeping the buildings and the tenants: us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements + us-gaap:PaymentsForTenantImprovements. Both present for BXP ($216.687m + $338.808m); capital improvements only for O ($131.800m); NEITHER for SPG (one undifferentiated us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets of $934.346m) and only development for VNO (us-gaap:PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets $144.609m).
- Building new things, which an owner separates out because it is optional: us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherProductiveAssets (BXP $683.750m — note this same family means ALL capex at Simon), us-gaap:PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets (VNO), us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireCommercialRealEstate / PaymentsToAcquireRealEstate (acquisitions).
- Gains to strip: us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets (BXP $176.732m — MISSING from the repo's concept list, which has only the '1'-suffixed variant), us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfProperties (O $177.640m), us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1 (VNO $35.291m), and NOTHING for SPG.
- What was actually taken out: us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends (BXP $643.107m, NLY $1,882.237m) or us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock (O $2,920.895m, VNO $141.277m), PLUS the partners' share via us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsMinorityInterest (SPG $439.384m) and, for BXP, the untagged 'Distributions to noncontrolling interests in property partnerships' ($83.129m) which sits in the financing section.
- Depreciation to add back, with care: us-gaap:DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization (BXP $912.088m, O $2,524.200m) or us-gaap:DepreciationAndAmortization (SPG $1,426.423m — its cash-flow figure is $1,554.346m).
- Materiality flag for the unconsolidated half of the business: us-gaap:IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments (all four equity REITs) and us-gaap:InvestmentsInAffiliatesSubsidiariesAssociatesAndJointVentures.

WHERE IT MUST STAY WITHHELD, concretely:
1. Any cross-filer maintenance-versus-development capex split, and therefore any AFFO. Simon does not disclose one; Vornado discloses only the development half. The two words do not exist in the us-gaap taxonomy — I checked every tag name in all five filers. What management calls 'recurring capex' is a number management chooses, and it appears in no filing I read this session.
2. Nareit FFO to the trust's own figure. Our formula printed $1,351.120m for Vornado against its reported $486.826m, and $1,188.888m for BXP against its reported $1,089.035m. FFO is never XBRL-tagged — it lives in MD&A, which is not detail-tagged — so it cannot be computed, only mis-approximated. Publish the arithmetic under an honest name or not at all.
3. Look-through leverage including the share of joint-venture debt. Vornado's $2,478.544m is an extension tag; BXP's $1,221,666 is untagged prose; Simon does not disclose its share of the $16,374.773m of mortgages sitting inside its unconsolidated ventures at all. Three filers, three different reasons, one answer: withhold and say why.
4. Cash interest on a look-through basis, and the interest incurred inside joint ventures.
5. Net asset value, cap rates, occupancy-adjusted value, same-store NOI — none are in the filings as computable data, and the doctrine forbids sourcing them elsewhere.
6. The tax character of the distribution (how much was return of capital). BXP tags it as a company extension (2024: 10.92%, $0.40 per share); Simon and Vornado do not disclose it in the 10-K at all. It can be quoted for a filer where we have read it, never computed for the pool.
7. For Simon specifically, the dividend itself. Its $2,792.872m payment carries only a company extension tag and no per-share fallback exists. The most important REIT question is unanswerable for the largest US mall REIT from tagged data, and the honest product move is to say exactly that.

THE ONE COMPLETE ARITHMETIC WE CAN PUBLISH, where the inputs exist: operating cash flow, less capital improvements, less tenant improvements, less every distribution including those to partners — printed as a dollar surplus or shortfall with each input named. For BXP FY2025 that is $1,245.157m − $216.687m − $338.808m − $643.107m − $83.129m = MINUS $36.574m, before a dollar of the $683.750m spent on construction, which $682.514m of building sales paid for. That sentence is defensible to the last decimal, and it is the whole of the Munger inversion in one line.


### MORTGAGE REITS — the warning

A mortgage REIT is not a pile of buildings; it is a levered bond portfolio wearing a REIT's tax status, and four of our property lines are meaningless or actively wrong for it.

WHAT IS ALREADY RIGHT, and should not be disturbed: C:/Users/ryanr/.surplus-scout/ownerscorecard-terminal/src/lib/archetype.mjs routes SIC 6500-6799 filers that report net interest income with negligible depreciation to kind 'bank', subtype 'mortgage-reit', so they never reach the FFO scorecard. I verified the tie-break holds for both filers tested: Annaly tags us-gaap:InterestIncomeExpenseNet ($1,135.500m FY2025) and has no depreciation values; AGNC tags us-gaap:InterestIncomeExpenseNet and has no depreciation tag at all. Separately, efficiencyRatio() in financials.mjs already refuses to compute when net interest income is not positive — which matters, because Annaly's net interest income was NEGATIVE $111.384m in 2023 and an ungated ratio would have printed a flattering 'lean' grade off a negative denominator.

WHAT IS ACTIVELY WRONG:
1. Return on equity. returnOnEquity() divides us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss — which is struck BEFORE preferred dividends — by us-gaap:StockholdersEquity, which INCLUDES preferred. Annaly's preferred is $1,802.480m, 11.2% of its 2025 equity of $16,090.772m, and preferred dividends were $157.931m. The line prints 12.60% where the return to common is 13.08%; in 2023 it prints −14.60% where the return to common is −18.36%. Fix: numerator us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic, denominator equity less preferred.
2. The dividend figure. CONCEPTS.dividendsPaid (fetchFundamentals.mjs line 194) prefers us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock, which for Annaly FY2025 carries $494.881m. I read the surrounding text in the filing: that number is 'Distributions paid to common stockholders after period end' — the January payment — not the year's dividend. The cash-flow line is 'Dividends paid (1,882,237)', tagged us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends. A 3.8x understatement is one tag-order away.
3. GAAP net income as a measure of anything distributable. Annaly's result is dominated by marks on mortgage securities and derivatives: −$2,163m (2019), −$891m (2020), +$2,390m (2021), +$1,725m (2022), −$1,643m (2023), +$1,002m (2024), +$2,027m (2025). It paid dividends in every one of those years.
4. Any leverage or coverage line built from property-REIT plumbing. debtToAssets() would need repo borrowings, and Annaly's repo is tagged two different ways with a $2.4bn gap — us-gaap:SecuritiesSoldUnderAgreementsToRepurchaseGross ($84,303.067m) versus us-gaap:SecuritySoldUnderAgreementToRepurchaseAfterOffsetSubjectToMasterNettingArrangement ($81,865.723m) — while the older us-gaap:SecuritiesSoldUnderAgreementsToRepurchase series stops in 2022. Annaly's own GAAP leverage of 7.2:1 (total GAAP debt $116,188.855m over total equity $16,159.911m) appears only as a text table.

WHAT IS MISSING AND MATTERS MOST — the return-of-capital reality, which no line currently shows. Common book value per share IS computable and I verified it to the penny against the filing: (us-gaap:StockholdersEquity − us-gaap:PreferredStockValue) ÷ us-gaap:CommonStockSharesOutstanding gives $20.21 (2025), $19.14 (2024) and $19.44 (2023) against Annaly's disclosed $20.21, $19.15 and $19.44. Place that record beside the declared dividend and the story tells itself without our asserting anything: book value per share fell from $31.88 at the end of 2021 to $20.21 at the end of 2025, a decline of $11.67, while dividends declared over 2022-2025 totalled $11.52 per share. Across 2019-2025 Annaly earned cumulative net income of $2,447m and paid dividends of $10,937m, while common shares outstanding roughly doubled. Annaly's own 10-K states that distributions 'could be classified as return of capital due to differences between book and tax accounting rules' and that it 'may make additional returns of capital' — that language is text-only, tagged nowhere.

GATES TO BUILD: (a) a 'Common book value per share' line for this subtype, gated to require the preferred deduction to be present and to exceed roughly 1% of equity — otherwise the tag is par value, not liquidation preference, and the figure would silently overstate (Annaly does not tag us-gaap:PreferredStockLiquidationPreferenceValue at all; AGNC does); (b) that line shown against dividends declared per share, never against earnings; (c) a fixed caption wherever a mortgage REIT's distribution appears: 'A mortgage REIT's distribution is not an earnings figure. Read it against the change in book value per share.'; (d) refuse, permanently, to reproduce 'earnings available for distribution', 'economic leverage' or 'economic book value' — Annaly's EAD of $2.92 per share in 2025 is management-defined, non-GAAP and untagged, and in 2023 it read $1,554m while GAAP net income read −$1,643m; (e) note that src/data/reit-subsectors.json contains no mortgage REITs and no mortgage subsector, so classification rests entirely on the archetype tie-break — a filer that reports interest income and expense separately without tagging us-gaap:InterestIncomeExpenseNet would fall through to kind 'reit' and be graded on property lines.
