- ABSENT: FFO / AFFO — no tag, us-gaap or extension.; Same-store or same-property NOI — no tag. AMT's property-segment operating profit is dimensioned by segment and therefore absent from companyfacts, which returns undimensioned facts only.; Weighted-average interest rate — no annual tag.; Debt maturity schedule — absent from FY2023 forward.; Maintenance/recurring capex separable from construction — no tag; one combined capex line.; Tenant improvements / leasing commissions — us-gaap:PaymentsForLeasingCostsCommissionsAndTenantImprovements is entirely absent for AMT.; Capitalised interest — absent from FY2017 forward.; Preferred equity — none outstanding; us-gaap:PreferredStockValue appears only at FY2008, 2009, 2013, 2014 and us-gaap:PreferredStockSharesOutstanding is 0 through FY2013. The 2014-2018 mandatory convertibles show only via us-gaap:DividendsPreferredStockCash (FY2012-2018, FY2018 18,900,000).
- HAZARDS: FFO IS NOT TAGGED. No FundsFromOperations/AFFO element in AMT's companyfacts and none in amt-20251231.xsd (grepped for Funds, FFO, AdjustedFunds, NetOperatingIncome, SameStore, SameProperty, Maintenance, Recurring — all hits false positives). AMT reports AFFO in its earnings release only.; REVENUE MIS-PICK RISK. scripts/fetchFundamentals.mjs lists revenue as [RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax, Revenues, ...] in that order. For AMT the first tag is SERVICES REVENUE ONLY — 935,900,000 in FY2025 against total Revenues of 10,644,600,000. If the extractor takes the first tag with a value, AMT's top line reads 9% of the truth, and every margin, FFO-margin and capex-intensity built on it is nonsense. Verify which AMT revenue the pipeline currently holds before publishing anything on this name.; NET INCOME MIS-PICK RISK. The repo's netIncome list is [NetIncomeLoss, NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic, ProfitLoss]. NetIncomeLoss is dead for AMT after FY2020. Whether AMT's FY2021-2025 net income resolves at all depends on whether collectAnnual falls through per YEAR or per TAG; a per-tag fallback that picks the first tag with ANY data would leave FY2021-2025 null.; DIVIDEND-PER-SHARE PERIOD-TYPE MIXING. us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareCashPaid holds both annual durations (6.80 for FY2025) and bare instants that are single quarterly declarations (1.62 at 2025-02-03). Same tag, same unit, same 10-K accession. A generic extractor keyed on 'end date within the fiscal year' will publish 1.62 as the annual dividend — a 4x understatement that would make coverage look wonderful.; RESTATEMENT ACROSS VINTAGES. FY2023 D&A was 3,086,500,000 as originally filed and 2,928,500,000 as re-presented after the ATC India disposal was moved to discontinued operations. Any continuity tripwire that compares a stored FY2023 against a refetched FY2023 will fire, and any growth rate spanning FY2023-FY2024 mixes two different perimeters.; DEBT TOTAL IS EASY TO GET WRONG. us-gaap:LongTermDebtAndCapitalLeaseObligations reads as a total from its name but is NONCURRENT ONLY (33,832,500,000 at FY2025). The total is ...IncludingCurrentMaturities (37,220,300,000). Adding LongTermDebtCurrent to the first tag happens to reproduce the total; adding it to the second double-counts by 3,387,800,000.; NO WEIGHTED-AVERAGE INTEREST RATE. AMT has no undimensioned annual weighted-average-rate tag of any kind. us-gaap:DebtInstrumentInterestRateStatedPercentage exists only as a single 10-Q fact. The cost of AMT's debt is not extractable.; THREE-YEAR HOLE in us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivities (FY2013-FY2015).; PRECISION DRIFT: AMT reports in $0.1m throughout, and some concepts (Depreciation, AmortizationOfIntangibleAssets in FY2023) are tagged at $100m precision from narrative notes. Values ending in eight zeros are disclosure text, not statement lines.

### EQIX (CIK 0001101239, FYE 12)

- `us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss` — Net income attributable to parent · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 969,000,000; FY2024 815,000,000; FY2025 1,350,000,000 · Continuous. But FY2023 was filed as 969,178,000 in accn 0001628280-24-005350 and re-presented as 969,000,000 in accn 0001628280-25-005126 — see the rounding seam in hazards. us-gaap:ProfitLoss tracks within 2,000,000.
- `us-gaap:DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization` — Total D&A (cash-flow add-back) · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023 1,845,000,000; FY2024 2,009,000,000; FY2025 2,050,000,000 · EQIX also files us-gaap:DepreciationAmortizationAndAccretionNet in the SAME years with slightly different values (FY2023 1,844,000,000; FY2024 2,011,000,000; FY2025 2,066,000,000). Two D&A tags, both continuous, disagreeing by 1-16m. Pick one and say which.
- `us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense` — Real-estate-only depreciation (Schedule III) · USD · FY2013-2025 · FY2023 1,317,000,000; FY2024 1,413,000,000; FY2025 1,464,000,000 · About 71% of total D&A. The remainder is data-centre equipment and intangibles — the part that genuinely wears out. Adding total D&A back for a colocation REIT is the least defensible FFO on this tier.
- `us-gaap:Depreciation` — Property depreciation (statement note) — dropped in FY2025 · USD · FY2008-2024 · FY2022 1,532,000,000; FY2023 1,637,000,000; FY2024 1,801,000,000 · Present FY2008-FY2024, ABSENT at 2025-12-31. A fresh tag-generation change in the most recent 10-K.
- `us-gaap:AmortizationOfIntangibleAssets` — Intangible amortisation · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 208,000,000; FY2024 208,000,000; FY2025 200,000,000 · Small and stable.
- `us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1` — Gains on asset disposals · USD · FY2014-2025 · FY2023 5,000,000; FY2024 18,000,000; FY2025 1,000,000 · Immaterial — EQIX does not recycle buildings, it builds and holds. This is the ONE ticker on the tier where the repo's gainOnSaleRealEstate list actually resolves (via this tag), and it happens not to matter. Legacy us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfPropertyPlantEquipment FY2014-2017 only.
- `us-gaap:AssetImpairmentCharges` — Impairments · USD · FY2014-2025 · FY2023 0; FY2024 233,000,000; FY2025 68,000,000 · FY2024's 233,000,000 decomposes into us-gaap:ImpairmentOfLongLivedAssetsHeldForUse 166,000,000 and us-gaap:ImpairmentOfIntangibleAssetsExcludingGoodwill 29,000,000, both of which are absent at FY2025 — summing component impairment tags across years double-counts in some and undercounts in others.
- `us-gaap:RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` — Total revenue · USD · FY2019-2025 · FY2023 8,188,000,000; FY2024 8,748,000,000; FY2025 9,217,000,000 · For EQIX this ASC 606 tag IS the total (unlike AMT). Predecessor us-gaap:Revenues FY2008-FY2020; the two overlap FY2019-2020 so the seam is checkable. EQIX's revenue is a service contract, not a lease — which is why there is no straight-line rent line at all.
- `us-gaap:CostOfRevenue` — Cost of revenue · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 4,228,000,000; FY2024 4,467,000,000; FY2025 4,508,000,000 · Continuous. Extension eqix:SegmentCostOfRevenue also exists in the FY2025 schema (not in companyfacts).
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherPropertyPlantAndEquipment` — All capital expenditure — one line · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 2,781,000,000; FY2024 3,066,000,000; FY2025 4,311,000,000 · Cash-flow caption 'Purchases of other property, plant and equipment'. us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets (FY2010-2025) carries the IDENTICAL values in every overlap year — the same line double-tagged. Summing both doubles EQIX's capex.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireRealEstate` — Real estate acquisitions (separate from capex) · USD · FY2012-2025 · FY2023 384,000,000; FY2024 337,000,000; FY2025 994,000,000 · A separate investing line from the capex line above; total property spend FY2025 = 5,305,000,000 against operating cash flow of 3,911,000,000 and dividends of 1,856,000,000.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebt` — Total debt — TAG DROPPED IN FY2025 · USD · FY2011-2024 · FY2022 12,880,507,000; FY2023 13,840,646,000; FY2024 15,334,000,000 · ABSENT at 2025-12-31. This is a live break in the most recent filing: any extractor keyed on LongTermDebt returns null for EQIX FY2025 and the leverage cell goes blank or falls back to something narrower.
- `us-gaap:SeniorLongTermNotes` — Senior notes — the surviving debt tag · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2023 12,062,000,000; FY2024 13,363,000,000; FY2025 16,910,000,000 · Noncurrent senior notes only. FY2025 companions at 2025-12-31: us-gaap:SeniorNotesCurrent 1,299,000,000 (= us-gaap:LongTermDebtCurrent, double-tagged), us-gaap:DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount 19,062,000,000, us-gaap:FinanceLeaseLiability 2,355,000,000. Reconstructing FY2025 total debt requires choosing among these; DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount is itself sparse (FY2014-2021, then nothing until FY2025).
- `us-gaap:FinanceLeaseLiability` — Finance lease liability · USD · FY2019-2025 · FY2023 2,261,141,000; FY2024 2,275,000,000; FY2025 2,355,000,000 · Material for EQIX — it leases many of its buildings. Excluded from LongTermDebt/SeniorLongTermNotes, so a debt figure that omits it understates leverage by ~2.4bn.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebtMaturitiesRepaymentsOfPrincipalInNextTwelveMonths` — Debt maturities · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023 1,007,704,000; FY2024 1,205,000,000; FY2025 1,317,000,000 · Present FY2011 then FY2015-2025 (hole FY2012-2014). Full ladder years two through five and after-five all present at FY2025: 1,764,000,000 / 1,433,000,000 / 2,211,000,000 / 2,739,000,000 / 9,598,000,000. EQIX is the only one of the three with a complete current maturity ladder.
- `us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized` — Capitalised interest — two contradictory tags · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2023 26,000,000; FY2024 36,000,000; FY2025 79,000,000 · us-gaap:InterestPaidCapitalized (FY2017-2025) carries FY2023 445,000,000 / FY2024 486,000,000 / FY2025 448,000,000 in the same filings — an order of magnitude apart from the tag above. Two capitalised-interest-named elements that cannot both mean what their names say. Do not use either without reading the note.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends` — Dividends paid (cash) · USD · FY2014-2025 · FY2023 1,375,000,000; FY2024 1,643,000,000; FY2025 1,856,000,000 · us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock is ABSENT for EQIX, so a list that leads with it must fall through. us-gaap:DividendsCommonStock (declared, FY2020-2025) gives FY2025 1,835,000,000 — a 21,000,000 gap to cash paid.
- `us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared` — Dividends per share · USD/shares · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 14.49; FY2024 17.04; FY2025 18.76 · us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareCashPaid carries the identical annual values. BOTH tags also carry quarterly INSTANT facts across 2015-2017 (e.g. 2015-02-19, 2015-05-07, 2016-03-23) mixed into the same unit — the same period-type trap as AMT, here confined to the older years.
- `us-gaap:Assets` — Total assets and operating cash flow · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023 32,651,000,000; FY2024 35,085,000,000; FY2025 40,141,000,000 · us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivities FY2008-2025 unbroken: FY2023 3,217,000,000; FY2024 3,249,000,000; FY2025 3,911,000,000.
- `us-gaap:CumulativeDividends` — Cumulative dividends (a trap) · USD · FY2014-2025 · FY2023 8,695,000,000; FY2024 10,342,000,000; FY2025 12,202,000,000 · This is a since-inception cumulative balance, not an annual flow. It sits in the same file with a dividend-shaped name and a plausible magnitude. A fuzzy tag matcher would happily publish 12,202,000,000 as EQIX's FY2025 dividend.
- ABSENT: FFO / AFFO / recurring capex — no tag, us-gaap or extension.; Straight-line rent — us-gaap:StraightLineRent is entirely ABSENT for EQIX. So are us-gaap:OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome and us-gaap:OperatingLeasesIncomeStatementLeaseRevenue. EQIX's revenue is ASC 606 service revenue, so there is no straight-line rent adjustment to find. The only rent-deferral trace is us-gaap:DeferredRentCreditCurrent/Noncurrent, FY2010-FY2018 only.; Above/below-market lease amortisation — no tag.; Same-store or same-property NOI — no tag. EQIX's segment disclosure is by geographic region and is dimensioned, hence absent from companyfacts.; Maintenance or recurring capex separable from expansion — no tag. The FY2025 investing section has exactly two property lines: 'Real estate acquisitions' and 'Purchases of other property, plant and equipment'.; Tenant improvements / leasing commissions — us-gaap:PaymentsForLeasingCostsCommissionsAndTenantImprovements absent entirely.; Weighted-average interest rate — no tag. us-gaap:DebtInstrumentInterestRateEffectivePercentage FY2014-2018 only.; Preferred equity — none. us-gaap:PreferredStockSharesOutstanding is 0 for FY2009-FY2025.
- HAZARDS: FFO IS NOT TAGGED. No FundsFromOperations/AFFO element in EQIX's companyfacts and none in eqix-20251231.xsd (grepped for Funds, FFO, AdjustedFunds, NetOperatingIncome, SameStore, SameProperty, Maintenance — the only Recurring hits are eqix:PercentageOfRecurringRevenue and two revenue-type members, which concern revenue mix, not capex). EQIX reports AFFO and recurring capex in its quarterly supplemental only.; ROUNDING SEAM, FY2024 10-K. EQIX re-presented its whole statement set from exact dollars to $-million rounding. Same fiscal year, two filed values: FY2023 net income 969,178,000 -> 969,000,000; FY2023 D&A 1,845,138,000 -> 1,845,000,000; FY2023 revenue 8,188,136,000 -> 8,188,000,000; FY2022 revenue 7,263,105,000 -> 7,263,000,000. Every continuity tripwire keyed on exact equality across vintages will fire on EQIX FY2022-FY2023 and the fires are all false.; us-gaap:LongTermDebt IS GONE AT FY2025. Present FY2011-FY2024 (15,334,000,000 at FY2024), absent at 2025-12-31. This will surface as a blank or a stale leverage figure the first time the pipeline refetches EQIX after 2026-02-11.; CAPEX DOUBLE-TAGGING. us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherPropertyPlantAndEquipment and us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets carry identical values for FY2010-FY2025. The repo's capex list contains PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets; if any variant sums rather than falls through, EQIX's capex doubles. Separately, us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquirePropertyPlantAndEquipment exists for EQIX at FY2010 ONLY (14,861,000) — a single stranded fact that a first-tag-wins picker would take for that year.; THE FFO CONCEPT ITSELF IS WRONG FOR EQIX, not just the tags. A data centre's depreciation is largely on generators, chillers, UPS plant and tenant fit-out with real economic lives of ten to twenty years, not a warehouse shell. FY2025 total D&A 2,050,000,000 of which only 1,464,000,000 is real estate. Adding all of it back and calling the result cash the owner can take is the exact error the desk's frame warns against. If the property column prints an FFO for EQIX it should say which depreciation it added back.; TWO D&A TAGS AND TWO CAPITALISED-INTEREST TAGS coexist in the same filings with different values. Whichever the pipeline picks, the basis string must name the tag.; us-gaap:CumulativeDividends is a since-inception balance sitting beside the annual dividend flows.

> THE DESK'S CENTRAL QUESTION, ANSWERED EMPIRICALLY: FFO is not tagged for any of PLD, AMT or EQIX — not undimensioned, not dimension-locked, not as a company extension. I confirmed this two ways. (1) companyfacts for all three contains no tag matching FundsFromOperation/FFO/AFFO/AdjustedFunds (the only regex hits were substring accidents: WriteOffOfDeferredDebtIssuanceCost, UndistributedEarningsOfForeignSubsidiaries, AccountsReceivableAllowanceForDoubtfulAccountsImpactOfForeignCurrencyExchange). (2) I downloaded each issuer's FY2025 extension schema — pld-20251231.xsd, amt-20251231.xsd, eqix-20251231.xsd — and grepped every declared element name for Funds, FFO, AdjustedFunds, NetOperatingIncome, SameStore, SameProperty, Maintenance, Recurring, Cash Available. Zero real hits in all three. FFO is a non-GAAP measure that lives in the earnings release (8-K Ex-99), which carries no XBRL. The desk cannot extract FFO for this tier. It can only construct one, and must then say so on the face of the number.

> COMPANYFACTS RETURNS ONLY STANDARD TAXONOMIES. The `facts` object for these three contains exactly dei, invest, srt, us-gaap (PLD) and dei, srt, us-gaap, ffd (AMT, EQIX). Company extension namespaces (pld:, amt:, eqix:) NEVER appear. I proved the consequence by value-probe: PLD's FY2025 rental revenue 8,158,904,000, its two gains-on-disposition lines 257,731,000 and 685,831,000, its operating-income-before-gains 3,414,302,000, its property improvements 327,355,000 and its straight-line-rent add-back 691,090,000 all appear in the filing (verified verbatim in R4.htm and R8.htm of accession 0001193125-26-051453) and NONE of them returns a single hit anywhere in PLD's companyfacts. Any REIT extractor built on companyfacts alone is structurally blind to whatever a REIT chose to extend — and REITs extend precisely the lines this desk cares about.

> COMPANYFACTS ALSO RETURNS ONLY UNDIMENSIONED FACTS. Segment NOI, property-type NOI, per-instrument debt terms and the Inc-versus-LP columns are all dimension-qualified and therefore invisible. This is why 'same-store NOI: text only?' has the answer 'not even text — dimensioned or extension, either way unreachable from this API.'

> THE fy AND fp FIELDS IN COMPANYFACTS ARE THE FILING'S FISCAL PERIOD, NOT THE FACT'S. A FY2023 fact re-reported in the FY2025 10-K carries fy:2025. Keying a year series on `fy` silently maps three different periods onto one year. Key on start/end and dedupe on (start,end) keeping the latest `filed`. I hit this on my own first pass and it produced duplicate 'FY2025' rows with FY2024 values.

> PAIRED OPERATING-PARTNERSHIP FILERS: of this tier, only Prologis. Prologis, L.P. (CIK 0001045610) co-files the identical accession — 0001193125-26-051453 appears as a 10-K in both entities' submissions feeds — and the combined instance separates the two with srt:ConsolidatedEntitiesAxis. The LP's companyfacts is a near-empty shell holding only `ffd` filing-fee facts from 424B2 prospectuses, no financial statements at all. American Tower and Equinix each show a single registrant on the FY2025 cover page (R1.htm) and have no OP co-filer.

> THE REPO'S ffo COLUMN IS NOT ADEQUATE AS WRITTEN. src/lib/groupingColumns.mjs describes it as 'net income + depreciation − property gains' and src/lib/reits.mjs implements ffo(L) = L.netIncome + L.depreciation − (L.gainOnSaleRealEstate || 0). Three defects, all verified on this tier: (a) `depreciation` resolves to a TOTAL D&A tag, not real-estate depreciation — for PLD that is 2,626,028,000 instead of 2,011,907,000, for EQIX 2,050,000,000 instead of 1,464,000,000, for AMT 2,041,600,000 instead of 974,000,000; NAREIT adds back real-estate depreciation only, and the us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense tag that gives it is present and continuous for ALL THREE. (b) the gainOnSaleRealEstate tag list [GainLossOnSaleOfPropertiesNetOfApplicableIncomeTaxes, GainLossOnDispositionOfRealEstate, GainsLossesOnSalesOfInvestmentRealEstate, GainLossOnSaleOfProperties, GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets1] resolves to nothing for PLD after FY2018 and to nothing ever for AMT, and the `|| 0` turns the miss into a silent zero rather than a withheld figure — for PLD FY2025 that hides 943,562,000 of gains. (c) it says nothing about the cost of keeping the buildings competitive. Recommendation for the pipeline, in doctrine order: withhold the FFO cell entirely where the real-estate depreciation tag or the gains treatment is not resolvable, rather than print a number; where it is resolvable, name the tags in the basis string.

> THE ONE PLACE THE DATA IS BETTER THAN EXPECTED: us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense is present, annual and continuous for all three (PLD FY2011-2025, AMT FY2012-2025, EQIX FY2013-2025). That single tag is the honest FFO add-back and it is already in companyfacts. It is the highest-value change available to the property column.

> THE AFFO SIDE SPLITS THE TIER CLEANLY. PLD gives us us-gaap:PaymentsForLeasingCostsCommissionsAndTenantImprovements continuously FY2009-2025 (FY2025 562,197,000) — genuine re-leasing cost, extractable. AMT and EQIX do not tag it at all and report one undifferentiated capex line each, so maintenance capex is NOT separable for either. Any 'cash after keeping the buildings competitive' column must therefore be withheld for AMT and EQIX and can only be partial for PLD (its property-improvements line is extension-only). That asymmetry is itself the finding: the two names with the least separable maintenance spend are the two whose depreciation add-back is least defensible.

> STRAIGHT-LINE RENT, THE THREE POSTURES. AMT tags it under us-gaap:StraightLineRent continuously FY2012-FY2025 and it has collapsed from 472,000,000 to 101,000,000 in two years. PLD tagged it under us-gaap:StraightLineRent only through FY2014 and has since buried it inside the extension pld:StraightLineRentsAndAmortizationOfAboveAndBelowMarketLeases — 691,090,000 in FY2025, 8.5% of rental revenue, invisible to the API. EQIX has no straight-line rent line at all because its revenue is an ASC 606 service contract, not a lease. So the single most Munger-relevant REIT accounting item is fully extractable for one name, hidden for another, and inapplicable to the third.

> RESTATEMENT AND PRECISION SEAMS TO EXPECT ON THIS TIER: AMT re-presented FY2023 after moving ATC India to discontinued operations (D&A 3,086,500,000 -> 2,928,500,000); EQIX re-presented FY2022-FY2023 from exact dollars to $-million rounding in its FY2024 10-K (net income 969,178,000 -> 969,000,000). Both will fire exact-equality continuity tripwires, and neither is an error.

### AVB (CIK 0000915912, FYE December (dei fiscalYearEnd 1231; every 10-K period ends 12-31))

- `us-gaap:ProfitLoss` — Net income, consolidated (income-statement 'Net income') · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 928,438,000 | FY2024 1,082,175,000 | FY2025 1,056,599,000 · Unbroken 19-year annual series and the only continuous bottom line. Confirmed on the face of the FY2025 income statement (R5) and the cash-flow statement (R8).
- `us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic` — Net income attributable to common stockholders · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 928,825,000 | FY2024 1,081,994,000 | FY2025 1,051,301,000 · This is the actual bottom line of AVB's income statement. AVB has no preferred outstanding, so it differs from ProfitLoss only by the noncontrolling-interest slice.
- `us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss` — Net income attributable to parent (the tag most extractors reach for) · USD · FY2007-2024 · FY2018 974,525,000 | FY2023 928,825,000 | FY2024 1,081,994,000 · DISCONTINUOUS. Present FY2007-FY2018, then absent for FY2019 through FY2022, reappearing only for FY2023 and FY2024 as facts filed in the FY2025 10-K (2026-02-27), and with NO FY2025 value at all. It never appears on the face of the income statement. Any pipeline keyed on NetIncomeLoss shows AVB a four-year hole and then a blank latest year.
- `us-gaap:Depreciation` — Depreciation (income-statement 'Depreciation expense') · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023 816,965,000 | FY2024 846,853,000 | FY2025 913,376,000 · AVB is a single-property-type apartment trust, so this line is real-estate depreciation in substance. Confirmed on the face of R5 and as the cash-flow add-back in R8.
- `us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense` — Real-estate depreciation, Schedule III roll-forward · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023 781,313,000 | FY2024 846,853,000 | FY2025 913,376,000 · Equal to us-gaap:Depreciation in FY2024 and FY2025 but NOT in FY2023 (781,313,000 vs 816,965,000). The two tags agree in some years and not others, so they are not interchangeable across the record.
- `us-gaap:CostOfServicesDepreciation` — Depreciation, pre-2011 tag generation · USD · FY2007-2012 · FY2011 239,060,000 as later restated (246,666,000 as originally filed) | FY2012 256,026,000 · Tag-generation change: depreciation moves from CostOfServicesDepreciation to us-gaap:Depreciation across FY2011-FY2012, with a two-year overlap where both are tagged.
- `us-gaap:GainsLossesOnSalesOfInvestmentRealEstate` — Gain on sale of communities (income statement) · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023 287,424,000 | FY2024 363,300,000 | FY2025 335,713,000 · The income-statement line 'Gain on sale of communities, net' (R5).
- `us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfProperties` — Gain on sale of real estate (cash-flow add-back) · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 287,987,000 | FY2024 364,159,000 | FY2025 339,954,000 · The cash-flow reversal 'Gain on sale of real estate assets, net' (R8). Runs 4-5 million higher than the income-statement tag because it folds in other real-estate activity. TWO property-gain tags coexist undimensioned for the same year: subtracting both from an FFO build would double-count roughly 336 million in FY2025.
- `us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfOtherAssets` — Gain on other real estate activity · USD · FY2012-2025 · FY2023 174,000 | FY2024 753,000 | FY2025 4,131,000 · Separate income-statement line 'Other real estate activity'.
- `us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate` — Impairment (reported as 'Casualty and impairment loss') · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 9,118,000 | FY2024 2,935,000 | FY2025 1,276,000 · AVB tags a BLENDED casualty-plus-impairment line with the impairment element; it is not a pure impairment figure. The cash-flow statement uses a different, custom element avb:CasualtyGainAndImpairmentLossNetOperatingActivities with different values (FY2023 4,622,000 | FY2024 1,415,000 | FY2025 1,276,000).
- `us-gaap:Revenues` — Total revenue · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 2,767,909,000 | FY2024 2,913,757,000 | FY2025 3,040,725,000 · Continuous. Confirmed as 'Total revenue' on R5.
- `us-gaap:OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome` — Rental and other income · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 2,760,187,000 | FY2024 2,906,676,000 | FY2025 3,033,683,000 · The rent line proper, 99.8% of revenue. Begins FY2017 with the lease-standard change.
- `us-gaap:OperatingLeasesIncomeStatementMinimumLeaseRevenue` — Rental income, pre-842 tag generation · USD · FY2012-2018 · FY2016 2,039,656,000 | FY2017 2,154,481,000 | FY2018 2,280,963,000 · Tag-generation change at the lease-standard boundary, with a FY2017-FY2018 overlap where both rent tags carry values.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements` — Capital expenditure on existing real estate (the maintenance proxy) · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 178,312,000 | FY2024 193,348,000 | FY2025 261,769,000 · Confirmed from R8 as the cash-flow line 'Capital expenditures - existing real estate assets'. The closest thing to recurring capex AVB tags, but it BLENDS true maintenance with revenue-enhancing redevelopment and AVB does not split them in the 10-K. Unbroken 19-year series, the best capex record of the three.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets` — Development and redevelopment spend · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 901,847,000 | FY2024 951,101,000 | FY2025 1,209,454,000 · Cleanly separable from the existing-asset line. Includes land acquisitions and deferred development costs per the R8 label.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherRealEstate` — Acquisitions of real estate · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023 215,889,000 | FY2024 464,419,000 | FY2025 682,163,000 · Third and separate investing line, so growth spend is fully distinguishable from the existing-asset line.
- `us-gaap:OperatingIncomeLoss` — Property net operating income (NOT GAAP operating income) · USD · FY2020-2025 · FY2023 1,790,786,000 | FY2024 1,910,080,000 | FY2025 2,020,949,000 · CRITICAL. AVB's income statement has NO operating-income line. AVB uses us-gaap:OperatingIncomeLoss, undimensioned, to carry TOTAL PROPERTY NOI in the segment note (confirmed on R55, the NOI-to-net-income reconciliation, and R57). NOI is struck BEFORE depreciation, G&A and interest. It is also restated every year: FY2020 was 1,508,184,000 in the FY2021 10-K and 1,472,421,000 in the FY2022 10-K; FY2023 was 1,886,748,000, then 1,856,443,000, then 1,790,786,000 across three successive filings.
- `us-gaap:OperatingExpenses under axis us-gaap:StatementOperatingActivitiesSegmentAxis with member avb:SameStoreMember` — Same-store operating expenses (dimension-locked) · USD · FY2023-2025 · FY2023 (759,084,000) | FY2024 (824,028,000) | FY2025 (856,617,000) · Read off R57 of the FY2025 10-K. Same-store revenue uses the custom element avb:RevenueExcludingManagementDevelopmentAndOtherFeesAndOtherAncillaryItems (FY2025 same-store 2,739,511,000). ALL same-store facts are dimension-locked and therefore absent from companyfacts; the counterpart member is avb:NonSameStoreOperatingExpenseMember. AVB does not tag a single same-store NOI number; you would have to subtract.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebt` — Total debt, net · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2023 7,981,822,000 | FY2024 8,077,249,000 | FY2025 9,328,552,000 · Instant fact. Ties out: UnsecuredDebt 7,879,380,000 + SecuredDebt 709,564,000 + CommercialPaper 740,000,000 = 9,328,944,000, and DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount 9,388,152,000 less DebtInstrumentUnamortizedDiscountPremiumAndDebtIssuanceCostsNet 59,600,000 = 9,328,552,000 exactly.
- `us-gaap:UnsecuredDebt` — Unsecured debt · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 7,256,152,000 | FY2024 7,358,784,000 | FY2025 7,879,380,000 · Instant.
- `us-gaap:SecuredDebt` — Secured debt · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 725,670,000 | FY2024 718,465,000 | FY2025 709,564,000 · Instant.
- `us-gaap:CommercialPaper` — Commercial paper outstanding · USD · FY2021-2025 · FY2023 0 | FY2024 0 | FY2025 740,000,000 · Instant. Zero for two years then 740 million: a debt total built by summing components would have been right by accident before FY2025 and wrong after, if this tag were omitted.
- `us-gaap:DebtWeightedAverageInterestRate` — Weighted-average interest rate on debt · pure · FY2021-2025 · FY2023 0.035 | FY2024 0.035 | FY2025 0.037 · Instant, undimensioned, five years only. The cleanest capital-stack disclosure AVB tags.
- `us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized` — Capitalised interest · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 47,133,000 | FY2024 43,185,000 | FY2025 50,115,000 · Material: 19% of the FY2025 interest expense of 259,181,000 is capitalised into development rather than charged. Unbroken 18-year series.
- `us-gaap:InterestExpense` — Interest expense, net · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023 205,992,000 | FY2024 226,589,000 | FY2025 259,181,000 · Presented net of capitalised interest and sits inside 'Total expenses' on the income statement.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividends` — Common dividends paid (cash) · USD · FY2018-2025 · FY2023 922,657,000 | FY2024 961,914,000 | FY2025 992,333,000 · Confirmed as the R8 financing line 'Dividends paid'. Replaced us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends (FY2007-FY2019), with a FY2018-FY2019 overlap in which BOTH tags carry the same value: summing dividend-ish tags double-counts those two years.
- `us-gaap:DividendsCommonStock` — Common dividends declared (dollars) · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023 935,305,000 | FY2024 969,345,000 | FY2025 993,683,000 · Declared basis, consistently about 1 million above the cash-paid figure.
- `us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared` — Dividends declared per common share · USD/shares · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 6.60 | FY2024 6.80 | FY2025 7.00 · Duration facts, annual, unbroken 19 years, and each equals the quarterly rate times four, so it is trustworthy without adjustment.
- `us-gaap:PreferredStockValue` — Preferred equity · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023 0 | FY2024 0 | FY2025 0 · Zero throughout; us-gaap:PreferredStockSharesOutstanding is likewise 0 for FY2010-FY2025. No preferred in the stack, which makes AVB's FFO-to-common build the simplest of the three.
- `us-gaap:Assets` — Total assets · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2024 21,000,737,000 | FY2025 22,192,137,000 · Instant, continuous.
- ABSENT: FFO: NOT TAGGED AT ALL. No element containing FundsFromOperations, FFO, CoreFFO or AdjustedFunds appears anywhere in AVB's FY2025 10-K. I inventoried all 1,246 elements used in that filing via MetaLinks.json (avb-20251231.htm) - standard, dimensioned and custom-extension alike - and there is no FFO element. Same result for the FY2019 10-K (1,103 elements). FFO is therefore not merely dimension-locked; it is outside the tagged 10-K entirely. It lives in the earnings release and supplemental, which carry no financial-statement XBRL.; AFFO / core FFO / normalised FFO: same, none.; Straight-line rent: no element containing StraightLine or DeferredRent anywhere in the filing. This is structural, not an omission - apartment leases run about twelve months, so there is nothing to straight-line.; Above/below-market lease amortisation: no element containing AboveMarket, BelowMarket, AcquiredLease or LeaseIntangible. Again structural for residential.; Debt maturity ladder: NO us-gaap:LongTermDebtMaturitiesRepaymentsOfPrincipal* bucket of any year appears on an annual 10-K frame, in any year, in any tag generation. AVB's maturity schedule is not machine-readable from companyfacts.; Recurring vs redevelopment capex split: not tagged; only the single blended existing-real-estate line exists.; Same-store NOI as a single number: not tagged (only revenue and expense components, dimension-locked).; Occupancy: only avb:AttainmentPercentageOfPhysicalOccupancy (90.00%), which is a same-store-qualification threshold, not actual occupancy.
- HAZARDS: us-gaap:OperatingIncomeLoss means PROPERTY NOI for AVB, not operating income. An EBITDA-style coverage of (operating income + depreciation) / interest computes (2,020,949 + 913,376) / 259,181 = 11.3x for FY2025, because NOI is already before depreciation, G&A and interest - the add-back double-counts. The repo's ebitdaCoverage in src/lib/reits.mjs uses exactly this construction and has no guard for a REIT that maps NOI onto the operating-income tag.; us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss has no FY2025 value and no FY2019-FY2022 values. If the pipeline's netIncome resolves to that tag, AVB's FFO column goes blank in the latest year and has a four-year hole mid-record.; us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAttributableToNonredeemableNoncontrollingInterest carries 1,056,599,000 for FY2025 - which is TOTAL net income, identical to ProfitLoss, not the noncontrolling slice. The real noncontrolling figure is 5,298,000, tagged us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAttributableToNoncontrollingInterest. Any 'parent income = ProfitLoss minus nonredeemable NCI' arithmetic returns roughly zero for AVB FY2025. Single undimensioned fact, filed 2026-02-27, frame CY2025.; Two property-gain tags coexist undimensioned for every recent year (335,713,000 and 339,954,000 in FY2025). A greedy match subtracts both and understates FFO by about 336 million, roughly 20%.; The NOI series is restated every year. FY2023 has been filed three times at three different values, so anything cached from an older filing disagrees with the current one.; The dividend cash tag changes generation mid-record (PaymentsOfDividends to PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividends) with a two-year overlap where both are populated.; us-gaap:Depreciation and the Schedule III depreciation tag agree in FY2024-FY2025 but differ by 35,652,000 in FY2023; they cannot be treated as one series.

### PSA (CIK 0001393311, FYE December (dei fiscalYearEnd 1231))

- `us-gaap:ProfitLoss` — Net income, consolidated · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 2,160,120,000 | FY2024 2,084,410,000 | FY2025 1,797,032,000 · Confirmed on the face of R5 as 'Net income'.
- `us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss` — Net income allocable to Public Storage shareholders (BEFORE preferred) · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 2,148,327,000 | FY2024 2,072,011,000 | FY2025 1,784,348,000 · Unbroken 19 years. IMPORTANT: for PSA this is struck BEFORE the 194,703,000 of annual preferred dividends. It is not income to the common.
- `us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic` — Net income allocable to common shareholders · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023 1,948,741,000 | FY2024 1,872,685,000 | FY2025 1,585,585,000 · The true common line, after preferred dividends and after an allocation to restricted share units and unvested LTIP units (custom element psa:AllocationOfNetIncomeToRestrictedShareUnitsAndUnvestedLTIPUnits).
- `us-gaap:DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization` — Depreciation and amortisation (income statement and cash flow) · USD · FY2014-2025 · FY2023 970,056,000 | FY2024 1,129,766,000 | FY2025 1,151,840,000 · The line on the face of both R5 and R9, and the correct add-back for an FFO build.
- `us-gaap:Depreciation` — Depreciation (narrower) · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2023 881,255,000 | FY2024 1,002,212,000 | FY2025 1,046,039,000 · A SECOND, smaller D&A tag running concurrently. The gap to DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization (105,801,000 in FY2025) tracks us-gaap:AmortizationOfIntangibleAssets (94,000,000) closely, consistent with this being real-property depreciation only - but I did NOT confirm that scope from the face of a statement, so I state the arithmetic and not the label.
- `us-gaap:DepreciationAndAmortization` — Depreciation and amortisation, prior generation · USD · FY2007-2021 · FY2019 512,918,000 | FY2020 553,257,000 | FY2021 713,428,000 · THREE overlapping D&A tags are simultaneously populated for FY2014-FY2021 with different values. A first-match extractor gets a different D&A depending on iteration order.
- `us-gaap:AmortizationOfIntangibleAssets` — Amortisation of intangible assets · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023 82,700,000 | FY2024 117,600,000 | FY2025 94,000,000 · Mostly in-place tenant intangibles from acquisitions.
- `us-gaap:GainsLossesOnSalesOfInvestmentRealEstate` — Gain on sale of real estate · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023 17,178,000 | FY2024 1,537,000 | FY2025 1,113,000 · Single, unambiguous property-gain tag on both R5 and R9. Trivially small - PSA does not recycle gains through earnings the way most REITs do. But FY2022 carried 2,128,860,000 under us-gaap:EquityMethodInvestmentRealizedGainLossOnDisposal, a gain of that size sitting OUTSIDE the property-gain tag.
- `us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate` — Impairment of real estate · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 0 | FY2024 0 | FY2025 4,348,000 · Only six annual facts across the whole 2008-2025 record; PSA tags it only when non-zero or when a comparative demands it.
- `us-gaap:Revenues` — Total revenue · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 4,517,690,000 | FY2024 4,695,616,000 | FY2025 4,824,113,000 · Unbroken. The self-storage / ancillary split uses the SAME us-gaap:Revenues element dimensioned on srt:ProductOrServiceAxis (self-storage 4,489,413,000 and ancillary 334,700,000 in FY2025), so the segments are invisible to companyfacts while the total is fine.
- `us-gaap:RealEstateRevenueNet` — Rental revenue, prior generation · USD · FY2007-2017 · FY2015 2,235,525,000 | FY2016 2,405,828,000 | FY2017 2,512,433,000 · Retired after FY2017. From FY2018 onward PSA tags NO rent-specific element undimensioned at all, only the total. Storage leases are month-to-month, so there is no OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome tag as AVB and INVH have.
- `psa:CapitalExpendituresToMaintainRealEstateFacilities` — MAINTENANCE CAPEX, explicitly separated (CUSTOM TAG) · USD · FY2020-2025 · FY2023 236,572,000 | FY2024 239,655,000 | FY2025 218,464,000 · The single most valuable disclosure on this tier and the only one of the three that names maintenance capex outright. Read verbatim off R9 of the FY2025 10-K as the investing line 'Capital expenditures to maintain real estate facilities'. Because it is a company extension it does NOT appear in the companyfacts API at any point. It sits alongside psa:CapitalExpendituresForPropertyEnhancements (FY2023 159,939,000 | FY2024 126,757,000 | FY2025 0) and psa:CapitalExpendituresForEnergyEfficiencies (FY2023 64,626,000 | FY2024 53,612,000 | FY2025 70,914,000), so enhancement spend is separable from keeping-it-standing spend.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements` — Capital improvements, standard tag (retired) · USD · FY2007-2019 · FY2017 120,600,000 | FY2018 141,000,000 | FY2019 187,300,000 · HARD SEAM. No annual 10-K fact after FY2019. From the FY2020 10-K onward the cash-flow maintenance line moved to the custom extension above, so PSA's maintenance capex vanishes from companyfacts after FY2019 while the real spend keeps running at 218-240 million a year.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToDevelopRealEstateAssets` — Development and expansion of real estate · USD · FY2021-2025 · FY2023 364,445,000 | FY2024 326,854,000 | FY2025 310,737,000 · Only five annual facts, and FY2021 (281,981,000) appears only in the FY2023 10-K filed 2024-02-20. FY2020 development capex is not tagged undimensioned under ANY payments element - I enumerated every /Payment|Capital|Develop|Construct/ fact with a 2020-12-31 end date and only PaymentsToAcquireRealEstate 792,266,000 exists. FY2020 is a genuine hole.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsForConstructionInProcess` — Construction in process, prior generation · USD · FY2007-2019 · FY2017 337,300,000 | FY2018 340,000,000 | FY2019 284,700,000 · Retired at the same FY2019/FY2020 seam as the capital-improvements tag.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireRealEstate` — Acquisitions of real estate · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 473,176,000 | FY2024 267,473,000 | FY2025 945,585,000 · Unbroken and the only capex-family tag that survives the FY2020 seam.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebt` — Total debt, net · USD · FY2016-2025 · FY2023 9,103,277,000 | FY2024 9,353,034,000 | FY2025 10,253,881,000 · Instant. DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount 10,310,320,000 less unamortised discount and issuance costs 56,581,000 gives 10,253,739,000, within 142,000 of this; the remainder is foreign-currency carrying on the euro notes.
- `us-gaap:DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount` — Debt at carrying amount (gross) · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 9,153,790,000 | FY2024 9,405,367,000 | FY2025 10,310,320,000 · Instant.
- `us-gaap:DebtWeightedAverageInterestRate` — Weighted-average interest rate on debt · pure · FY2018-2025 · FY2023 0.031 | FY2024 0.031 | FY2025 0.032 · Instant, but only FOUR annual facts across 2018-2025 (FY2018, FY2023, FY2024, FY2025). FY2019 through FY2022 are untagged undimensioned.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebtMaturitiesRepaymentsOfPrincipalInNextTwelveMonths (plus ...InYearTwo, ...InYearThree, ...InYearFour, ...InYearFive, ...AfterYearFive)` — Debt maturity ladder · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2025 year-1 1,150,138,000 | year-2 1,200,146,000 | year-3 1,200,129,000 | year-4 1,000,088,000 | year-5 1,297,819,000 | thereafter 4,462,000,000 · The full six-bucket ladder is present for FY2025 and ties to the carrying amount. But it is tagged in only SEVEN years of nineteen: FY2010, FY2011, FY2012, FY2013, FY2014, FY2016 and FY2025. FY2015 and the whole FY2017-FY2024 stretch have no undimensioned ladder at all.
- `us-gaap:PreferredStockValue` — Preferred equity · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 4,350,000,000 | FY2024 4,350,000,000 | FY2025 4,350,000,000 · Instant. 4.35 billion of perpetual preferred, 21.5% of the 20.2 billion balance sheet; PreferredStockLiquidationPreferenceValue carries the same figure. The largest preferred stack of the three by a wide margin, and any common-level FFO must clear it.
- `us-gaap:PreferredStockDividendsIncomeStatementImpact` — Preferred dividends · USD · FY2013-2025 · FY2023 194,703,000 | FY2024 194,703,000 | FY2025 194,703,000 · Confirmed on R5 as the 'Preferred shareholders' allocation. Also duplicated as us-gaap:DividendsPreferredStock (FY2016-FY2025, identical) and us-gaap:DividendsPreferredStockCash (FY2007-FY2025, rounded to 194,700,000). Three tags, one number.
- `us-gaap:InterestPaidCapitalized` — Capitalised interest (cash paid) · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023 9,300,000 | FY2024 10,500,000 | FY2025 6,500,000 · Small against 304,495,000 of interest expense. Note PSA uses the CASH-PAID capitalised-interest element while AVB and INVH use the accrual element us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized - a cross-filer tag mismatch for the same concept.
- `us-gaap:InterestExpense` — Interest expense · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023 201,132,000 | FY2024 287,401,000 | FY2025 304,495,000 · Sits inside 'Total expenses' on R5.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsOfCapitalDistribution` — Total distributions paid, ALL classes combined · USD · FY2020-2025 · FY2023 2,305,322,000 | FY2024 2,301,935,000 | FY2025 2,303,381,000 · The R9 financing line reads 'Distributions paid to preferred shareholders, common shareholders, restricted share unitholders and unvested LTIP unitholders'. A single blended number. PSA does NOT tag common dividends paid separately anywhere from FY2015 onward.
- `us-gaap:DividendsCommonStock` — Common dividends declared (dollars), retired · USD · FY2013-2021 · FY2019 1,398,570,000 | FY2020 1,399,361,000 | FY2021 1,402,309,000 · Last annual fact is FY2021. Its rounded sibling us-gaap:DividendsCommonStockCash runs to FY2022 (2,300,000,000, inflated by the 13.15 special). For FY2023, FY2024 and FY2025 there is NO dollar figure for common dividends at all - paid or declared - only the per-share rate and the blended PaymentsOfCapitalDistribution.
- `us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared` — Dividends declared per common share · USD/shares · FY2008-2025 · FY2023 12.00 | FY2024 12.00 | FY2025 12.00 · Flat at 3.00 a quarter for three straight years, and the only clean common-dividend datum PSA still tags. NOTE a unit change: for FY2007-FY2009 the same element is tagged with unit 'pure' (2.00 / 2.80 / 2.20) rather than USD/shares, so a unit-filtered read silently drops those years.
- `us-gaap:Assets` — Total assets · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2024 19,754,934,000 | FY2025 20,208,604,000 · Instant, continuous.
- ABSENT: FFO: NOT TAGGED. I inventoried every element in PSA's FY2025 10-K via MetaLinks.json (psa-20251231.htm) - standard, dimensioned and custom - and there is no FundsFromOperations, FFO, CoreFFO or AdjustedFunds element. Same for the FY2019 10-K (695 elements). The only /FFO/ regex hit is the false positive psa:DevelopmentInProcessWriteOffOfCancelledProjects.; AFFO: same, none.; Straight-line rent: no StraightLine or DeferredRent element anywhere. Structural - self-storage leases are month-to-month.; Above/below-market lease amortisation: none.; Same-store / same-property NOI: NO element containing SameStore, SameProperty or NetOperatingIncome anywhere in the filing. PSA's Same Store Facilities table lives in Item 7 MD&A, which is outside the XBRL tagging requirement. Text only.; Operating income: us-gaap:OperatingIncomeLoss exists only FY2015-FY2017. PSA's income statement has no operating subtotal - it runs total revenue, total expenses (interest inside), other items, then net income.; Rent-specific revenue tag for FY2018 onward: none undimensioned.; Common dividends paid or declared in dollars for FY2023-FY2025: none.; Occupancy and square footage: not tagged.
- HAZARDS: The maintenance-capex number that makes AFFO possible - 218,464,000 in FY2025 - is a COMPANY EXTENSION and is structurally invisible to the companyfacts API. Any AFFO for PSA must be built by parsing the filing's R-files or MetaLinks, not from the API. This is the desk's central practical problem for PSA.; TAG-GENERATION CHANGE, WITH A SPELLING ERROR. In the FY2019 10-K the maintenance element was psa:CapitalExpendituresToMaintainRealEstateFacilites - note 'Facilites', missing an i. By the FY2020 10-K the main element is corrected to ...Facilities while its companion is still psa:CapitalExpendituresToMaintainRealEstateFacilitesRemainingUnpaidAtPeriodEnd (still misspelled); by FY2021 both are corrected. A name-keyed extractor breaks across that seam in either direction.; us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss for PSA is struck BEFORE 194,703,000 of preferred dividends. An FFO built on it and compared against a common-level dividend overstates coverage. The correct common-level start is NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic.; Three overlapping D&A tags are simultaneously populated for FY2014-FY2021 with three different values.; The dividend cash line is BLENDED across preferred, common, RSU and LTIP holders. Dividing 2,303,381,000 by any FFO measure mixes classes. The clean read is 12.00 a share times about 175.4 million weighted shares, roughly 2.1 billion of common, with the 194.7 million preferred taken out separately.; FY2020 has no development or maintenance capex tagged undimensioned under any element. A true hole, not a mis-mapping.; The debt maturity ladder is tagged in only seven of nineteen years, with an eight-year blackout FY2017-FY2024.; FY2022 contains a 2,128,860,000 gain under us-gaap:EquityMethodInvestmentRealizedGainLossOnDisposal that is not a property-gain tag. An FFO build that only removes GainsLossesOnSalesOfInvestmentRealEstate leaves that 2.1 billion sitting in FY2022 FFO.; us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareCashPaid carries facts with period ends of 2022-08-04 and 2024-08-04 valued 13.15 - the 2022 special dividend, tagged against odd non-fiscal contexts. A latest-fact read of that element returns 13.15 rather than 12.00.; us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared switches unit from 'pure' to 'USD/shares' after FY2009.; CIK resolution: PSA's live CIK 1393311 begins only in 2007 with the reorganisation. The predecessor PUBLIC STORAGE INC /CA is CIK 0000318380 (last filing 2014), and an EDGAR name search also surfaces a dozen legacy Public Storage Partners limited partnerships.

### INVH (CIK 0001687229, FYE December (dei fiscalYearEnd 1231))

- `us-gaap:ProfitLoss` — Net income, consolidated · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 521,028,000 | FY2024 455,365,000 | FY2025 589,909,000 · Confirmed on R5 as 'Net income'. The record starts FY2015 (predecessor periods in the FY2017 IPO-year 10-K); there is nothing before that.

- `us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic` — Net income available to common, basic · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 518,774,000 | FY2024 453,164,000 | FY2025 586,964,000 · After the participating-securities allocation (960,000 in FY2025).
- `us-gaap:Depreciation` — Depreciation and amortisation (income-statement line) · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 674,287,000 | FY2024 714,326,000 | FY2025 746,933,000 · MISLEADING ELEMENT NAME. The R5 label is 'Depreciation and amortization' and R9 uses the same element for the cash-flow add-back, but the element chosen is us-gaap:Depreciation, not a D&A element. It is total D&A, not depreciation.
- `us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense` — Real-estate depreciation only (Schedule III) · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 663,398,000 | FY2024 699,474,000 | FY2025 728,652,000 · The genuinely real-estate-specific figure, 18,281,000 below the income-statement D&A in FY2025. This is the NAREIT-correct add-back if you want depreciation of real property only; the difference is about 1.6% of an FFO build.
- `us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfProperties` — Gain on sale of property, net of tax · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 183,540,000 | FY2024 244,550,000 | FY2025 218,235,000 · Single, unambiguous, undimensioned, on both R5 and R9 - the cleanest gains tag of the three, with no competing second tag. Note the scale: 218 million of gains against 588 million of net income means 37% of INVH's FY2025 reported earnings is proceeds from selling houses.
- `us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate` — Impairment of real estate · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 427,000 | FY2024 506,000 | FY2025 657,000 · Continuous but tiny. The income-statement line is broader: 'Casualty losses, impairment, and other' uses the custom element invh:AssetImpairmentChargesAndCasualtyLosses (FY2023 8,596,000 | FY2024 82,925,000 | FY2025 11,443,000), invisible to companyfacts. The FY2024 spike of 82,925,000 is not visible in the standard tag.
- `us-gaap:AssetImpairmentCharges` — Impairment, prior generation · USD · FY2015-2023 · FY2021 8,676,000 | FY2022 28,697,000 | FY2023 8,596,000 · Retired after FY2023 in favour of the custom blended element.
- `us-gaap:Revenues` — Total revenues · USD · FY2019-2025 · FY2023 2,432,278,000 | FY2024 2,618,942,000 | FY2025 2,729,296,000 · Only seven years. For FY2015-FY2017 the total was tagged us-gaap:RealEstateRevenueNet (FY2017 1,054,456,000); FY2018 has neither.
- `us-gaap:OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome` — Rental revenues and other property income · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 2,418,631,000 | FY2024 2,548,964,000 | FY2025 2,641,957,000 · The rent line proper on R5.
- `us-gaap:OperatingLeaseVariableLeaseIncome` — Variable lease income (resident recoveries) · USD · FY2019-2025 · FY2023 153,016,000 | FY2024 163,700,000 | FY2025 173,631,000 · A component of the rent line above, not additive to it. It is the resident-billback share, 6.6% of rental revenue and growing.
- `us-gaap:PropertyManagementFeeRevenue` — Third-party management fee revenue · USD · FY2021-2025 · FY2023 13,647,000 | FY2024 69,978,000 | FY2025 87,339,000 · A fee business grown six-fold in two years and now 3.2% of revenue. It is not rent and carries no buildings, so it should not be read through a property lens.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherProductiveAssets` — RECURRING capex on homes · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 221,102,000 | FY2024 219,394,000 | FY2025 242,752,000 · Read verbatim off R9 as 'Other capital expenditures for single-family residential properties'. THIS is INVH's ongoing keep-the-houses-competitive spend, about 2,825 dollars per home per year across 85,914 homes. It hides behind a generic productive-assets element that no capex heuristic would associate with maintenance.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements` — INITIAL renovation capex on newly acquired homes · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 30,207,000 | FY2024 30,605,000 | FY2025 28,332,000 · Read verbatim off R9 as 'Initial renovations to single-family residential properties'. This is up-front rehab on homes just bought - a GROWTH cost, not maintenance. The identical us-gaap element carries the opposite meaning at AVB, where it is the recurring existing-asset line.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireResidentialRealEstate` — Home acquisitions · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 963,305,000 | FY2024 737,939,000 | FY2025 752,893,000 · Clean, unbroken, and clearly separable from both capex lines.
- `us-gaap:RealEstateImprovements` — Real estate improvements (Schedule III roll-forward) · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 29,793,000 | FY2024 30,041,000 | FY2025 27,773,000 · A fourth capex-adjacent tag sitting within a few hundred thousand of the initial-renovation figure. I did not confirm its scope from a face statement. Its near-identity to PaymentsForCapitalImprovements makes it a live collision risk for any /Improvement/ pattern match.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebt` — Total debt, net · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 8,546,052,000 | FY2024 8,202,302,000 | FY2025 8,380,020,000 · Instant. THIS is the total: 44.9% of the 18.68 billion balance sheet.
- `us-gaap:DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount` — Debt at carrying amount (gross) · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 8,612,946,000 | FY2024 8,287,197,000 | FY2025 8,458,399,000 · Instant, before discount and issuance costs.
- `us-gaap:SecuredDebt` — Secured debt · USD · FY2016-2025 · FY2023 1,627,256,000 | FY2024 1,385,573,000 | FY2025 1,384,114,000 · Instant. A COMPONENT ONLY.
- `us-gaap:UnsecuredDebt` — Unsecured debt · USD · FY2016-2025 · FY2023 3,211,814,000 | FY2024 2,446,041,000 | FY2025 2,451,985,000 · Instant. A COMPONENT ONLY - see hazards; the components do not sum to the total.
- `us-gaap:LineOfCredit` — Revolving facility drawn · USD · FY2016-2025 · FY2023 0 | FY2024 570,000,000 | FY2025 145,000,000 · Instant.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebtMaturitiesRepaymentsOfPrincipalInNextTwelveMonths (plus ...InYearTwo, ...InYearThree, ...InYearFour, ...InYearFive, ...AfterYearFive)` — Debt maturity ladder · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2025 year-1 0 | year-2 988,013,000 | year-3 3,370,000,000 | year-4 0 | year-5 450,000,000 | thereafter 3,650,386,000 · The ONLY complete and unbroken maturity ladder of the three tickers: all six buckets tagged for every year FY2017-FY2025, summing to 8,458,399,000, exactly the gross carrying amount. The FY2028 wall of 3.37 billion is the thing an owner would want to see, and here it is machine-readable.
- `us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized` — Capitalised interest, annual amount · USD · FY2021-2025 · FY2023 2,804,000 | FY2024 2,128,000 | FY2025 1,528,000 · The R9 supplemental line 'Interest capitalized as investments in single-family residential properties, net'. Immaterial against 353 million of interest expense.
- `us-gaap:RealEstateInventoryCapitalizedInterestCosts` — Capitalised interest, CUMULATIVE balance · USD · FY2016-2025 · FY2023 78,073,000 | FY2024 78,776,000 | FY2025 79,124,000 · INSTANT - a cumulative balance sitting in the carrying value of the homes, fifty times the annual figure. Reading this as the year's capitalised interest overstates it by about 77.6 million.
- `us-gaap:InterestExpenseOperating` — Interest expense · USD · FY2022-2025 · FY2023 333,457,000 | FY2024 366,070,000 | FY2025 353,327,000 · Current tag. Replaced us-gaap:InterestExpense with a FY2022-FY2023 overlap carrying identical values.
- `us-gaap:InterestExpense` — Interest expense, prior generation · USD · FY2015-2023 · FY2021 322,661,000 | FY2022 304,092,000 | FY2023 333,457,000 · Retired after FY2023. An extractor keyed only on us-gaap:InterestExpense loses INVH's interest bill entirely from FY2024 onward.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends` — Dividends and dividend equivalents paid (cash) · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2023 638,129,000 | FY2024 689,244,000 | FY2025 712,842,000 · The R9 financing line, unbroken eleven years.
- `us-gaap:Dividends` — Dividends declared (dollars) · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 638,836,000 | FY2024 864,259,000 | FY2025 717,977,000 · DECLARED basis, and FY2024 contains FIVE declarations, not four: 864,259,000 declared against 689,244,000 actually paid. The declared series is not comparable year to year without knowing the declaration count.
- `us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared` — Dividends declared per common share · USD/shares · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 1.04 | FY2024 1.41 | FY2025 1.17 · The FY2024 figure of 1.41 is NOT the annual rate - it is 1.12 (four quarters at 0.28) plus the 0.29 declared in December 2024 for payment in 2025. FY2025's 1.17 is likewise 0.29 x 3 plus 0.30. Only FY2023's 1.04 equals four times the quarterly rate. A yield or payout built on 1.41 overstates INVH's dividend by 26%. The same element ALSO carries 33 instant facts at individual declaration dates (0.29, 0.30, and one oddity of 0.0028 at 2023-12-08) mixed in with the duration facts.
- `us-gaap:PreferredStockValueOutstanding` — Preferred equity · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023 0 | FY2024 0 | FY2025 0 · None outstanding. Note INVH uses PreferredStockValueOutstanding where AVB and PSA use PreferredStockValue.
- `us-gaap:LimitedPartnersCapitalAccountUnitsOutstanding` — Operating partnership units outstanding · shares · FY2019-2025 · FY2023 1,869,483 | FY2024 1,979,009 | FY2025 2,099,937 · INVH is an UPREIT with an operating partnership, but the OP does NOT co-file (single FILER block on the 10-K). 2.1 million units against 613 million shares - immaterial dilution, but present.
- `us-gaap:Assets` — Total assets · USD · FY2015-2025 · FY2024 18,700,951,000 | FY2025 18,680,290,000 · Instant, continuous.
- ABSENT: FFO: NOT TAGGED. I inventoried every element in INVH's FY2025 10-K via MetaLinks.json (invh-20251231.htm) and its FY2019 10-K (824 elements) - standard, dimensioned and custom - and there is no FundsFromOperations, FFO, CoreFFO or AdjustedFunds element. The only /FFO/ regex hits are the false positives us-gaap:StockIssuedDuringPeriodSharesRestrictedStockAwardNetOfForfeitures and ...ValueRestrictedStockAwardNetOfForfeitures.; AFFO: same, none.; Straight-line rent: no StraightLine or DeferredRent element. Structural - single-family leases run about twelve months.; Above/below-market lease amortisation: none.; Same-store / same-home NOI: NO element containing SameStore, SameProperty, SameHome or NetOperatingIncome anywhere in the filing. INVH's same-store pool disclosure is MD&A text and supplemental only. The segment note (R86, R87) discloses only a significant-expense schedule using custom elements invh:DirectCostsOfLeasedAndRentedPropertyOrEquipmentControllableCosts and ...FixedCosts.; Operating income: us-gaap:OperatingIncomeLoss exists only FY2015-FY2017. INVH's income statement has no operating subtotal and puts INTEREST EXPENSE INSIDE 'Total expenses' (2,341,670,000 in FY2025), so revenue less total expenses is an after-interest number, not an operating margin.; Weighted-average interest rate: NO tag of any kind. My probe over all elements in the FY2025 filing returned nothing matching /WeightedAverage.*(Interest|Rate|Maturity)/. The cost of INVH's debt is not machine-readable from the 10-K.; Preferred equity: none outstanding since the FY2017 redemption of 1,153,000.; Occupancy and turnover: not tagged.
- HAZARDS: THE SINGLE WORST CROSS-FILER TRAP ON THIS TIER. us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements means 'Initial renovations to newly acquired homes' at INVH (28,332,000 in FY2025) and 'Capital expenditures - existing real estate assets' at AVB (261,769,000). Same element, opposite economic meaning. INVH's actual recurring capex, 242,752,000, hides under us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherProductiveAssets. A maintenance-capex heuristic keyed on the standard element reads INVH's upkeep as 28 million when it is 243 million - an 8.6x understatement that would make INVH's AFFO look 214 million a year better than it is.; DEBT COMPONENTS DO NOT SUM TO THE TOTAL. SecuredDebt 1,384,114,000 plus UnsecuredDebt 2,451,985,000 plus LineOfCredit 145,000,000 equals 3,981,099,000, against a true LongTermDebt of 8,380,020,000. The roughly 4.4 billion of term-loan facilities is captured by none of those three elements. A component-summing debt total under-captures INVH by 53% and yields debt/assets of 21% instead of 45% - which PASSES the existing under-capture guard in src/lib/reits.mjs (that guard only fires below 10%) and would print a materially false leverage figure.; us-gaap:Depreciation carries TOTAL D&A at INVH (746,933,000), not depreciation. The real-estate-only figure is 728,652,000 under the Schedule III element.; The dividend-per-share series counts DECLARATIONS, not quarters: FY2024's 1.41 covers five declarations. Any payout or yield built on it is 26% too high. The same element also carries 33 instant facts at declaration dates, so a naive latest-fact read can return a single quarterly 0.30 instead of the annual figure.; Interest expense changes element after FY2023 (us-gaap:InterestExpense to us-gaap:InterestExpenseOperating) with a two-year overlap of identical values.; us-gaap:RealEstateInventoryCapitalizedInterestCosts (79,124,000, instant, cumulative) sits beside us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized (1,528,000, annual). Grabbing the wrong one overstates the year's capitalised interest by fifty times.; The income statement's casualty-and-impairment line is a custom element invisible to companyfacts; the standard ImpairmentOfRealEstate tag shows 506,000 for FY2024 when the reported line was 82,925,000.; us-gaap:Revenues starts only at FY2019; FY2018 has no total-revenue tag under either generation.; EDGAR NAME COLLISION: a company-name search for 'Invitation Homes' returns TWO CIKs - the live 0001687229 and 0001579471, also named 'Invitation Homes', the former Starwood Waypoint / Colony Starwood entity merged in and deregistered in 2018 (last filing 2018-07-31). Name-based CIK resolution can land on a dead shell.

> THE DESK'S CENTRAL QUESTION, ANSWERED EMPIRICALLY: FFO is not tagged for any of AVB, PSA or INVH. Not undimensioned, not dimension-locked, not as a company extension. I did not infer this from the companyfacts API alone (which by construction returns only undimensioned facts and only the us-gaap, dei, srt, ecd, ffd and invest namespaces - I confirmed those are the only namespaces present in all three companyfacts files, with no company prefix). I inventoried the COMPLETE element list of six filings via MetaLinks.json - the FY2025 and FY2019 10-K of each ticker, 695 to 1,246 elements apiece - and no FundsFromOperations, FFO, CoreFFO, AdjustedFundsFromOperations or Normalized element exists in any of them. FFO is a non-GAAP measure disclosed in the earnings release and the quarterly supplemental, neither of which carries financial-statement XBRL. The consequence is plain: on this tier FFO cannot be read, only BUILT. Any FFO OwnerScorecard prints is the house's construction and must be labelled as such, with the formula shown.

> VERDICT ON src/lib/groupingColumns.mjs and src/lib/reits.mjs. The definition 'net income + depreciation - property gains' is the right shape but is not adequate as written, for four reasons I can name with numbers. (1) No preferred deduction. PSA carries 4.35 billion of perpetual preferred paying 194,703,000 a year, and us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss for PSA is struck BEFORE it: 1,784,348 + 1,151,840 - 1,113 = 2,935,075 thousand, of which 194,703 belongs to preferred holders. That is a systematic 6.6% overstatement of common FFO. (2) No impairment add-back. NAREIT adds back impairment of depreciable real estate; the current formula does not, so a bad year is punished twice. (3) 'Depreciation' begs the question of which tag: PSA has three concurrent D&A tags with three different values for FY2014-FY2021; INVH's us-gaap:Depreciation is total D&A while its Schedule III tag is real-estate-only, 18.3 million apart in FY2025; AVB's two depreciation tags agree in FY2024-FY2025 but differ by 35.7 million in FY2023. (4) 'Property gains' begs the same question: AVB carries TWO undimensioned property-gain tags for every recent year (335,713,000 and 339,954,000 in FY2025), and subtracting both understates AVB's FFO by about 20%. Recommendation, in the withhold-never-guess spirit: name the exact tag per ticker in an explicit map rather than pattern-matching, deduct preferred where PreferredStockDividendsIncomeStatementImpact is non-zero, add back impairment, and print the formula in the cell's basis line the way the scorecard already does.

> AND THE HARDER POINT, WHICH IS WHAT THE FRAME ACTUALLY ASKS. FFO is the easy half. The honest owner's question is what can be taken out after keeping the buildings competitive, and that turns on recurring capex, which fails differently for each name. PSA is the only one that names it outright: psa:CapitalExpendituresToMaintainRealEstateFacilities, 218,464,000 in FY2025, separated from psa:CapitalExpendituresForPropertyEnhancements and psa:CapitalExpendituresForEnergyEfficiencies. That is exactly the disclosure the desk wants - and it is a company extension, invisible to companyfacts, so the pipeline cannot see it without parsing R-files or MetaLinks. AVB tags a single blended existing-real-estate line (us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements, 261,769,000) mixing true upkeep with revenue-enhancing redevelopment, unsplittable from the 10-K. INVH tags its recurring spend under a generic productive-assets element (242,752,000) while the standard capital-improvements element carries growth-stage rehab (28,332,000). One filer discloses it honestly in a tag we cannot fetch, one blends it, one inverts the obvious mapping. There is no generic rule; there is only a per-filer map verified by reading the cash-flow statement.

> AVAILABILITY SUMMARY ACROSS THE SEVEN ASKS. (1) FFO/AFFO: absent for all three. (2) FFO ingredients: net income yes for all three but via different elements and with AVB's NetIncomeLoss broken; real-estate-specific depreciation available for AVB and INVH via us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense but NOT for PSA; property gains clean for PSA and INVH, doubled for AVB; impairment tagged for all three but blended with casualty at AVB and superseded by a custom element at INVH. (3) Rental revenue yes for AVB and INVH via us-gaap:OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome, PSA only as a total; straight-line rent and above/below-market lease amortisation absent from all three, which is CORRECT and structural for residential and storage - these leases are twelve months or month-to-month, so there is nothing to straight-line and no market-lease intangible to amortise. The desk should note that the straight-line-rent hazard is real for office, retail and net-lease and simply does not bite here. (4) Recurring vs development capex: separable for all three but by three different tag patterns, one of them a custom extension. (5) Same-store NOI: dimension-locked at AVB (us-gaap:StatementOperatingActivitiesSegmentAxis with avb:SameStoreMember), entirely untagged at PSA and INVH, because same-store pools live in Item 7 MD&A which carries no XBRL. (6) Capital stack: total debt clean for all three via us-gaap:LongTermDebt; weighted-average rate available for AVB (5 years) and PSA (4 scattered years) via us-gaap:DebtWeightedAverageInterestRate and ABSENT for INVH; maturity ladder complete only for INVH, seven-of-nineteen years for PSA, entirely absent for AVB; preferred material only at PSA; capitalised interest present for all three but under a different element at PSA (us-gaap:InterestPaidCapitalized) than at AVB and INVH (us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized). (7) Dividends: per-share declared clean for AVB and PSA, distorted by declaration count at INVH; dividends paid clean for AVB and INVH, and for PSA blended across preferred, common, RSU and LTIP holders in us-gaap:PaymentsOfCapitalDistribution with no common-only figure since FY2014.

> DUAL-CIK AND PAIRED-ENTITY CHECK: NONE of AVB, PSA or INVH co-files an operating partnership. I read the SGML filer blocks of all three FY2025 10-K submissions and each shows a single FILER. This tier is free of the paired REIT/OP problem that bedevils the office and industrial desks. Two caveats for CIK resolution: PSA's live CIK 1393311 begins in 2007 with the reorganisation - the predecessor PUBLIC STORAGE INC /CA is CIK 0000318380, last filing 2014, and a name search also surfaces a dozen legacy Public Storage Partners limited partnerships; INVH's name collides with the deregistered CIK 0001579471, also titled Invitation Homes. AVB is clean on a single CIK, with former names AVALON BAY COMMUNITIES INC and BAY APARTMENT COMMUNITIES INC. INVH does have an operating partnership (2,099,937 LP units outstanding at FY2025) but it does not file.

> WHAT WOULD BREAK A GENERIC EXTRACTOR, RANKED. First, the PaymentsForCapitalImprovements inversion between AVB and INVH - same element, opposite meaning, 8.6x error at INVH. Second, INVH's debt components summing to 47% of its actual debt while clearing the existing under-capture guard. Third, AVB mapping property NOI onto us-gaap:OperatingIncomeLoss, which turns the existing EBITDA coverage check into a double-count and prints 11.3x. Fourth, PSA's maintenance capex living in a custom tag the companyfacts API cannot return, plus the FY2019-to-FY2020 seam where the standard capex tags simply stop. Fifth, AVB's NetIncomeLoss having no FY2025 value and a four-year hole. Sixth, AVB's undimensioned NetIncomeLossAttributableToNonredeemableNoncontrollingInterest carrying TOTAL net income (1,056,599,000) rather than the noncontrolling slice (5,298,000) for FY2025. Seventh, INVH's per-share dividend counting five declarations in FY2024. Eighth, PSA's tag rename from 'Facilites' to 'Facilities' across FY2019-FY2021 - a literal typo fix that breaks name-keyed lookups.

> ONE STRUCTURAL OBSERVATION THE DESK MAY FIND USEFUL. All three of these trusts sell the SHORTEST leases in the REIT universe - twelve months at AVB and INVH, month-to-month at PSA. That is why the whole straight-line-rent and market-lease-intangible family of accounting flattery is simply absent from their filings, confirmed by element inventory rather than assumed. What replaces it as the place to look is the capex definition: with no lease-term games available, the only material judgement management makes about how good the year looked is what it calls maintenance and what it calls enhancement. PSA makes that judgement visible and splits it three ways; AVB blends it; INVH buries it under a generic element. That is the one line on this tier where the reader should be told exactly which number the record is using and why.

> A NOTE ON METHOD, so the desk can reproduce this. The companyfacts API returns ONLY undimensioned facts and only the standard namespaces; it cannot distinguish 'dimension-locked' from 'absent'. To separate those two cases I fetched MetaLinks.json from each filing directory (for example https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/915912/000091591226000004/MetaLinks.json), which lists every element used in the filing including custom extensions and dimension members, and I read the rendered R-files (R5 income statement, R8/R9 cash flow, R55/R57 segment) to map element names to the labels the company actually prints. Working files are under C:/Users/ryanr/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/c--Users-ryanr/6d6f1b58-2746-4214-95da-20156eb83cfd/scratchpad/reit/ - resi_census.mjs (annual-frame tag census over companyfacts), resi_meta.mjs (full element inventory and probes over MetaLinks), rparse.mjs (R-file label-to-element parser) and facts.mjs (single-tag fact dumper). Every tag name and every number in this report was read out of data fetched in this session; nothing is recalled from memory.

### SPG (CIK 0001063761, FYE 12 (Dec 31; dei fiscalYearEnd 1231, unchanged 2007-2025))

- `us-gaap:ProfitLoss` — Net income, consolidated incl. noncontrolling interests · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=2617018000; FY2024=2729021000; FY2025=5364120000 · This is the ONLY consolidated bottom line SPG tags. us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss is absent from SPG's entire 2007-2025 companyfacts - verified, not assumed. ProfitLoss includes the Simon Property Group L.P. noncontrolling interest (~8% of the OP), so pairing it with SPG Inc's 326m diluted shares overstates per-share figures.
- `us-gaap:NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic` — Net income available to common · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=2279789000; FY2024=2367559000; FY2025=4624275000 · Unbroken 19-year annual series. This is the numerator that matches the diluted share count. Gap to ProfitLoss in FY2025 is 739,845,000.
- `us-gaap:OperatingIncomeLoss` — Operating income · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=2807022000; FY2024=3092796000; FY2025=3175396000 · Clean 19-year annual series.
- `us-gaap:DepreciationAndAmortization` — Depreciation and amortisation (income statement / cash flow) · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=1262107000; FY2024=1265340000; FY2025=1426423000 · Total D&A, not real-estate-specific. SPG does NOT tag us-gaap:DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization at all.
- `us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense` — Real-estate depreciation (Schedule III rollforward) · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023=1193391000; FY2024=1118198000; FY2025=1481880000 · The nearest thing to a real-estate-only depreciation charge. It disagrees with the income-statement D&A line in both directions across years (FY2024: 1,118,198,000 vs 1,265,340,000; FY2025: 1,481,880,000 vs 1,426,423,000). The record does not say which belongs in an FFO add-back; the desk must choose and disclose the choice.
- `us-gaap:RealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense` — Real-estate depreciation, predecessor tag (generation change) · USD · FY2009-2012 · FY2010=874450000; FY2011=906554000; FY2012=1069607000 · Retired in favour of the SECScheduleIII-prefixed element. Overlap years 2011-2012 carry BOTH tags; an extractor that sums or prefers the wrong one double-counts or breaks continuity at the 2012/2013 seam.
- `spg:AcquisitionOfControllingInterestSaleOrDisposalOfOrRecoveryOnAssetsAndInterestsInUnconsolidatedEntitiesGainOrLossAndImpairmentNet` — Gains/losses on property and interest disposals (CUSTOM - unreachable via companyfacts) · USD · FY2023-2025 · FY2023=-3056000; FY2024=-75818000; FY2025=2887460000 · Read verbatim from the FY2025 instance (spg-20251231x10k_htm.xml), undimensioned, and declared in spg-20251231.xsd. It is a company extension element, so the SEC companyfacts API omits it entirely. This single line is 54% of FY2025 ProfitLoss and it BUNDLES gains, losses AND impairments into one number - you cannot separate the sale gain from the write-down. A tagged extensible-enumeration fact in the same filing routes the Taubman Realty Group step-acquisition remeasurement gain into this line.
- `spg:GainLossOnDisposalExchangeOrEvaluationOfEquityInterests` — Gains/losses on disposal or exchange of equity interests (CUSTOM) · USD · FY2023-2025 · FY2023=362019000; FY2024=451172000; FY2025=-86119000 · Second, separate non-operating gain line, also custom and also invisible to companyfacts. FY2024's +451,172,000 is 17% of that year's net income.
- `us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate` — Impairment of real estate · USD · FY2024-2024 · only fact: 2024-10-01 to 2024-12-31 = 57000000 (appears in both the FY2024 and FY2025 10-K) · There is NO annual impairment figure for SPG anywhere in companyfacts, ever. The only two facts under this tag are a single Q4-2024 stub. us-gaap:AssetImpairmentCharges exists only in 10-Q and 10-Q/A forms. Annual impairment is inside the bundled custom gain line above.
- `us-gaap:Revenues` — Total revenue · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=5658836000; FY2024=5963798000; FY2025=6364505000 · Unbroken 19 years.
- `us-gaap:OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome` — Lease income (total, incl. variable) · USD · FY2019-2025 · FY2023=5164335000; FY2024=5389760000; FY2025=5839160000 · ASC 842 era only. Fixed component sits in us-gaap:OperatingLeaseLeaseIncomeLeasePayments (2017-2025; FY2025=4727341000). Pre-2019 the comparable disclosure was us-gaap:TenantReimbursements (2007-2017, retired).
- `us-gaap:StraightLineRent` — Straight-line rent adjustment (cash-flow reconciling item) · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2023=-9866000; FY2024=-802000; FY2025=27790000 · Sign flips across the record. Magnitude is trivial versus the balance-sheet stock (below), which is the number that matters: SPG's straight-line accrual is nearly flat in-year but the accumulated receivable is over half a billion.
- `spg:StraightLineRentReceivable` — Accrued straight-line rent receivable (CUSTOM) · USD · FY2024-2025 · @2024-12-31=539600000; @2025-12-31=565500000 · Custom element, dimensioned on us-gaap:BalanceSheetLocationAxis=us-gaap:AccountsReceivableMember. Doubly invisible to companyfacts (extension AND dimensioned). This is the cumulative revenue booked ahead of cash - Munger's question - and it is the single most useful straight-line number SPG discloses.
- `us-gaap:BelowMarketLeaseNet` — Below-market lease intangible, net · USD · FY2013-2025 · FY2023=11092000; FY2024=7516000; FY2025=5333000 · Balance, not the amortisation. The income-statement amortisation is spg:AmortizationLeaseIncomeExpense (custom): @2025-12-31 undimensioned = 4830000, and -503000 on us-gaap:AboveMarketLeasesMember.
- `us-gaap:RealEstateImprovements` — Capital expenditure (Schedule III improvements) · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023=823705000; FY2024=744574000; FY2025=935200000 · SEC Schedule 12-28 improvements line. Runs alongside a near-but-not-equal cash-flow capex figure (next row) - two tags, two definitions, similar magnitudes.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireProductiveAssets` — Capital expenditure (cash flow statement) · USD · FY2014-2025 · FY2023=793283000; FY2024=755584000; FY2025=934346000 · Label: 'The cash outflow for purchases of and capital improvements on property, plant and equipment...'. Differs from RealEstateImprovements by 30,422,000 in FY2023 and 854,000 in FY2025. No split between maintenance and development anywhere.
