- `us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized` — Capitalised interest · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=39906000; FY2024=36059000; FY2025=30961000 · Clean 18-year annual series. Small relative to SPG's interest bill, and declining.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebt` — Total debt, carrying amount net of discounts and issuance costs · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2023=26033423000; FY2024=24264495000; FY2025=28430175000 · Annual frames present 2010, 2011, then 2015-2025 - a three-year hole at FY2012-FY2014. Gross principal before discounts/costs is us-gaap:DebtInstrumentCarryingAmount (2012-2025; FY2025=28595539000), which fills the 2012-2014 hole but on a different basis.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebtMaturitiesRepaymentsOfPrincipalInNextTwelveMonths (and ...InYearTwo/Three/Four/Five, ...AfterYearFive)` — Debt maturity ladder · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2025: next12=5905606000; yr2=3936773000; yr3=2296651000; yr4=2940031000; yr5=1657864000; after5=11858614000 · Best-tagged ladder of the three names: undimensioned, complete, 16 consecutive years. - `us-gaap:PreferredStockValue` — Preferred equity · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=41106000; FY2024=40778000; FY2025=40451000 · Trivial (~$40m). Preferred dividends via us-gaap:PreferredStockDividendsAndOtherAdjustments (2013-2025, FY2023-25 all = 3337000); the older us-gaap:DividendsPreferredStock covered 2009-2014.
- `us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareCashPaid` — Common dividend per share, cash paid · USD/shares · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=7.45; FY2024=8.10; FY2025=8.55 · Clean annual duration facts, 18 years. us-gaap:DistributionMadeToLimitedPartnerDistributionsPaidPerUnit (2023-2025) carries the identical values for the OP unit. us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared has annual frames in only 2 years (FY2013=1.15, FY2020=1.30) and must not be used.
- `spg:PaymentsOfOrdinaryDividendsCommonStockAndPreferredStock` — Common dividends paid in dollars (CUSTOM) · USD · FY2023-2025 · FY2023=2439233000; FY2024=2645213000; FY2025=2792872000 · Custom element, undimensioned in the instance, absent from companyfacts. A second custom element spg:DistributionsMadeToCommonStockholdersAndLimitedPartners gives the all-in figure including OP units (FY2023=2794781000; FY2024=3044399000; FY2025=3232256000). The ONLY dividend-dollars tag companyfacts exposes for SPG is us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsMinorityInterest (FY2025=439384000) - the OP-unit distribution, i.e. the small piece.
- `spg:NetOperatingIncome` — Net operating income (CUSTOM) · USD · FY2023-2025 · FY2023=4315580000; FY2024=4626974000; FY2025=4914715000 · Tagged UNDIMENSIONED in the FY2025 instance (also broken out by srt:ConsolidationItemsAxis into an operating-segment figure and an eliminations figure). Custom element, so companyfacts shows nothing. This is total portfolio NOI, NOT same-store NOI - no same-store element exists in SPG's taxonomy at all.
- `us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivities` — Cash from operations · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=3930793000; FY2024=3814655000; FY2025=4136551000 · Unbroken 19 years. Note FY2025 CFO of 4.14bn against ProfitLoss of 5.36bn - the gap is the non-cash gain.
- `us-gaap:WeightedAverageNumberOfDilutedSharesOutstanding` — Diluted weighted-average shares · shares · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=326807326; FY2024=326097137; FY2025=326366632 · Annual frames missing for FY2016-FY2018 (present 2008-2015, then 2019-2025). SPG Inc shares only - excludes the OP units held by noncontrolling holders, which is why a ProfitLoss numerator is the wrong pairing.
- ABSENT: FFO: NOT TAGGED AT ALL. No us-gaap element containing 'FundsFromOperations' appears in SPG's 2007-2025 companyfacts, and no such element is DEFINED in spg-20251231.xsd (463 extension elements inspected) or in the older spg-20151231.xsd. It is neither undimensioned, nor dimension-locked, nor a custom extension. It exists only as untagged MD&A prose.; AFFO: same - no element, no fact, any year.; us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss: never used by SPG, in any year, in any form.; Annual impairment of real estate: no annual fact exists; only a Q4-2024 stub.; us-gaap gain on sale of property: us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfPropertiesBeforeApplicableIncomeTaxes has exactly ONE fact, in a 10-Q, and the element is marked '(Deprecated 2020-01-31)'.; Common dividends paid in dollars under any us-gaap tag.; Same-store / same-property NOI: no element in us-gaap or in the spg extension taxonomy.; Recurring or maintenance capex: no element separates it from total improvements.; Weighted-average interest rate undimensioned: us-gaap:DebtWeightedAverageInterestRate is used only with dimensions (4.04% on USD unsecured notes under the LP legal entity; 4.85% on unconsolidated JV debt at 2025-12-31), so companyfacts returns nothing.
- HAZARDS: FATAL for the repo's ffo() formula: with gainOnSaleRealEstate defaulting to 0 (no us-gaap tag exists), SPG's FY2025 'FFO' computes to roughly ProfitLoss 5,364,120,000 + D&A 1,426,423,000 = 6.79bn, versus roughly 3.99bn on the same formula for FY2024 - a fabricated 70% jump that is entirely the untagged 2,887,460,000 Taubman remeasurement gain. This is a wrong number, not a missing one.; Second fatal path: if netIncome maps to us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss the pipeline gets NOTHING for SPG in any year, because the tag is never used.; Dividend coverage: the only us-gaap dividend-dollars tag is PaymentsOfDividendsMinorityInterest (439,384,000 FY2025). If that is what ffoPayout() picks up, SPG prints a ~6-11% payout against a real common+preferred cash dividend of 2,792,872,000. Catastrophically wrong and it would read as 'Lightly covered'.; Numerator/denominator mismatch: ProfitLoss includes OP noncontrolling interests; WeightedAverageNumberOfDilutedSharesOutstanding does not. FFO-per-share built from the pair is overstated by roughly the NCI share.; Dual-CIK: SPG's 10-K is a COMBINED filing with SIMON PROPERTY GROUP L P, CIK 0001022344 (confirmed on the FY2025 filing index page). Every context in the instance carries the parent identifier 0001063761; the LP's separate figures ride on dei:LegalEntityAxis=spg:SimonPropertyGroupLimitedPartnershipMember (114 contexts). Consequence: companyfacts for CIK 0001022344 is FROZEN AT FY2015 (ProfitLoss last annual value FY2015=2139375000). Any route that maps an LP entity to companyfacts silently returns a decade-stale record.; Tag-generation seam at FY2012/FY2013 in real-estate depreciation (RealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense -> SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense), with both tags populated in 2011 and 2012.; LongTermDebt annual gap FY2012-FY2014.; Diluted share count annual gap FY2016-FY2018 - FFO-per-share cannot be built for those years.; Revenue tag migration: TenantReimbursements (2007-2017) retired; OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome only begins FY2019. A revenue-mix series breaks at FY2018.

### O (CIK 0000726728, FYE 12 (Dec 31; dei fiscalYearEnd 1231, unchanged))

- `us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss` — Net income attributable to the company · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=872309000; FY2024=860772000; FY2025=1058590000 · Clean 18-year annual series. O is the only one of the three that tags this. us-gaap:ProfitLoss (2011-2025) runs alongside: FY2025=1069783000.
- `us-gaap:DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization` — Depreciation, depletion and amortisation (cash flow) · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=1895177000; FY2024=2395644000; FY2025=2524200000 · Total D&A. Includes amortisation of acquired in-place lease intangibles from the Spirit/VEREIT-era portfolios, which is why it runs 900m above the real-estate-only Schedule III charge.
- `us-gaap:SECScheduleIIIRealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense` — Real-estate depreciation (Schedule III rollforward) · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023=1233709000; FY2024=1508492000; FY2025=1623713000 · FY2025 gap to total D&A is 900,487,000 - 36% of the reported D&A line is NOT real-estate depreciation. Whichever the desk adds back materially changes FFO. Predecessor tag us-gaap:RealEstateAccumulatedDepreciationDepreciationExpense covers 2009-2012 (note: FY2009-FY2012 values are tagged to the dollar, e.g. FY2012=135470091, not rounded).
- `us-gaap:GainLossOnSaleOfProperties` — Gain/loss on sale of property · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=25667000; FY2024=117275000; FY2025=177640000 · The cleanest gain-on-sale tag of the three names: undimensioned, us-gaap, and continuous FY2015-FY2025. Annual frames absent FY2011-FY2014.
- `us-gaap:ImpairmentOfRealEstate` — Impairment of real estate · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=87082000; FY2024=425833000; FY2025=471335000 · Unbroken 18-year annual series - the best impairment disclosure of the three. FY2025 impairment (471,335,000) is 2.7x the gain on sale (177,640,000) and 45% of net income. Nareit FFO does NOT add impairments back, so this is a real charge against the measure.
- `us-gaap:LeaseIncome` — Rental (lease) revenue · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023=3958150000; FY2024=5043748000; FY2025=5437332000 · Undimensioned; this is O's rental top line. us-gaap:Revenues (FY2025=5749377000) is the all-in total. The former us-gaap:RealEstateRevenueNet ran 2010-2017 and was retired.
- `us-gaap:OperatingLeaseVariableLeaseIncome` — Variable lease income · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023=14800000; FY2024=16000000; FY2025=18200000 · Tiny, as expected for net lease.
- `us-gaap:BelowMarketLeaseNet` — Below-market lease intangible, net · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023=1406853000; FY2024=1635770000; FY2025=1493958000 · A large liability. Accumulated amortisation is us-gaap:BelowMarketLeaseAccumulatedAmortization (2017-2025; FY2025=641304000). The forward income effect is in the CUSTOM o:FiniteLivedIntangibleAssetsRentalIncomeIncreaseDecrease (@2025-12-31 = 186299000, dimensioned on us-gaap:FiniteLivedIntangibleAssetsByMajorClassAxis=o:LeasesAboveAndBelowMarketMember) - invisible to companyfacts.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements` — Capital expenditure (cash flow line, undimensioned) · USD · FY2011-2025 · FY2023=68692000; FY2024=121411000; FY2025=131800000 · WARNING: O uses this SAME element twice in the same filing with two different meanings. Undimensioned it is the cash-flow capex line (131,800,000 FY2025). Dimensioned on us-gaap:PropertyPlantAndEquipmentByTypeAxis=o:RealEstateInvestmentsInExistingPropertiesMember it means RE-LEASING COSTS and equals 142,700,000 FY2025 (122,900,000 FY2024). Any extractor that ignores dimensions will pick one at random or sum them.
- `o:RecurringCapitalExpendituresRealEstateImprovements` — Recurring (maintenance) capex (CUSTOM, dimension-locked) · USD · FY2024-2025 · FY2024=400000; FY2025=300000 · THE most important finding for this ticker. O is the only one of the three that separates recurring from non-recurring capex - and it reports recurring capex of $0.3 MILLION on a ~$26bn portfolio, because the net-lease tenants pay. Read verbatim from the FY2025 instance; the element is declared in o-20251231.xsd and was already declared in o-20151231.xsd, so the split has been tagged for at least a decade. It is a company extension AND dimensioned, so companyfacts returns nothing.
- `o:NonrecurringBuildingRealEstateImprovements` — Non-recurring building improvements (CUSTOM, dimension-locked) · USD · FY2024-2025 · FY2024=113900000; FY2025=132900000 · The other side of the split. Recurring 0.3m + non-recurring 132.9m + re-leasing 142.7m = the estate spend. Management's definition of 'recurring' is doing very heavy lifting here - exactly the Munger point about maintenance capex being defined by management.
- `us-gaap:RealEstateImprovements` — Capital expenditure (Schedule III improvements) · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023=54904000; FY2024=122887000; FY2025=130102000 · Annual frames missing FY2013-FY2015. Close to but not equal to the cash-flow capex line.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireCommercialRealEstate` — Acquisitions (cash flow) · USD · FY2017-2025 · FY2023=8053595000; FY2024=3262437000; FY2025=4647873000 · Growth spend, cleanly separable from estate spend. FY2017 annual frame missing. Mortgages payable are us-gaap:SecuredDebt (2018-2025; FY2025=37761000, down from 821587000 in FY2023). Revolver/commercial paper balances are dimension-locked and therefore not in companyfacts after FY2019.

Also present: us-gaap:ShortTermDebtWeightedAverageInterestRate = 0.037 on the revolver, and us-gaap:ShortTermDebtWeightedAverageInterestRateOverTime = 0.023 (FY2025) / 0.046 (FY2024) on commercial paper.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebtMaturitiesRepaymentsOfPrincipalInNextTwelveMonths (and the yr2-yr5 / AfterYearFive siblings)` — Debt maturity ladder (dimension-locked in current years) · USD · FY2011-2016 · Undimensioned annual frames END at FY2016. FY2025 ladder exists only dimensioned on us-gaap:LongtermDebtTypeAxis=us-gaap:NotesPayableToBanksMember: next12=2375000000; yr2=2374500000; yr3=2499800000; yr4=2820300000; yr5=2472300000; after5=12801900000 · A generic extractor sees a nine-year hole and would report 'no maturity data' for the entire modern record, even though the ladder is fully tagged.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock` — Common dividends paid, dollars · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=2111793000; FY2024=2691719000; FY2025=2920895000 · Clean 18-year annual series - the best dividend-dollars disclosure of the three. Declared (rather than paid) is us-gaap:DividendsCommonStockCash (2022-2025; FY2025=2950056000). Preferred: us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsPreferredStockAndPreferenceStock (FY2024=7763000; FY2025=0).
- `us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareCashPaid` — Common dividend per share, cash paid · USD/shares · FY2017-2025 · FY2023=3.051; FY2024=3.1255; FY2025=3.217 · Annual duration facts. O pays MONTHLY, so us-gaap:DividendsPayableAmountPerShare (FY2025=0.27) is a one-month rate, not a quarter and not a year - a 12x trap if mistaken for the annual rate. us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared has NO annual 10-K frame at all.
- `us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivities` — Cash from operations · USD · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=2958769000; FY2024=3573276000; FY2025=3994754000 · Unbroken. FY2025 CFO 3,994,754,000 against common dividends paid 2,920,895,000 - the raw cash-out test the desk cares about, buildable entirely from clean us-gaap tags for O.
- `us-gaap:WeightedAverageNumberOfDilutedSharesOutstanding` — Diluted weighted-average shares · shares · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=693024000; FY2024=863792000; FY2025=908334000 · Unbroken 18 years. Share count up 31% over two years - the denominator is moving fast, so any per-share series must use it rather than period-end shares.
- ABSENT: FFO: NOT TAGGED AT ALL. No 'FundsFromOperations' element in O's 2008-2025 companyfacts, and none DEFINED in o-20251231.xsd (386 extension elements inspected) or o-20151231.xsd. The only 'Nareit' string in O's taxonomy is a compensation-plan index element (o:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardAwardValueRelativeToNareitFreestandingIndexPercentage).; AFFO: same - no element, no fact.; Straight-line rent, ANNUAL: us-gaap:StraightLineRent exists for O but every fact is a 10-Q quarterly duration, first appearing in a 10-Q filed 2026-05-07 (Q1 2026 = 41,225,000; Q1 2025 = 45,512,000). There has never been an annual straight-line rent fact for O in any 10-K.; Above/below-market lease amortisation, ANNUAL: us-gaap:AmortizationOfAboveAndBelowMarketLeases has six facts, all in 10-Qs (2011-2012 and Q1 2025/2026). No annual value.; Net operating income: NO element at all - not in us-gaap, and none defined in o-20251231.xsd. O is the only one of the three with no NOI element whatsoever.; Same-store / same-property NOI: nothing.; Capitalised interest after FY2019.; Undimensioned debt maturity ladder after FY2016.; Undimensioned weighted-average interest rate in any 10-K.
- HAZARDS: The recurring-capex split is real, decade-old, and completely invisible to companyfacts because it is BOTH a company extension AND dimension-locked. Anyone building AFFO for O from companyfacts alone will conclude the split does not exist. It does - and it says recurring capex is $0.3m.; us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements carries TWO different meanings in the same FY2025 filing (131,800,000 undimensioned = cash-flow capex; 142,700,000 dimensioned = re-leasing costs). Dimension-blind extraction produces a wrong number here.; us-gaap:Revenues has no annual frame FY2012-FY2016 (present 2008-2011, then 2017-2025). Any revenue-based ratio silently breaks for five mid-record years.; DividendsPayableAmountPerShare is MONTHLY for O. Annualising it as a quarterly rate overstates the dividend 3x; treating it as annual understates 12x.; us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared has zero annual frames - a pipeline that prefers 'declared' over 'paid' gets nothing.; Total D&A exceeds real-estate-only Schedule III depreciation by 900,487,000 in FY2025 (36% of the line). An FFO built on total D&A and one built on Schedule III differ by that amount - roughly 85% of a year's net income.; O is a SINGLE filer - no operating-partnership co-registrant on the FY2025 10-K (confirmed on the filing index page). Unlike SPG and BXP there is no dual-CIK problem here.; Gain-on-sale annual frames missing FY2011-FY2014.

### BXP (CIK 0001037540, FYE 12 (Dec 31; dei fiscalYearEnd 1231, unchanged). Registrant renamed from BOSTON PROPERTIES INC to BXP, Inc.)

NCI) runs alongside: FY2024=84188000; FY2025=383995000.

FY2012=367625000, FY2013=419908000, FY2014=456176000; predecessor FY2008=258789000, FY2009=269394000, FY2010=292561000 · Two generations of us-gaap tag (2008-2010, then 2012-2014), then migration to the custom element. - `us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements` — Building capital improvements (cash flow) · USD · FY2009-2025 · FY2023=171424000; FY2024=189667000; FY2025=216687000 · 17 consecutive annual frames, undimensioned.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsForTenantImprovements` — Tenant improvements and leasing costs (cash flow) · USD · FY2010-2025 · FY2023=310925000; FY2024=258312000; FY2025=338808000 · BEST-IN-CLASS for this desk: BXP is the only one of the three that separately tags tenant improvements, undimensioned, in us-gaap, for 16 consecutive years. Capital improvements 216,687,000 + tenant improvements 338,808,000 = 555,495,000 of FY2025 spend on the existing estate, against CFO of 1,245,157,000 - i.e. 45% of operating cash consumed before a dollar of development. This is the honest AFFO deduction the frame asks for, and it is tagged.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherProductiveAssets` — Development / construction spend (cash flow) · USD · FY2014-2025 · FY2023=525963000; FY2024=651346000; FY2025=683750000 · Separable from the estate-maintenance lines above, so development can be excluded from an AFFO deduction. Older years used us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireAndDevelopRealEstate (2007-2022, overlapping) and us-gaap:ConstructionAndDevelopmentCosts (2009-2015, tagged NEGATIVE: FY2015=-374664000 - a sign trap). Schedule III improvements are the custom bxp:RealEstateImprovementsExcludingFurnitureFixturesAndEquipment (FY2025=1225473000).
- `us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized` — Capitalised interest · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=42633000; FY2024=41976000; FY2025=50626000 · Unbroken 19-year annual series - the best of the three. FY2025's 50,626,000 is 7.8% of the 653,138,000 interest expense, i.e. that much of BXP's borrowing cost never reaches the income statement.
- `us-gaap:InterestExpense` — Interest expense · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=579572000; FY2024=645117000; FY2025=653138000 · Unbroken 19 years. Up 50% since FY2021 (423,346,000).
- `us-gaap:SeniorNotes / us-gaap:SecuredDebt / us-gaap:CommercialPaper / us-gaap:LineOfCredit` — Debt components (the only undimensioned pieces) · USD · FY2008-2025 · SeniorNotes FY2024=10645077000, FY2025=9806100000; SecuredDebt FY2024=4276609000, FY2025=4280067000; CommercialPaper FY2023=0, FY2024=500000000, FY2025=750000000; LineOfCredit FY2023-25 = 0 · Sum of the undimensioned pieces at 2025-12-31 = 14,836,167,000. The filing's own carrying total is 16,609,483,000 (see next row). The 1,773,316,000 difference is NOT recoverable from any undimensioned tag.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebt` — Total debt, carrying amount (DIMENSION-LOCKED) · USD · FY2010-2021 · Undimensioned annual frames stop at FY2021 (FY2019=2949154000, FY2020=2931986000, FY2021=3300000000 - and these are PARTIALS, not total debt). FY2025 total is tagged only under us-gaap:FairValueByMeasurementBasisAxis=us-gaap:CarryingReportedAmountFairValueDisclosureMember: 16609483000 at 2025-12-31, 16220499000 at 2024-12-31 · Two hazards in one tag. First, the undimensioned FY2019-FY2021 values (~3bn) are a fraction of BXP's actual debt (~13bn) - reading them as total debt understates leverage by 75%. Second, after FY2021 the total lives only inside the fair-value footnote behind a dimension.
- `us-gaap:DebtWeightedAverageInterestRate` — Weighted-average interest rate (dimension-locked) · pure · FY2025-2025 · @2025-12-31 = 0.0380 on us-gaap:LongtermDebtTypeAxis=bxp:FixedRateMortgageMember (0.0368 @2024-12-31); 0.0399 on us-gaap:CashAndCashEquivalentsAxis=us-gaap:CommercialPaperMember · The element does not appear in BXP's companyfacts AT ALL, because every use is dimensioned. Confirms the exclusion rule empirically.
- `us-gaap:LongTermDebtMaturitiesRepaymentsOfPrincipalInNextTwelveMonths (and siblings)` — Debt maturity ladder · USD · FY2010-2021 · Undimensioned frames END at FY2021. FY2025 ladder exists only under us-gaap:LongtermDebtTypeAxis=us-gaap:MortgagesMember: next12=4357000; yr2=2304580000; yr3=804815000; yr4=184311000; yr5=0; after5=1000000000 · The FY2025 dimensioned ladder covers MORTGAGES ONLY - the senior-notes ladder is not in the instance under this element. Even a dimension-aware extractor cannot assemble BXP's full maturity wall from XBRL.
- `bxp:NetOperatingIncome` — Net operating income (CUSTOM, dimension-locked) · USD · FY2023-2025 · FY2023=1965106000; FY2024=1984056000; FY2025=1976785000 · Every fact carries srt:ConsolidationItemsAxis=us-gaap:MaterialReconcilingItemsMember (and a duplicate set under the LP legal entity). Custom AND dimensioned - doubly invisible. NOI has been flat for three years. Companion elements bxp:PercentageOfTotalNetOperatingIncome and bxp:PercentageofCompanysShareofNetOperatingIncome give the six-market split (Boston 38.2%, New York 29.2%, San Francisco 15.1%, Washington DC 13.4%, Los Angeles 2.4%, Seattle 1.8% for FY2025). This is portfolio NOI, NOT same-store.
- `us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends` — Dividends paid, dollars · USD · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=687809000; FY2024=689870000; FY2025=643107000 · Unbroken 19 years. Note the tag is PaymentsOfDividends, not PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock - a mapping keyed to the latter returns nothing for BXP.
- `us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareCashPaid` — Common dividend per share - TAGGED AS QUARTERLY INSTANTS · USD/shares · FY2012-2025 · FY2025 payment dates: @2025-01-30=0.98; @2025-04-30=0.98; @2025-07-31=0.98; @2025-10-31=0.70. (@2026-01-29=0.70 in the Q1 2026 10-Q.) · BXP tags this element as INSTANT facts at each quarterly payment date, four per year - 50 facts in total, not 14 annual values. A pipeline that takes 'the FY value' picks up a single quarter (0.98 or 0.70) and understates the annual dividend roughly fourfold. Read verbatim, these facts also record a DIVIDEND CUT from 0.98 to 0.70 per quarter effective the 31 October 2025 payment - a 28.6% reduction, the single most material fact on this ticker and one that no annual tag reports. us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareDeclared has annual frames in only five scattered years (2012, 2014-2017).
- `us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivities / us-gaap:Assets` — Cash from operations and total assets · USD · FY2007-2025 · CFO FY2023=1301520000, FY2024=1234501000, FY2025=1245157000; Assets FY2023=26026149000, FY2024=26084980000, FY2025=26166164000 · Both unbroken. CFO 1,245,157,000 less capital improvements 216,687,000 less tenant improvements 338,808,000 = 689,662,000 against dividends paid of 643,107,000 - which is the honest 'is the distribution earned' arithmetic, and it is buildable for BXP from tagged facts.
- `us-gaap:PreferredStockValue / us-gaap:PreferredStockSharesOutstanding` — Preferred equity · USD / shares · FY2008-2025 · FY2023=0; FY2024=0; FY2025=0 (both tags) · BXP has no preferred outstanding. Redeemable noncontrolling interest is us-gaap:RedeemableNoncontrollingInterestEquityCarryingAmount (FY2025=7538000).
- `us-gaap:WeightedAverageNumberOfDilutedSharesOutstanding` — Diluted weighted-average shares · shares · FY2007-2025 · FY2023=157201000; FY2024=157793000; FY2025=158869000 · Unbroken 19 years. BXP Inc shares only; the OP units are outside this count.
- ABSENT: FFO: NOT TAGGED AT ALL. No 'FundsFromOperations' element in BXP's 2007-2025 companyfacts, none DEFINED in bxp-20251231.xsd (411 extension elements inspected), and none in bxp-20181231.xsd. The only 'Nareit' element in BXP's historical taxonomy is bxp:NareitOfficeIndexAdjustedMember, a compensation-benchmark member.; AFFO: same - no element, no fact.; Same-store / same-property NOI: no element in us-gaap or in the bxp extension taxonomy.; Total debt undimensioned after FY2021.; Debt maturity ladder undimensioned after FY2021; the FY2025 dimensioned ladder covers mortgages only.; Weighted-average interest rate: absent from companyfacts entirely (all uses dimensioned).; Annual above/below-market lease amortisation under any us-gaap tag.; us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividendsCommonStock: BXP uses the unqualified us-gaap:PaymentsOfDividends instead.; An ANNUAL dividend-per-share fact: only quarterly instants exist.
- HAZARDS: Dividend-per-share is tagged as four quarterly INSTANT facts a year, not an annual duration. A naive FY pick returns 0.70 or 0.98 instead of the year's total, understating payout ~4x and reading as 'Lightly covered' when the truth is the opposite. The same facts show a 28.6% dividend CUT effective the October 2025 payment.; Total debt: the undimensioned pieces sum to 14,836,167,000 while the filing's carrying total is 16,609,483,000 - a 1,773,316,000 (10.7%) shortfall. On assets of 26,166,164,000 that is debt/assets of 56.7% versus the true 63.5%. In the repo's reits.mjs banding that is the difference between 'Elevated' and 'Heavy', and the under-capture guard (r < 0.1) will NOT fire, so the wrong number prints with no warning.; Worse, the undimensioned us-gaap:LongTermDebt values for FY2019-FY2021 (~2.9-3.3bn) are partials, not totals. Reading them as total debt gives debt/assets near 12% - which WOULD survive the 0.1 guard and print an absurdly conservative leverage for an office REIT carrying ~13bn.; Duplicate D&A tagging: DepreciationDepletionAndAmortization and DepreciationAndAmortization carry identical values every year 2007-2025.; us-gaap:StraightLineRentAdjustments changes SIGN at the FY2017/FY2018 boundary with no change in economics.; Two straight-line rent tags with different values in the same filing (FY2025: 101,878,000 vs 107,100,000).; Five different gain-on-sale tags across the record, three of them simultaneously populated in FY2012-FY2014.; Real-estate depreciation migrates across three tag generations and ends up in a company extension after FY2014, so companyfacts shows the series dying in 2014 while the disclosure continues.; Dual-CIK: BXP's 10-K is a COMBINED filing with BOSTON PROPERTIES LTD PARTNERSHIP, CIK 0001043121 (confirmed on the FY2025 filing index page). All 1,195 contexts carry the parent identifier 0001037540; the LP's figures ride on dei:LegalEntityAxis=bxp:BostonPropertiesLimitedPartnershipMember (325 contexts) with genuinely different values (e.g. Schedule III depreciation 788,884,000 LP vs 795,670,000 parent). Consequence: companyfacts for CIK 0001043121 is FROZEN AT FY2014.; us-gaap:ConstructionAndDevelopmentCosts (2009-2015) is tagged with NEGATIVE values (FY2015=-374664000) while the successor capex tags are positive - a sign trap across the FY2015/FY2016 seam.

> THE CENTRAL ANSWER: FFO is NOT TAGGED, AT ALL, BY ANY OF THE THREE. Not undimensioned, not dimension-locked, not as a company extension element. Verified four ways: (1) no us-gaap tag matching /FundsFromOperation|FFO|Nareit/ appears in any of the three companyfacts files covering 2007-2025; (2) no element containing 'FundsFrom' is DEFINED in spg-20251231.xsd, o-20251231.xsd or bxp-20251231.xsd; (3) the string 'FundsFromOperations' appears zero times in the FY2025 XBRL instance documents; (4) the same absence holds in the older spg-20151231.xsd, o-20151231.xsd and bxp-20181231.xsd. FFO lives in Item 7 MD&A prose, which is not detail-tagged. Any FFO the terminal shows for these names is CONSTRUCTED BY US, and must be labelled as such. The desk cannot source a filer-stated FFO for retail-office from XBRL.

> THE STRUCTURAL FINDING BEHIND EVERYTHING ELSE: the SEC companyfacts API returns ONLY standard-taxonomy elements (us-gaap, dei, srt, ecd, ffd) AND only facts with NO dimensions. Proved empirically: SPG's companyfacts contains 414 tags across exactly three taxonomies and ZERO spg: elements, while spg-20251231.xsd declares 463 extension elements that are demonstrably used with values in the instance. Every concept this desk most needs for REITs - NOI, recurring capex, the real gain-on-sale line, dividend dollars for SPG, the weighted-average interest rate, the maturity ladder for O and BXP - is either a company extension, dimension-locked, or both. A companyfacts-only pipeline is structurally blind to the REIT-specific disclosure. Reading the instance document (Archives/.../{ticker}-{date}_htm.xml) is the only complete route, and it costs one extra fetch per filing.

> VERDICT ON src/lib/reits.mjs ffo() = netIncome + depreciation - gainOnSaleRealEstate: NOT ADEQUATE for this tier, and it fails unsafely rather than safely. Three independent breaks. (a) netIncome: SPG never tags us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss in any year, so the mapping either returns null for SPG entirely or falls back to ProfitLoss, which includes the operating-partnership noncontrolling interest and therefore does not pair with the diluted share count. (b) depreciation: the formula wants REAL-ESTATE depreciation but the available line is total D&A. For O the gap is 900,487,000 in FY2025 (36% of the line, ~85% of a year's net income); for BXP the income-statement line runs 116,418,000 (13%) above the real-estate-only figure and the real-estate tag is a company extension invisible to companyfacts. (c) gainOnSaleRealEstate: `|| 0` is the dangerous default. SPG has NO us-gaap gain-on-sale tag with any annual value, so the term silently becomes zero while the true FY2025 figure is 2,887,460,000 sitting in a custom element - producing a fabricated ~70% year-on-year FFO jump. BXP's live gain tag is us-gaap:GainLossOnDispositionOfAssets, not GainLossOnSaleOfProperties, so a mapping keyed to the latter also returns zero after FY2014. Recommendation: the formula should REFUSE to print FFO where the gain term cannot be sourced for the year, rather than default it to zero - a missing number, not a wrong one.

> FFO IS THE WRONG STOPPING POINT ANYWAY, AND THE DATA SAYS SO. The honest owner's question - what can be taken out after keeping the buildings competitive and the tenants in place - is answerable from tagged us-gaap facts for BXP alone: CFO 1,245,157,000 less capital improvements 216,687,000 less tenant improvements 338,808,000 = 689,662,000 against dividends paid 643,107,000. That is 1.07x cover, and BXP cut its dividend 28.6% in October 2025 anyway. For O the same arithmetic is CFO 3,994,754,000 less capital improvements 131,800,000 = 3,862,954,000 against dividends paid 2,920,895,000 - but O's own tagged split says only $0.3m of that capex is 'recurring', which is management's definition doing the work, not economics. For SPG neither the capex split nor the dividend dollars are reachable from us-gaap at all. The three names are not comparable on any single tagged AFFO, and the desk should say so rather than manufacture one.

> RECURRING vs DEVELOPMENT CAPEX - the answer is 'sometimes, and never the same way twice'. BXP is best: us-gaap:PaymentsForCapitalImprovements (2009-2025) and us-gaap:PaymentsForTenantImprovements (2010-2025) are both undimensioned us-gaap tags, with development separately in us-gaap:PaymentsToAcquireOtherProductiveAssets. O has an explicit recurring/non-recurring/re-leasing split but it is a company extension AND dimension-locked, and it reports recurring capex of $0.3m. SPG offers no split at all - only a Schedule III improvements total and a cash-flow capex total that disagree with each other. A cross-name 'maintenance capex' column would be comparing three different things.

> SAME-STORE NOI IS NOT TAGGED BY ANY OF THE THREE. Portfolio NOI is tagged by two of them as custom elements - spg:NetOperatingIncome (undimensioned; FY2025=4914715000) and bxp:NetOperatingIncome (dimension-locked; FY2025=1976785000) - and by O not at all. Same-store/same-property NOI exists in no taxonomy, standard or extension, for any of the three. It is MD&A text only.

> STRAIGHT-LINE RENT SPLITS THE FIELD. BXP: 16 unbroken annual years under us-gaap:StraightLineRent (FY2025=101878000), plus a second tag with different values and a sign flip at FY2018. SPG: 16 annual years but trivial and sign-flipping in-year (FY2025=+27790000), with the meaningful number - the accumulated receivable, spg:StraightLineRentReceivable = 565,500,000 at 2025-12-31 - hidden behind a custom element AND a balance-sheet-location dimension. O: NEVER tagged annually; every us-gaap:StraightLineRent fact for O is a 10-Q quarterly, the first appearing in a filing made 2026-05-07. So the Munger question 'how much revenue was booked before the cash arrives' is answerable for BXP, partially for SPG (if you read the instance), and not at all annually for O.

> DUAL-CIK / OPERATING-PARTNERSHIP CO-FILING - and the trap is not the one it looks like. SPG co-files with SIMON PROPERTY GROUP L P (CIK 0001022344) and BXP co-files with BOSTON PROPERTIES LTD PARTNERSHIP (CIK 0001043121); O is a single filer. In the combined instances EVERY context carries the PARENT's CIK - the LP figures are carried on dei:LegalEntityAxis with a custom member. So the parent's companyfacts is clean (LP facts are dimensioned and therefore excluded). The trap is the LP's OWN companyfacts: SPG LP is frozen at FY2015 and BXP LP at FY2014, exactly when each filer switched from separate LP entity contexts to a legal-entity dimension. Any route that maps an operating-partnership CIK to companyfacts returns a decade-stale series with no error. Recommend the pipeline refuse LP CIKs for these names outright.

> WEIGHTED-AVERAGE INTEREST RATE: all three disclose it and none of them expose it. us-gaap:DebtWeightedAverageInterestRate is used by all three but every FY2025 use is dimensioned (O: 3.8% on notes payable, 4.9% on mortgages; BXP: 3.80% on fixed-rate mortgages, 3.99% on commercial paper; SPG: 4.04% on USD unsecured notes at the LP, 4.85% on unconsolidated JV debt). companyfacts shows the tag for O only as two stray 2018 10-Q facts and for BXP and SPG not at all.

> DEBT MATURITY LADDER: only SPG is extractable. SPG carries a complete, undimensioned six-bucket ladder for 16 consecutive years (FY2025 next-twelve-months = 5,905,606,000, more than double FY2024). O's undimensioned ladder ends at FY2016 and the live one is dimension-locked. BXP's undimensioned ladder ends at FY2021 and the live dimensioned one covers mortgages only, omitting the senior notes - so BXP's maturity wall is not fully assemblable from XBRL at all.

> CAPITALISED INTEREST is tagged undimensioned by all three under us-gaap:InterestCostsCapitalized, but only SPG (2008-2025) and BXP (2007-2025) run to the present; O's series STOPS AT FY2019. BXP's FY2025 capitalised interest of 50,626,000 is 7.8% of its 653,138,000 interest expense - a real amount of borrowing cost kept off the income statement, and the kind of thing the frame asks us to watch.

> GENERIC-EXTRACTOR KILL LIST for this tier, in order of damage: (1) defaulting an unfound gain-on-sale to zero; (2) treating BXP's quarterly dividend-per-share instants as an annual value; (3) mapping netIncome to us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss, which SPG never uses; (4) summing BXP's duplicate D&A tags; (5) reading BXP's partial us-gaap:LongTermDebt (FY2019-FY2021) as total debt; (6) ignoring dimensions on O's PaymentsForCapitalImprovements, which carries two meanings and two values in one filing; (7) crossing the tag-generation seams in real-estate depreciation and gain-on-sale without deduplicating the overlap years; (8) crossing BXP's FY2018 straight-line-rent sign flip and the FY2015/FY2016 sign flip in ConstructionAndDevelopmentCosts.

> One number worth carrying to the notes desk regardless of the census: BXP's own tagged facts record a quarterly common dividend of 0.98 paid on 30 Jan, 30 Apr and 31 Jul 2025 and 0.70 on 31 Oct 2025. That is a 28.6% cut, verbatim from the FY2025 instance, and it is exactly the outcome the frame predicts when the distribution stops being earned after the roofs, lifts and tenant improvements are paid for.
